# Architecture notes

The detailed map of canopy's modules, moved out of CLAUDE.md so it does not count against Claude Code's instruction-file limit. Each section is one area; read the one for the code you are about to touch. When a change makes a section wrong, fix it here.

## Shared-backend mode

Shared-backend mode (see `docs/prd-shared-backend.md`, `docs/deploy.md`): canopy can run headless in a container (`Dockerfile`, `docker-compose.yml`; the port is published on the host's tailnet address from `.env`, and `docker-compose.sidecar.yml` adds a tailscale sidecar for a host that is not on the tailnet; the `tunnel` service, under the compose profile `tunnel` that `.env` turns on with `COMPOSE_PROFILES=tunnel`, is cloudflared with `TUNNEL_TOKEN` for the public name, its ingress `http://canopy:7850` kept in the Zero Trust dashboard, `CANOPY_PUBLIC_ORIGIN` passed to canopy for the origin gate, and Cloudflare Access the only auth in front; `TUNNEL_TOKEN` defaults empty rather than `:?` since compose interpolates profiled services too) as one always-on backend for every client on the tailnet; the tmux server that holds the shells runs in a second container, the `shells` service, off the Dockerfile's `shells` stage (runtime, user, gh, claude, codex, `lib/tmux.conf`, ble.sh and a `~/.bashrc` that sources `shell/bashrc` off the mounted `~/.claude` when the host keeps one, no canopy code, so a canopy rebuild does not move its image and compose leaves it running) as `tmux -S /config/tmux.sock -f /app/lib/tmux-server.conf -D` (foreground; that conf sources the shared one and sets `exit-empty off`), with every mount canopy has since the shells run there (the read-only `~/.config/git` too, for a commit's author and the global ignore) and canopy's `GH_TOKEN` and `GIT_CONFIG_*`, so gh is git's credential helper for github.com in a shell as it is for the server; a change to that service's mounts or env recreates the container, ending every shell, a `list-sessions` healthcheck that canopy's `depends_on` waits for (a `new-session` before the socket exists would start a server inside the canopy container, gone with it), which is what makes a canopy redeploy or crash keep every shell (Phase 4; a host reboot ends the tmux server, and `keepShells` is what brings each shell's terminal and history back after one, not the processes); it runs on the mini since 2026-09-22, as the host user (`bun`, uid remapped by build args) because claude refuses `--dangerously-skip-permissions` as root, with `SHELL=/bin/bash` since `userShell()` falls back to bash off a Mac and a missing shell kills every tmux session at birth. A third service, `stages`, runs the incubator's agents and their checks with no token (see the incubator bullet). `hostOpeners()`/`backendCaps()` in `core/openers.ts` put a `Backend` (`openers`, `sshHost`) on `ScanResult`: when the host is not a mac desktop the server refuses the launcher with `NO_DESKTOP` and the UI hides the launch section. The desktop openers follow the client instead, through a helper: `canopy helper --backend URL` (`core/helperd.ts`, Bun-only: dials `GET /api/helper?name=&platform=&openers=&harnesses=` as a websocket (`harnesses` the ones this helper reads off an intent; an older helper sends none, and `helperRefusal` in `askHelper` refuses it an agent or herdr start on any harness but claude with a 400 saying to update it, since it would drop the harness and start claude with codex's flags), reconnects at doubling waits, runs each intent through `openIn`/`openFile`/`openGroup` here and answers under its id; `localOpeners` finds what is installed through `helperOpeners`; both ends ping every `HELPER_PING` (20s) and give up after `HELPER_DEAD` (60s) of silence, since a backend that dies with its host sends no close frame and the socket would sit half-open forever, the helper believing it is registered and the server offering one that is gone (`staleHelpers` is the sweep, `retire` the helper's own side)) speaks the pure protocol in `core/helper.ts` (`parseHelperQuery`, `parseHelperIntent`, `parseHelperReply`, `reachFrom` which rewrites a backend-local path to `ssh://$CANOPY_SSH_HOST<path>` for the helper, `parseDefaultGateway` off `/proc/net/route`). The server keeps `state.helpers` by name (a newer one under a name replaces the older), lists them at `GET /api/helpers`, broadcasts the list as a `helpers` event on attach and detach, and answers `GET /api/client` with a `ClientInfo` (`address`, `local` = loopback on a mac desktop, `shared` = the address is the container's default gateway, which is what docker's userland proxy hands it for every client, so it tells no browser apart); the open routes (`repos/open`, `repos/openfile`, `workspaces/open`) take `helper` in the body and relay through `askHelper` (`HELPER_TIMEOUT` 20s then 504, a helper's error is a 502), or run on the backend's own desktop for a loopback browser on a mac, else 400 `NO_HELPER`. The browser derives what it can open through the pure, browser-safe `clientCaps` in `core/client.ts` (its `helper` setting first, the one helper at its own address when the address is not shared or loopback, `local` next, else nothing; the arrays it returns are stable references so a `useShallow` selector over it settles) as `capsFor(s)` in the store, and `helperFor(s)` is the name every open call sends; `RepoMenu` gates agent, herdr and the "open in" row by those openers and shows the VS Code `vscode-remote://ssh-remote+$CANOPY_SSH_HOST` link when there is no `code` among them, the workspace buttons in `TopBar` do the same, and `Settings` has the picker ("desktop openers", `auto` or a helper by name) with the address the browser is seen as. Presence (Phase 3) is by browser, not by address, since the proxy collapses them: each browser profile mints a 16-hex id once (`clientId()` in `ui/src/client.ts`, localStorage `canopy.client`) and names itself from the `device` setting or the user agent (`deviceName`/`platformOf` in `core/presence.ts`, browser-safe), and `identity()` puts `client`, `name` and `platform` on the event stream's query and `client` on every shell socket's; the server keeps `state.streams` by SSE controller (`parseStream` validates the query; a stream without a client is no device), folds them into `Device[]` through `devicesOf` (one per id, newest name, oldest `since`, `streams` count) for `GET /api/devices` and the `devices` event (`tellDevices`, debounced 300ms), and `termInfo` adds `viewers` (device names with a socket on the shell) to every `TermInfo`, broadcast whole as a `terms` event by `tellTerms` on every start, join, leave and end; a run keeps `by` (the starting browser's device name, from `client` in the run request) and `RunSheet` shows it. The store holds `devices` and `shells` (the live `TermInfo[]`), `KeptShells.tsx` is the top bar's kept-shells chip (a drawn clock-and-arrow and the count; the `⟲` character rendered at half height in the mono face's fallback) and popover, there only when a machine going down left shells behind: one row each, newest first across backends (`newestFirst` in `ui/src/kept.ts`), with the backend's name when more than one is registered (repo, how long ago, lines, the agent it had) and restore, "restore + continue" for a claude shell, and forget; a checkbox per row, "pick all" and a per-backend count button (`keptByBackend`) build a pick that restores, continues (`restorePlan`: the continue only where an agent was running) or forgets every picked shell one after another (`eachOf` reports how many failed), with a bulk forget asking first, while the switch itself is a row in `Settings` since it is the backend's setting and not this browser's; `Shells.tsx` is the top bar's `▸_ n` chip, the shells picker: every held shell (`shells`) with join (`joinTerm`: its tab here, or a new one onto it, its panel opened for a panel shell; a dockless window goes to the shell's own `view=shell&term=` url instead), hide here (`hideTerm`: the tab goes without ending the shell, and the name joins `hiddenTerms`, persisted in the layout, which `reconcileTerms` and `adoptTerms` pass over and `pruneHidden` trims to the live shells) and end; a checkbox per row, "pick all" and a "nobody watching" pick (`unwatched` in `ui/src/kept.ts`) build a pick that `stillPicked` keeps to the live shells, and the pick joins (not offered in a dockless window, where the first join navigates away), hides here (only the picked shells open here) or ends every picked shell through `eachOf`, a bulk end asking inline first and a failure shown under the list; and a repo's agent conversations, both harnesses', through `GET /api/repos/resumable?id=` (`core/sessions.ts`: `agentSessions` merges `codexSessions` (below) with `claudeSessions`, which reads `$CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` or `~/.claude` `/projects/<projectFolder(path)>/*.jsonl`, newest first, the first typed prompt, summary and branch off each file's head through the pure `parseSessionHead`; empty for a `host` repo), where resume is `POST /api/repos/resume?id=` `{term, place, session, cols, rows}` (`resumeTerm`: 400 for a bad name or id or a `host` repo, 404 for a conversation not filed under that repo, 409 for a name in use; starts a shell under the browser's name on tmux or a plain pty and types `resumeLine`, `claude <agent flags> --resume <id>` or `codex resume <flags> <id>` by the body's `harness`, in after a beat) and the store's `resumeAgent` adds the tab; tests in `sessions.test.ts` and `server/resume.test.ts`; `Devices.tsx` is the top bar's `⌘ n` chip and popover (platform word, `ago()`, windows, this browser, "in a shell at …" off the viewers), and `adoptTerms` in `ui/src/term.ts` (tested) turns every held shell into a tab on the `terms` event and at load, a panel shell only once its panel is open (`openPanel` adopts too), so a shell opened on one device is a tab on every other; the feed reads `helpers`, `devices` and `terms` events into device and shell lines. Presence labels and never authorizes. `openers.ts` has Linux builders next to the macOS ones (`linuxCommandFor`, `linuxRemoteCommandFor`, `linuxAgentArgs`: kitty `--detach`, `xdg-open`, no Terminal.app) and `openIn`/`openAgent`/`openGroup` branch on `process.platform`, so the helper runs on Omarchy too. `CANOPY_NO_DESKTOP=1` forces the headless behaviour on a mac; `CANOPY_SSH_HOST` names the ssh alias for the VS Code link and the helper's locators; `canopy ui --no-open` skips opening a browser. The launcher (`core/launcher.ts`) stays backend-side and macOS-only by design; the in-browser core (shells, runs, git, search, diffs, history) is backend-host and works anywhere. The `coord` service (no profile) is the coordinator's runner (FR-010): a second stage runner from the stages image on `coord-net` (`br-canopy-crd`, 10.250.14.0/24), its root `~/.local/share/canopy-coord/seeds`, its own claude and codex logins, `CANOPY_STAGE_PASS_ENV=CANOPY_API,CANOPY_TOKEN`, and `CANOPY_STAGE_PROXY`, which the runner turns into its children's proxy env (`proxyEnv` in `core/stagewire.ts`, applied last by `childEnv`; never the container's own env, since the runner's fetch would send its fence probe through the proxy); the shells publish that network's gateway (10.250.14.1:7850) onto the coordinator's own listener (`CANOPY_COORD_PORT`, 7859) without joining it, `coord-proxy` publishes the egress proxy there (10.250.14.1:3128) from `egress-net`, and the fence drops each address and port from every interface but the bridge and loopback and everything else from the bridge, the internet and DNS included, which is why `coord` has no profile (the binds need the bridge). The egress proxy (`src/egress/`: `allow.ts` pure, `parseConnect` and `isPublicV4`; `proxy.ts` the server, `startEgressProxy` with the resolver, address rule and dial injectable for its tests; `main.ts` the entry and `--health`) tunnels `CONNECT <name>:443` for the exact names in `ALLOWED_HOSTS` only, resolves the name itself, refuses when any address is not public, dials the address it checked, and logs each refusal once per host; and canopy uses it only with `CANOPY_COORD_SOCKET` and `CANOPY_COORD_API` set: a second `StageClient` with its own watch (`coordExec`, held while it is away or unfenced), `<CANOPY_COORD_ROOT>/<run id>` at 0700 as the run's cwd (`StartExtras.cwd`), removed when the run ends or goes (`dropCoordFolder`: that exact directory under the root, never a link), its process tracked by that folder in `Runner.coordProcs`, `About.coordinator` while it answers (and only under `CANOPY_CALLERS=enforce` with its listener up, `coordinatorRefusal`), and the exact host of `CANOPY_COORD_API` let through the origin gate on the coordinator's listener alone (`coordApiHost`). `docs/deploy.md`, "The coordinator", has the setup.

## src/core

`src/core/` — pure logic, no HTTP: host (locators: a repo path is absolute, or `ssh://<host><path>` for another machine; `parseLocator`, shell quoting, the ssh argv with a shared ControlMaster socket per host under the config dir; pure and tested), exec (`onHost(host, cmd)` runs here or over ssh, and `git()` routes by the repo's locator so nothing above it knows where a repo lives; for a path under a seeds dir it first runs `seedGitRefusal`/`guardSeed` from `core/seedgit.ts` and adds `SEED_GIT_FLAGS`, and refuses with `SEED_BUSY` while the seed is busy), scan (find `.git` dirs, never descend below a repo root except into its checked-out submodules, which `parseGitmodules` reads off `.gitmodules` and which get cards of their own however deep they sit, while a plain clone vendored under a repo stays hidden; `scanSource` takes a `Source` and yields repos with ids `<source id>:<rel>`, bare under the launch root; the remote walk is one `find -L … -print0` mirrored from the local one that also prints every `.gitmodules`, read in a second round trip through `catCommand`, then `withSubmodules` keeps the top-level repos plus their listed submodules down the chain), git (porcelain-v2 parsing, all mutations; `getStatus` also reads the newest remote-tracking commit the checkout does not contain through `tipArgs(remotes?)` (`for-each-ref --no-merged=HEAD` under every remote, or just the ones named; parsed by `parseRemoteTip`, skipping each remote's HEAD symref) into `status.tip`, absent when there is none, and never read when `tipRemotes` is an empty list; it also reads `status.elsewhere` through `readElsewhere`, only on a main checkout (`--git-dir` equal to `--git-common-dir`, so a linked worktree's card never repeats the refs and stash it shares): the linked worktrees from `worktree list --porcelain` (prunable ones, canopy's own pull request builds under `builds/`, and any path under a seeds root skipped, and a seed repo reads none of it, since its worktree list is agent-written) each get a `status --porcelain=v2 -unormal` count, capped at 12; the unmerged local branches come from one `for-each-ref --no-merged=HEAD` with `%(ahead-behind:HEAD)` (git 2.41+, asked again without on an older git) and `%(worktreepath)`, which puts a branch checked out in a worktree under that worktree; the stash is `stash list`. `elsewhereDetail` is the changes section's drill (`GET /api/repos/elsewhere?id&branch=` or `&worktree=`, which 404s on any name or path the repo's last status did not list): `log HEAD..refs/heads/<branch>` (20, with a `rev-list --count` for the rest) and, for a worktree, its `status --porcelain=v2 -unormal` entries, whose diffs come from `GET /api/repos/diff` with `&worktree=`, held to the same rule. `getStatus` also sums `diff-index --numstat HEAD` into `lines` (tracked changes against HEAD, binary and untracked files left out, absent on a clean tree or an unborn HEAD); it is the plumbing diff-index because porcelain `diff` refreshes and writes the index, which the background-status test pins. The watcher counts `.git/worktrees/` among its hints so a commit in a linked worktree re-reads the main card; edits inside a worktree under the repo (Claude's `.claude/worktrees/`) already match the repo by path, and a worktree outside the scan root refreshes only when the main checkout does; `fetchRepo(path, remotes?)` is `git fetch --all` or `--multiple <names>` with `--prune` and `GIT_TERMINAL_PROMPT=0` and reports whether `remoteRefs` changed; `getCommit` reads one commit with `--numstat -z` and `--name-status -z` joined on path, `getDiff` takes a `DiffTarget` union: worktree, untracked, or one commit's change to a file), suggest (the commit message through the repo's `suggest` route, whose model and effort come only from a pick made for it, the repo's suggest pick, the suggest role's route or a launch pick, while the repo's whole pick or the default profile lends its harness alone at that harness's defaults (`forSuggest` in `resolveAgent`), since a max-effort repo would run out the 90 s timeout: `claude -p` with `--settings {"disableAllHooks":true}`, since an async Stop hook such as tailchan's `--wake` waiter otherwise keeps print mode alive past the timeout (`claudeSuggestArgs`) or `codex exec --sandbox read-only --ephemeral --skip-git-repo-check -C <dir> -o <tmpfile>` with the prompt as its argument, `<dir>` the repo here or the scratch folder for a repo on another host (`codexSuggestDir`) (`codexSuggestArgs`, both argv builders pure and tested in `suggest.test.ts`), the heuristic when that CLI is missing or says nothing; the server and `canopy suggest`/`commit --ai` resolve the route), openers (macOS `open`-based launchers + .code-workspace/kitty-session generation; `agent` runs `claude` through the login interactive shell in a held kitty window, or Terminal via osascript when kitty is missing; every launcher takes the repo's `AgentSettings` and `agentLine` turns them into the harness's shell line; the argv builders are pure and tested), herdr (the `herdr` opener: drives the herdr CLI's socket API to focus the workspace already at a repo's folder or create one and `agent start --kind <harness>` in its pane with the settings after `--`; the name/argv builders and the JSON readers are pure and tested, `openHerdr` is Bun), agent (browser-safe: `normalizeAgent` validates settings field by field and per harness, `agentArgs` (core/harness) makes the flags, `splitArgs` reads the extra-flags box like a shell, `describeAgent` is the menu's one line), github (`PULLS_QUERY` is the one GraphQL query over every repo the gh login can see with its open PR count, `parsePullCounts` reads `gh api graphql --paginate --slurp` output into a map by lowercased slug, `githubSlug`/`pullsFor`/`linkPulls` join it onto repos by remote url; pure and tested), access (`ownRemote(url, deps)` decides which remotes the server fetches in the background, self-hosted per `isSelfHosted` (no public forge) or a GitHub remote `accessFromUrls` says is pushable, and `ownRemotes` names a repo's own ones in config order; `pushAccess` is the same judgement over a repo path's remotes), forge (a self-hosted Forgejo through its API: `apiBase` normalizes the address it was added as, `listForgeRepos` pages `/api/v1/user/repos` with a token read from a file or `$CANOPY_FORGEJO_TOKEN`, `forgeRepo` turns one API repo into a card with `status: null` and the forge's page as its `path`, `linkForgeClones` matches those cards to local clones by remote url and hands a clone the forge's page when it has no link of its own; everything but the fetch is pure and tested), meta (what a card says beyond git state: the remote as a web URL via `webUrl` in access.ts, and a one-line description from package.json/Cargo.toml/pyproject.toml, else the README's first prose line; read at scan time only, so a file event does not re-read it), store (config at `~/.config/canopy`, overridable via `$CANOPY_CONFIG_DIR` — tests rely on this; `sources` are the extra scanned folders, `addSource`/`removeSource`, ids slugged from labels and unique; `agents` are the per-repo `RepoAgent` overrides keyed by repo path, beside `profiles` and `agentRoles`, `setRepoAgent`/`setProfile`/`setRole` write them, `agentFor(cfg, path, role)` resolves one), actions (browser-safe: labels, preconditions, and prompts for the Claude-driven actions; `chat` is the conversation, whose prompt keeps the safety rules and puts the first message last; tested), runner (Bun-only: the `Runner` keeps the live runs, one per repo's checkout plus up to three branch runs (`BranchStart`, the landing queue), a chat's turns, stops, pruning and the end-of-run status read that sets `outcome` via `statusFingerprint` (a branch run reads its worktree through the `branchState` hook instead); the wire is a `RunDriver` (`driver.ts`) picked by the resolved settings' harness (`RunnerOptions.driver` swaps it in tests, `defaultDriver` otherwise), each run's bookkeeping a `RunCtx` (steps and their cap, the prompt queue with "allow all", settle notes, `result`, `exitOutcome`); `Run.harness` says which agent, `Run.session` is Claude's session id or Codex's thread id; a stage run (`RunnerOptions.stage`) takes its spawn from `RunnerOptions.stageExec` (the stage runner's `StageClient` as `DriveCtx.spawn`, null while it is away, which parks the flow) and, once its own process ends, asks the runner's `busy` before the status read; every run's process gets `runEnv` (`CANOPY_RUN`, `CANOPY_REPO`, and `CANOPY_BACKEND` from `RunnerOptions.backend`, the server's `selfName`), so a harness hook can tell a canopy run, plus any names `start` was handed in `StartExtras.env` (a coordinator's `CANOPY_API` and `CANOPY_TOKEN`), which also ride `DriveCtx.pass` over a stage spawn's `stageEnv` filter; `StartExtras.id` lets the server pick the run's id first, so a token can be minted for it; `driver.check()` refuses a missing CLI before a run exists and the run routes refuse a harness the backend lacks with `missingHarness`. `ClaudeDriver` (`claudedrive.ts`) spawns the `claude` binary, through `DriveCtx.spawn` when it is set, with stream-json on both ends and the agent settings as flags (`cliArgs`; yolo → `--permission-mode bypassPermissions`; a stage takes `--setting-sources user` alone), parks on can_use_tool control requests through `ctx.ask`, `control_cancel_request` is `ctx.withdraw`, the first `session_id` is the session, a result is `ctx.result` with `costUsd`, except that one arriving while a background subagent runs (`local_agent` in `task_started`/`task_notification`/`background_tasks_changed`; a background shell never counts) is held with stdin open until the real one, applied after `heldGraceMs` (60 s) once the subagents are done if no turn begins (a fresh `init`, a message or a prompt), or applied on exit, and dropped on a stop; a chat keeps stdin open after each `result` and `say()` writes the next user message; `stop()` closes stdin on an idle chat (the CLI exits, the chat is `done`) and kills otherwise; tested against `testdata/fake-claude.ts` in `claudedrive.test.ts`. `CodexDriver` (`codexrun.ts`, over `codexrpc.ts`) is one `codex app-server` per run (through `DriveCtx.spawn` when it is set; an unisolated stage first drops the seeds' trust through `sweepCodexTrust`) (handshake with canopy's version as `clientInfo`, `thread/start` with the policy (`threadPolicy`: yolo is `never` + `danger-full-access`; otherwise `untrusted`, so Codex asks for every command outside its own known-safe reads and for every patch and canopy's rules judge each one, `on-request` having let the sandbox run anything unasked, with the `read-only` sandbox unless the rules hold a bare `Edit`/`Write`/`MultiEdit`, then `workspace-write`), questions on, and `CANOPY_*` added to `shell_environment_policy.set` in the thread's config, a `turn/start` per message, `turn/interrupt` then a kill to stop), items become steps through the shared `describeTool`/`toolDetail`, approvals and `request_user_input` park through `ctx.ask`, a job's `allowedTools` are enforced by canopy itself (`autoAnswer`: one simple command matching a `Bash(x:*)` rule's words whose `cwd` is inside the repo, by its words and on disk through `runsInside`, file changes inside the repo under `Edit`/`Write`), `serverRequest/resolved` withdraws a parked prompt from any thread (a sub-agent's too), a chat's result denies and drops whatever prompt its turn left and a settle never wakes an idle chat, `maxTurns` counts the turn's finished commands and file changes and interrupts past it with the limit as the problem (Codex has no turn cap of its own), and the result carries `tokens` and a `costUsd` of 0 (`NO_COST`, for a page older than harnesses that calls `costUsd.toFixed`; `resultLine` shows tokens first); Codex's sandbox (bwrap) cannot make a user namespace in the shells container, so there every workspace-write command fails and asks to rerun outside the sandbox: that stays a per-command gate the job's rules still answer, and the first such request leaves one note in the run (`escalationNote`); the stand-in `testdata/fake-codex-app-server.ts` drives `codexrun.test.ts` and `runner-drivers.test.ts`, and `CANOPY_CODEX_IT=1` runs one real turn, taking the trusted-project table codex writes for its scratch repo back out of `~/.codex/config.toml`), term (a shell inside canopy: `shellArgs` is the login interactive shell for a folder here or `sshSessionArgs` for one elsewhere, `termSize` clamps what the browser asks for, `parseTermMessage` reads the one text frame it sends, `isTermId` checks the 32-hex name the browser gives a shell, `Scrollback` is the capped ring of what a pty wrote for the next socket to see, all pure and tested; `spawnOnPty` and `startTerm` are Bun-only, a command (the shell) spawned with the pty's options (`terminal: {cols, rows, data}`, never a `Bun.Terminal` made first, which leaves the child in canopy's process group with no controlling terminal) so it leads a session of its own on the pty, and a plain pty outlives the socket: the server holds it by name until it exits, the tab's × ends it, or the server stops), tmux (the shell that outlives the server: one session per shell named `canopy-<id>` on a tmux server of canopy's own, socket `tmux.sock` under the config dir, started with `lib/tmux.conf` (status off, `prefix None`, `smcup@:rmcup@` so it draws on xterm's normal screen and scrollback works, `exit-empty on` so the tmux server goes with its last session); the repo, place and path ride as `@canopy_*` user options set in the same `new-session` call, `LIST_FORMAT`/`parseSessions` read them back, `primeText` turns a `capture-pane` of the history into terminal lines pushed off a screen of `rows` so they land in scrollback ahead of tmux's first draw; the argv builders, the parser and `primeText` are pure and tested, `tmuxBase()` is null under `CANOPY_TMUX=0` or without tmux on PATH, and `attachTmuxTerm` is one pty client per socket through `spawnOnPty`, so tmux hands each browser the screen and modes and its terminal queries are answered live; `newSession` runs `shellArgs` (an ssh line for a remote repo, so the other host needs no tmux); tests in `tmux.test.ts` and the integration in `server/term.test.ts`), keep (what a shell leaves behind, so a reboot does not take it with the tmux server: while config `keepShells` is on, the server snapshots every held shell every `KEEP_EVERY` (60s) through `snapshotArgs` (`capture-pane` over the history *and* the visible screen, which `captureArgs` leaves out since an attach redraws it and a reboot does not), `clip`s it to `KEEP_LINES`/`KEEP_BYTES` and writes a 0600 record plus history per shell under `shells/` in the config dir, the volume the shells container shares; `lostShells` is the records no live session answers for, `expiredShells` drops one `KEEP_DAYS` (7) after it was written, or `SWEPT_DAYS` (14) for a record marked `swept`, `agentIn(command, title)` tells what was running off what tmux reports (measured: Claude Code's process title is its own version, `2.1.278`, and its pane title starts `claude`, codex reports `codex`, a plain shell reports the shell, which is what keeps a shell in a folder called `claude-history` from looking like an agent), `continueLine(kind, settings?, env?)` is the `claude --continue` or `codex resume --last` a restore can type in, `resumeLine(kind, session, settings?, env?)` the `--resume <id>` or `codex resume <id>` it types instead when the record names its conversation (`KeptShell.session`, which `cardSession` reads off the registry card whose `where.canopy.term` is the shell, a real session id only, the shell's own harness first), `restoredBanner` is the line ahead of a restored shell's history; all but the four fs calls pure and tested), search (`grepArgs` is the `git grep -n -I --column -z -i -F -e … --` argv, `parseGrep` reads its `path\0line\0col\0text` records, drops leading indentation, clips a long line around the match through `clipLine`, and stops at `GREP_LIMIT` with `truncated`; `searchRepo` runs it through `git()` so a remote repo works the same and treats exit 1 as no match; `mapPool` is the bounded fan-out; all but `searchRepo` pure and tested), history (Bun-only: shells out to the claude-history CLI with `--json`; `historyOverview` matches repos to archive projects by realpath and folds `activity` into per-day arrays for the rings; `localDays`/`localMidnightIso` are pure and tested), workflow (browser-safe, pure and tested: `parseWorkflow(text, source) -> Workflow | { error }` reads a workflow markdown file's frontmatter and `##` steps into a `Workflow`; `agent: <profile>` in a step's key block, or in the frontmatter for every step without one, is `WorkflowStep.agent`, checked against `PROFILE_RE`); a step's key block also takes `retries` (0 to 10), `back` (this step's name or an earlier one's, where a retry rewinds to; the parser refuses a check-only step with `retries` that has no `back` naming an earlier step) and `evidence` (repo-relative files, only with `gate: judge`), and the frontmatter takes `budget: <runs> runs, <hours>h` (hours may be a decimal, `0.5h`), workflows (Bun-only: `loadWorkflows(repo)` reads the three sources — `lib/workflows/*.md` bundled with canopy, `$CANOPY_CONFIG_DIR/workflows/*.md`, and `<repo>/.canopy/workflows/*.md` for a local repo — and merges them by `name`, later sources winning, including the broken ones with their `error`; re-read on every request, no watcher, `findWorkflow` looks one up by name among the ok ones), flow (class `Flows`: the orchestrator over Runner, one Run per workflow step through the pure `stepSpec`, built against a small `FlowRunner` interface so tests use a fake and the server is the only caller that wires in the real Runner; after a step's Run ends it runs the step's `check` through `onHost` (the server's check hook first turns `@gates` into the repo's gates as one script, `gatesCheck` in `core/check.ts`; on a seed it runs any other `@` line as a built-in in canopy and the rest through the stage runner), then the gate — continue, ask, or verdict through an injected evaluator hook, or judge through the judge hook with the step's evidence read by the evidence hook (`evidence.ts`: `readEvidence` is realpath-contained, reads a file up to 96 KB whole and a bigger one as its head and its end) — and `resume()` continues, retries or stops a gated flow; a check or gate that says no rewinds to the step's `back` while it has `retries` left (`tries`, `rewinds` and `retryReason` ride on the `Flow`), and once the retries are spent a failing check on a step that has `retries` parks the flow with the check's reason, while a step with none still fails; a judge's rejection and a missing or failing evaluator never retry; a budget is checked before a run starts, so one long run can pass the runs or hours budget and the flow parks before the next run (`parkedFor`, grace; working time stops while a run waits on a prompt or the flow is parked); every change is saved through the save hook as a `FlowRecord` (the flow, a snapshot of its workflow, the status fingerprint, the live working time, the repo's absolute path, and the launch root `FlowFiles` stamps on it) by `flowstore.ts`'s `FlowFiles` under `$CANOPY_CONFIG_DIR/flows/<id>.json`, `loadFlowRecords` skipping a truncated or bad record with a log line and removing stray `.json.tmp` files older than a minute, `restore()` takes them back once the port is bound, only on the server holding `flows/owner.lock` (`lockFlows`: a pid file linked into place, taken over when its pid is dead, is this process's own under a token it never took, or the file is older than the machine's boot, since after a reboot its pid may be some other program's; a server without it neither loads nor writes records, and says so), only the records written for its own root (others stay on disk untouched), each repo found by path and the flow given its id in this scan, a record it cannot take back skipped with a log line (`parseFlowRecord` also checks each step is an object with a name and a status) (a gated or finished flow comes back unchanged and is not re-emitted, a flow caught mid-step reruns the step, and one whose repo left the scan fails whether it was gated or mid-step), and `detach()` stops saving before the server stops every flow. A step keeps its run's `session` and `harness` on the `FlowStep`, saved the moment the run names them, so the rerun after a restart resumes a Claude session (`resume` with `RESUME_NOTE` as the whole first message, through `ActionSpec.resume` and `ActionSpec.message`) when both the recorded harness and the step's agent now are claude; anything else starts fresh with `RESTART_NOTE`, "canopy restarted during this step; read the repo's state and finish the step". `canopy ui` takes SIGTERM and SIGINT (launchd, `docker stop`, Ctrl-C) as a call to the server's `stop()` before it exits (a second signal, or 5s, exits at once), so every run is killed first and no agent from the old process keeps working beside the step the next one resumes. A resume that fails with no turns (Claude Code's answer to a session it cannot find) falls back once to the fresh run; a retry or a rewind drops the session, a restore and a stage park after it keep it. A start may carry a `request` the browser mints per plan sheet (`isRequestId`, 32 hex digits; the routes 400 on any other shape): `start` and `startFleet` look it up first (`forRequest`, `fleetForRequest`), so a start sent again after a lost answer gets the flow or fleet the first made, and a flow's request rides in its record. Stops go bottom-up: a stop during a check aborts the check hook's signal, which `runCheck` hands to `exec` (SIGTERM then SIGKILL to the process group, as the timeout does), to the stage client (its `kill` frame) or to `onHost` (the ssh client alone; the remote command may run on), and the flow ends once the check has settled or after `STOP_GRACE` (10s); `stopFleet` marks the fleet `stopping` and it ends `stopped` only once its last flow has, staying `working` until then. Fleets run one workflow over many repos at `FLEET_CONCURRENCY` (3) at a time, skipping any repo `fleetSkipReason` flags as not meeting the workflow's `when`; `start` takes settings or a `StepAgent` (a step's profile → settings) and `startFleet` an `agentFor(repo, profile?)`, so a step naming `agent:` starts on that profile as the explicit pick to `resolveAgent` (role `flow`) and the rest on the repo's flow route; `FlowStep.profile` shows it, and the server refuses a workflow naming a profile it has not got, or a harness it lacks, before the first step), verdict (browser-safe, pure and tested: `VERDICT_QUESTIONS` the evaluator is asked, `decide(answers)` turns them into a go/park `Verdict` against the thresholds in `THRESHOLDS`, `verdictState` builds the text the evaluator reads from a step's summary, check output and whether status changed; `JUDGE_QUESTIONS` (fit meets/partly/misses, evidence, rules), `decideJudge` (go at meets 0.7, evidence 0.35, rules under 0.3; a sure miss, misses at 0.7, is a rejection only when evidence is at least 0.35, and on thinner evidence it parks with "not enough to go on") and `judgeState` (the step's task as the criteria, evidence clipped to `EVIDENCE_EACH` (12 KB) and `EVIDENCE_TOTAL` (40 KB), each clipped file keeping its first half and its end, where a research file's pick and an eval's verdict sit) are the judge gate's), launch (browser-safe, pure and tested: `normalizeLaunch`/`isDefaultLaunch`/`describeLaunch` for the four per-repo lines, `pickAsset(names, platform, glob)` picks the release asset for this machine, `parseReleases`/`parsePulls` read GitHub's API shapes, `unpackKind` and `launchTarget` say what a download turns into and what to run out of it, `buildKey`/`parseBuildKey` name a build `release:<tag>`, `pr:<n>` or `local`), launcher (Bun-only: class `Launcher`, the generic take on freecad-launcher: `releases`/`pulls` through `gh api`, `install` downloads an asset into `$CANOPY_CONFIG_DIR/builds/<owner>/<name>/release/<tag>/` and unpacks it (dmg via hdiutil, zip via ditto, tar), `build` fetches `pull/<n>/head` into a worktree under `.../pr/<n>/` or builds the checkout itself, both as a `Job` whose output tail broadcasts throttled, `launch` starts a build (an `.app` through `open -n -W`, whose wait is the tracked process while the app itself belongs to launchd, so `stopLaunch` quits it with `pkill -f` on its `Contents/MacOS/` path; a binary directly; a checkout through its run line) and keeps the process so `stopLaunch` can end it and `exited` can be broadcast, `builds` lists what is here with launch counts from the repo dir's `state.json`; hooks `onJob`/`onJobGone`/`onBuilds` are what the server broadcasts; tested against a fixture repo for the local build and launch path, a stub `.app` through `open -W`, and every unpack shape (`unpack` is exported for that)), jev (Bun-only: the evaluator itself, `typesafe-ai/jev` over the Vercel AI Gateway through `@ai-sdk/gateway`, which asks the verdict questions, while `jevJudge` asks the judge questions and `withRetries` is the shared retry on a short timeout; `hasGatewayKey` is true only with `AI_GATEWAY_API_KEY` or `VERCEL_AI_GATEWAY_API_KEY` set, and `flow.ts`'s evaluator hook is `null` without one, so a `verdict` gate behaves like `ask`), peers (pure, browser-safe, tested: `normalizePeers`/`normalizeSeed` validate the config field by field like `normalizeAgent`; `peerUrl(peer, id)` is `<alias>:<root>/<id>` (`alias` null for a peer mounted as a local path, in tests); `peerRefspecs(name)` is the pair of refspecs a peer's remote fetches, `refs/heads/*` into `refs/remotes/<name>/*` and `refs/wip/*` into `refs/peer-wip/<name>/*`, and every fetch adds `PEER_SKIP_RUNS` (`^refs/heads/run/*`) so a branch run's branch stays on its machine, as `peerTips` skips `run/` on both sides and the gate's upload-pack hides them (`HIDE_RUNS`); `globMatch`/`repoWanted`/`seedWanted` are the `repos` and `seed` globs; `ffTarget(branch, tips, contains)` picks the one peer tip that contains every other candidate as the fast-forward target, or folds every tip that disagrees into `diverged`; `BUSY_MARKERS` are the `.git` paths (`MERGE_HEAD`, `rebase-merge`, `rebase-apply`, `CHERRY_PICK_HEAD`, `REVERT_HEAD`, `BISECT_LOG`, `index.lock`) that block a move; `parseRefLines`/`parseWipLines` read `git for-each-ref` output; `peerMissing`/`peerUnreachable` tell a fetch's stderr apart, a peer that simply lacks the repo from one that did not answer at all (the gate's `NO_SUCH_SEED` answer, for a seed whose incubator has ended, counts as lacking it, so the clone left here reads as only here, not as an error); `parseQuotedWords` is the ssh gate's own word parser for `SSH_ORIGINAL_COMMAND` (bare words, single and plain double quotes, a backslash-escaped character outside quotes, no `$`, backtick or bare backslash inside double quotes. Anything a shell would expand or chain is refused, so the gate itself never runs one); `linkPeers` re-attaches `PeerState` to repos across a scan, the way `linkPulls` does) and peersync (Bun: every git call goes through the existing `git()`/`onHost`, so ssh reuses canopy's per-host ControlMaster socket; `snapshotWip` builds the tree to commit inside a copy of `.git/index` under `GIT_INDEX_FILE`, so the user's own index and working tree are never touched, and under `peerSync: "dry"` routes new objects to a scratch object directory instead of the repo's own store; before that, outside dry, it drops `refs/wip/<branch>` for every branch no worktree here is on (`dropStaleWips`, a compare-and-swap per ref), since a snapshot of a branch left behind would otherwise stay, here and on every peer, for good; `occupiedBranches` counts a branch a worktree is rebasing or bisecting as on, as git does, though both detach HEAD and drop it from `git worktree list`, and when it can't tell, nothing is dropped; `initRepo` adds or repairs each git peer's remote, its push url the literal `canopy-peer-no-push`, recognizing a remote as its own by that marker or by an exact url match (what a crash between `remote add` and the marker looks like) and leaving a same-named remote that matches neither alone as the user's; `fetchPeer` fetches by url and explicit refspecs rather than by remote name, so a dry pass, which skips `initRepo`, still reaches every peer, and a peer that lacks the repo or fails to answer is not an error (`peerMissing`/`peerUnreachable`, amendments 6 and 7 in the spec); a fetch runs with `--progress` under an idle timeout (`FETCH_IDLE_MS`, 90 s of silence) and a cap (`FETCH_CAP_MS`, 30 min), in place of the fixed 60 s that ended every fetch of a big upstream update and left a `tmp_pack_*` in objects/pack per pass (100 GiB on the mini, 2026-10-25), and `sweepTmpPacks` removes the partial packs a failed fetch of its own left plus any older than `TMP_PACK_STALE_MS`, leaving a fresh one that is someone else's; `fastForward` moves the checked-out branch only when clean and not busy, moves other branches by compare-and-swap (`update-ref <new> <old>`, never backwards) unless a linked worktree has them checked out, and under dry returns `would` instead of moving anything; `advanceUpstreams` runs after it for each peer that answered, since the background fetch leaves alone an origin that is someone else's (`ownRemote`) or one this machine cannot reach, so a branch the pass brought in would otherwise read as hundreds of unpushed commits against the origin the clone started with: for a branch ahead of or diverged from its upstream under `refs/remotes/` (origin, or a `fork` the user pushes to, named alike on both machines; never a peer's own remote such as `mac/main`), it asks the peer for that one ref by name (`ls-remote`, never a glob, which would pull every branch the peer ever fetched into a shallow clone), fetches the tip into the object store alone when it is not here, and moves the local ref by compare-and-swap only forward (a fresher fetch here is never undone, and an unrelated history moves nothing, which is also the only check that both remotes are one repo), reporting the moves as `PeerState.upstreams`, which the server answers with a status re-read like `moved`; nothing under dry; `peerWips` reads `refs/peer-wip/<peer>/*`; `serveList`/`serveSeeds`/`serveSeed` are what the ssh gate serves, `safeId` and `gateCommand` refusing anything that resolves, lexically, through a symlink, or through a worktree's gitfile and its `commondir`, outside the realpath'd workspace root, `ENTER_REPO_SUFFIXES` covering the same suffix probing git's own `enter_repo()` does without `--strict` (a regular file it lands on is opened as a gitfile, so the gate follows one there too); linked worktrees (`isLinkedWorktree`: a `.git` gitfile whose gitdir has a `commondir`) are left out of peer sync entirely, since one shares its main checkout's config, refs and objects and a peer already reaches its branches through that checkout: `serveList` never lists one (a peer would clone it as a repo of its own, then clone it back once it is removed here), `safeId` and the gate refuse one (`WORKTREE_NOT_SERVED`, its gitdir asked for directly included), `syncAll`, the CLI's launch ids and the server's `peerable` drop it, and `syncRepo` answers one asked for by id with `WORKTREE_NOT_SYNCED` before any snapshot or fetch, while a checked-out submodule or a `--separate-git-dir` repo, whose gitfile points at a full gitdir of its own, syncs as before; `initRepo` also writes nothing unless the repo's `--git-dir` is its `--git-common-dir` (`ownsConfig`), so a missed case still cannot rewrite another repo's remotes, which is what a worktree's `remote set-url` did to its main repo's peer remote on 2026-10-17; the card grid still shows a linked worktree, as scan always has, and only peer sync skips it; `askPeer` calls a local peer's serve functions in-process or a remote one's over ssh as `canopy-peer <words>`; `seedRepo`/`cloneMissing` treat a peer's listing as untrusted (`isSafeRel`, a realpath containment check on the destination before and after its directory is created, an origin reaching `git remote add` only when `networkOrigin` says it names a network remote, never a local path, a file url, a bare alias:path, a leading `-` or a `::`, and no clone landing inside a folder that is already a repo here; `serveList` drops an http(s) origin's userinfo before sending it), and seeding runs only when `cloneMissing` lands a repo or on demand, not on every pass (amendment 3); `takeWip` lands nothing when HEAD descends from the WIP's parent and already holds every path the WIP changes, as the WIP has it (`present`: the same edit was committed here while the WIP was in flight; on another branch the paths can match by chance, as a deletion of a file that branch never had does), else a WIP as uncommitted files when the tree is clean and nothing on disk occupies a path it adds, else on a scratch branch (`landWip`, replacing an earlier `wip/<peer>/<branch>` in place only when it is untouched since canopy put it there, its tip committed as `canopy`, and not checked out anywhere, otherwise `-2` and up, since local branches only ever move forward); `trackBranch` makes a local branch from a peer-only one; `syncRepo` holds a per-repo lock and `syncAll` a whole-pass lock keyed by the root, both so the timer and a manual sync never race the same repo; `PassSeen` remembers a peer's first failure for the rest of a pass, so an asleep machine costs one timeout, not one per repo (amendment 7); the gate itself, wired into `src/cli/index.ts`'s `peers gate` subcommand (which answers a seed with canopy's mirror of it and refuses every other path into the seeds dir, `gateCommand`), clears every `GIT_*` variable from its own process env before doing anything (so the in-process `serveList`/`serveSeeds` calls inherit none), spawns `git-upload-pack` with an explicit minimal env (`PATH`, `HOME`, `GIT_PROTOCOL` and `LANG`/`LC_ALL` when set) and nothing else, and reads the config read-only, refusing every command on one it cannot read or parse rather than falling back to defaults; integration tests wire peers by plain path, which `git` treats as a remote the same as ssh. Workspaces carry an optional look: `Workspace.primary` (the member new code goes in, one of `repos`) and `color` (one of `WS_COLORS`, the palette's own token names), and `effectivePrimary` reads the marked primary or else the first member. `normalizeWorkspace` runs on every stored workspace at load and drops a primary that is not a member and a color outside the palette rather than keeping them to fail later; `setWorkspaceLook(name, {primary?, color?})` sets or clears (`null`) each field without touching membership, throwing "unknown workspace" or "not a member of <name>"; and `removeWorkspace`'s repo removal clears the primary when it was the one removed. `PATCH /api/workspaces` (`{name, primary?, color?}`, the primary a repo id converted at the edge) answers 404 for an unknown workspace and 400 for a non-member, a member on another host (a run starts only on this machine) or an unknown color, and broadcasts the `workspaces` event. `POST /api/workspaces/run` (`{name, action, note, client?}`) starts a run on the effective primary through `startRepoRun`, the same checks `POST /api/repos/run` makes (it refuses a primary on another host or on the forge itself, so a hand-edited config cannot slip one past it), with a `WorkspaceScope` (`workspace`, `primary`, `others`, `skipped`): `splitMembers` sorts the other members into local repos, which the runner passes to Claude as `--add-dir` (`ActionSpec.addDirs`), and ones it cannot open here, which are skipped and named in the run's first note ("left out of this run"), namely a member on another host, on the forge, missing from the last scan, or an incubator seed (the server passes `isSeedPath` into `splitMembers`, which stays browser-safe). A seed's agents run only through the incubator, behind its git guards, so a seed member never goes in as `--add-dir` and a seed primary refuses the run with `SEED_AGENT_REFUSAL`. `buildPrompt` then names the workspace and its primary and states the safety rules over "these folders", `Run.workspace` records it, and a chat keeps the scope for its later messages. A workspace run is Claude Code's alone and Codex is refused with "workspace runs need Claude Code". Any local member with a run or a workflow going answers 409 before anything starts, and the repo-run route now answers 409 too when the repo already has a run going. The limits are accepted for v1: while the run goes only the primary is locked by the runner, so another run can start in a member, and the card's change fingerprint (`statusFingerprint`) covers the primary alone, so a change the agent makes in a member does not show as the run's result. Opening a workspace in the agent opener (`openGroup`, from the route, the helper and `canopy ws open --app agent`) follows the primary the same way: `agentTabs` gives one agent tab at the effective primary, with every other member on the primary's machine passed as `--add-dir` (`AgentLaunch.dirs`, last on the line since claude's flag takes every plain word after it), and a tab of its own for each member on another machine. The helper intent's `group.primary` is optional, so a backend from before it gets the first member. The kitty, code, terminal, finder and herdr openers still open every member.

Plan, then build and background subagents (`docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-06-kilo-borrowings-design.md`, "P" numbers are its probes): the `propose` action (`RUN_ACTIONS`, `ACTIONS.propose` in `actions.ts`, mode `ask`, `maxTurns` 200 since one reply covers the plan and the build) starts Claude in plan mode through `ActionSpec.permissionMode` and `DriveSpec.permissionMode`, which win over the agent's own `bypassPermissions`/`default`; a yolo agent also gets `--allow-dangerously-skip-permissions`, since a later switch to bypass is refused without it (P8), and the server refuses `propose` on Codex before the workspace and install checks (400, "plan, then build needs Claude Code"). The names `propose`, `proposal` and `todos` avoid "plan" and "tasks" on purpose: "plan" is already the pre-flight sheet (`planWords`) and "tasks" is the repo's named processes. In plan mode the CLI asks `can_use_tool` for `ExitPlanMode`, and `ClaudeDriver.permission` turns that into a `proposal` `RunPrompt` (`{kind: "proposal", plan, auto}`, `auto` true only for a yolo agent) after `ctx.proposal(plan)` has put the text on `run.proposal`, an empty plan being denied with a message to present it again. Only an approval allows it (the driver reads a bare `allow` as a non-auto approve, but `answerMisfit` keeps one from arriving through the answer route); a `deny` carries the user's note back as the reason, which is how "revise" works (the agent plans again and the next `ExitPlanMode` replaces `run.proposal`), and a bare deny is "turn it down". Canopy answers allow first and then sends `set_permission_mode`, because the allow resets the session to default mode (P8): `PermissionReply.after` holds the switch, `switchMode` asks for `bypassPermissions` only when `a.auto && agent.yolo` (the driver re-checks, and `RunCtx.answer` and the driver downgrade an `auto` the prompt never offered) and falls back to `acceptEdits` if bypass is refused, and a switch that errors or gets no `control_response` in `REQUEST_MS` (10 s) still leaves the plan allowed and says so in a note. A non-yolo repo's approve is therefore always `acceptEdits`. `answerMisfit` (`driver.ts`, shared by `RunCtx.answer` and `Runner.misfit`) refuses an answer that does not fit the waiting prompt, and `POST` of the answer route returns 400 before any rule is kept: a proposal takes only `approve` or `deny`, and `approve` fits nothing else (`allow`, `allow-all` and `answers` to a proposal are refused, since they would settle it as a turn-down nobody chose). Ahead of that, the route answers 404 "no such run" for a run the runner does not hold, 404 "this run never asked that prompt" for an id the run never gave a prompt (`Runner.asked`, prompt ids being p1, p2 and on), and 409 "that prompt is no longer waiting" for one it asked and is not waiting on (answered, withdrawn, or the run over; `Runner.waiting`), and `RunCtx.answer`'s `NotWaitingError` maps to the same 409 when another answer settles it while a remembered rule is being kept. `parseAnswer` moved to `server/answers.ts` and knows `{kind: "approve", auto}`. `settleNote` writes the timeline note ("approved the plan, running on its own" or ", asking before commands", "sent the plan back: <note>", "turned the plan down"). The settle state is also kept on the run as `run.proposalState` (`ProposalState` in `types.ts`: `waiting`, `approved`, `approved-auto`, `sent back`, `turned down`), since the note falls off the front of the timeline once a long build passes `STEP_CAP`: `RunCtx.proposal` sets the text and `waiting` together, so new text never sits under the last one's label, and `park`'s settle sets the state through `proposalSettled` for a `proposal` prompt only while its plan is still the text on show (a stop or a withdraw lands on `turned down`, like the note). P9: every propose run leaves a plan file in `~/.claude/plans` on the machine whose CLI ran it (on the mini, the bind-mounted `~/.claude`); canopy neither reads nor cleans it. Stdin and canopy's own requests: `request()` sends a `control_request` (`canopy-N` ids) and settles on the matching `control_response`, on an error, on `REQUEST_MS` or when stdout ends (`closed`); those replies are not the CLI working on a turn, so they leave the held-result grace alone. `endInput` closes a job's stdin only once every pending request and every follow-up (the mode switch is tracked from before its allow goes out) has settled, because closing it first cuts them off (P6); it waits with `Promise.allSettled`, and `control()` catches a follow-up that throws, so a failed switch neither holds stdin open nor goes unhandled. The held result: a `result` that arrives while a `local_agent` task is live is held with stdin open (P5; a background shell never holds one), and `heldGraceMs` applies it once the subagents are done unless a turn begins. The `woke` flag is that exception: subagents finishing wake the main thread for a new turn (a fresh `init`, a message or a prompt; `wakes` never counts a message carrying `parent_tool_use_id`, since a subagent's straggler after the task list empties is not the main thread's turn and would otherwise switch the grace off), and a held result waits that turn out, so the run ends on the result of the turn that really ends it; with no new turn the grace ends the run with the held one, an exit applies it, a crash while one is held fails the run, and a stop drops it. The CLI counts `num_turns` and `duration_ms` per result, so a result after a hold (or a held one the run ends on) adds the turns of every result held before it (`heldTurns`) and is timed from the message that began the turn (`turnStart`), which is what the footer shows; `total_cost_usd` is already the session's running total and is taken as it comes. A run that stops or fails ends every tool step still `running` as `error` in `RunCtx.finish` (the process is gone, so no tool_result comes; a plan waiting when the run stopped is one), the way Codex marks an interrupted item. Todos: `core/todos.ts` (pure, browser-safe) reads the agent's checklist off main-thread todo tools only (`TODO_TOOLS`: TaskCreate, TaskUpdate, TaskList, TaskGet, TodoWrite; a call with a `parent_tool_use_id` is a subagent's and never reaches it). `TaskCreate` and `TaskUpdate` are kept by id in `todoInputs` until their result arrives and applied only when it is not an error (`todoCreated` takes the id from "Task #N created", `todoUpdated` handles status, subject and `deleted`); `TodoWrite` replaces the whole list in one go (`todoWritten`, ids are indexes). `ctx.todos` sets `run.todos` (`RunTodo`: `id`, `subject`, `status`, optional `active`). `RunStep.parent` is the step id of the `Agent` call a subagent's steps ran under, found through `parent_tool_use_id` in the `tools` map. `describeTool` words `TaskCreate`/`TaskUpdate` as "todo: ..." steps, and the inbox, the feed and the tailchan hand-off (`tailchan.ts`) word a waiting proposal as "has a plan to approve".

## src/server

`src/server/` — Bun.serve: REST + SSE (`/api/events`) + static. State holds one `SourceRuntime` per scanned folder (the launch root first, then config `sources`); `scanOne` swaps a source's repos into the aggregate `result` and `rebuildResult` reorders by source. One recursive `fs.watch` per local source maps changed paths (relative to that source) to the longest-matching repo via `repoRel`, debounces 400ms, re-reads status, broadcasts; ssh sources have no watcher and get their statuses re-read every `REMOTE_REFRESH` (5 min); the same timer, and once `ACTIVITY_DELAY` after start, runs `refreshActivity`: `fetchLocal` fetches every local repo's own remotes (`ownRemotesOf`, memoized per path in `state.own` over `listRemotes` and `ownRemotes`), `FETCH_CONCURRENCY` at a time, skipping a repo with an active run since a fetch would move the fingerprint its outcome is judged by, and calls `scheduleRefresh` (the same 400ms debounce the watcher uses, so the two coalesce) for each repo whose refs moved or whose own remotes were just learned; `scanOpts` hands `scanSource` a `tipRemotes` hook and `refreshAndBroadcast` hands `refreshRepo` the same list, so a status read only ever takes a tip off the user's own remotes and none at all before the first pass has looked; then `refreshPulls` runs `PULLS_QUERY` through gh, keeps the map in `state.pulls`, and broadcasts each repo whose `pulls` changed; `rebuildResult` re-attaches the counts through `linkPulls` so a scan keeps them; config `fetch: false` skips the fetch; a forgejo source is a server rather than a folder, so `scanSource` lists it through the API, it gets no watcher, and the same timer re-lists it. `rebuildResult` runs `linkForgeClones` over the whole tree, since which forge repos are already cloned here can only be told once every source is in one list, and every `/api/repos/*` route answers 400 for a repo with a `forge`: there is no checkout to run git in. Sources: `GET/POST/DELETE /api/sources` (POST validates: local paths must be directories and not overlap an existing local source, ssh hosts must match `isSshHost` and the path is resolved with `cd … && pwd -P` on the host; a 502 means ssh itself failed; a forge is proved by listing its repos, 400 for a bad address or a refused token, 502 for one that will not answer), `POST /api/sources/rescan?id=`, `GET /api/hosts` (aliases from `~/.ssh/config`), `GET /api/browse?path=&host=` (one folder's visible subfolders with a repo flag; local via readdir in `core/browse.ts`, remote as one `sh -c` script that prints `pwd -P` then `r name`/`d name` lines, parsed by `parseBrowse`). `/api/repos/run` answers 400 for a repo with a `host`. Runs: `POST /api/repos/run?id=` starts one, `/api/runs` lists, `/api/runs/answer|stop|say` and `DELETE /api/runs?id=` drive it (`say` is a chat's next message); every change broadcasts the whole `Run` as a `run` event (`run-gone` on dismiss). Agent routing: see the harnesses bullet below; `/api/repos/open`, `/api/repos/run`, `/api/workspaces/open` and the flow and fleet routes resolve through it by role. Archive: config `archived` is the repo paths archived in canopy (`setArchived`), held in `state.archived`; `PULLS_QUERY` also reads GitHub's `isArchived` into `PullCount.archived`, and the pure `linkArchived` (`core/archive.ts`, after `linkPulls` in `rebuildResult` and `refreshPulls`) sets each repo's `archived` to `canopy` or `github` through `archivedBy`, canopy's mark winning; `POST /api/repos/archive?id=` `{archived}` sits ahead of the forge refusal so a forge-only card takes it too, and answers and broadcasts the repo. The UI's `hideArchived` setting (on by default, the filter menu's "hide archived" chip) drops them in `scopedRepos`; `archivedCount` counts them either way, and the card menu's "in canopy" row archives or unarchives. Favorites work the same way: config `favorites` is the starred repo paths (`setFavorite`), held in `state.favorites`, the pure `linkFavorites` (`core/favorite.ts`, last in `rebuildResult`) sets `favorite: true`, and `POST /api/repos/favorite?id=` `{favorite}` sits beside the archive route ahead of the forge refusal. In the UI a card is a favorite when any of its checkouts is (`cardFavorite` in `checkouts.ts`, read through `isFavorite`, `boardFavorite` and `favoriteCount` in the store); `Star.tsx` is the ☆/★ on a card and the panel head (it swallows its own click and keys like `RepoLink`, all but Escape, through `keepKeys` in `surface.ts`, which `RepoLink`, the run, flow, task and agent chips, the lead chip on a card and the pull count use too, so a panel filling the window leaves with the focus on any of them; a starred card's tree row shows a plain ★ since the row is itself a button), the card menu's "in canopy" row stars too, and the store's `favoriteRepo` unstarring takes the star off every checkout of the card. The store's `favoritesOnly` (the filter menu's "★ favorites only" chip, or `*`) rides in `RepoQuery.favorites`, is part of `visibleCards`' memo key, counts in `activeFilterCount` and goes with `clearFilters`; the `favorites` grouping (★ in the sort control) buckets favorites first, then everything else, each newest change first, with `groupRepos`' `fav` hook fed `boardFavorite` on a board with several backends. These are canopy's own stars, not the Library's devhub favorites. Drill: `GET /api/repos/commit?id=&hash=` (400 for a non-hash, 404 for an unknown one) and `GET /api/repos/diff?id=&file=&commit=&orig=` for one file's diff in that commit. Shells: `GET /api/term?id=&term=&place=&cols=&rows=` upgrades to a websocket (404 unknown repo, 400 forge or a malformed `term`, 426 without an upgrade); `term` is the browser's 32-hex name for the shell: a name the server holds is joined (the `Scrollback` replays first, then the pty takes the socket's size so a full-screen program repaints), any other starts a shell under it, and `attach=1` joins only, closing with `TERM_GONE` (4404) for a name not held; binary frames are keystrokes down and pty output up, a text frame is `{"resize":{"cols","rows"}}` down and `{"exit":code}` up before the close. A socket closing detaches and leaves the shell running (several sockets may sit on one shell, every one gets its output); `state.terms` is the `Map` by name, `GET /api/terms` lists them as `TermInfo` (with `attached`), `DELETE /api/terms?term=` ends one, and stop ends them all. `POST /api/terms/paste?term=` takes an image as the raw body (png, jpeg, gif or webp by `Content-Type`, up to `PASTE_MAX`, 415/413/400 otherwise, 404 for a shell not held, 400 for one on an ssh host) and saves it under `pastes/` in the config dir, the volume the shells container shares, clearing ones older than `PASTE_DAYS` (`core/paste.ts`, pure but for `savePaste`, tested); it answers `{path, text}`, and `TermView` catches an image paste (capture phase, ahead of xterm's) or drop, uploads it and `term.paste`s `text`, which Claude Code attaches as an image, since a browser terminal carries only text and the backend has no clipboard. On tmux (`state.tmux`, the argv front, non-null when `tmuxBase()` found it) a `LiveTerm` has `pty: null` and `joinTmuxTerm` does the work: `newSession` for a name not held, `TERM_GONE` for a held name whose session died, then `history` sent as the first frame, then the socket's own client (`ws.data.client`) attached to the session, which `close(ws)` detaches; a client exiting while the session lives (a `tmux detach` inside) closes the socket with no exit frame so the browser rejoins with a new client, and one exiting with the session sends `{exit}` and drops the entry. `listTerms` reconciles the `Map` with `listSessions` on every list and at startup, which is how a restarted server finds the shells the last one left; `endTerm` is `kill-session`. Shells kept across a reboot (Phase 4): `GET /api/terms/kept` is `{ keeping, kept }` (the config switch and the `KeptShell[]` no live session answers for), `POST /api/terms/restore` `{term, cols, rows, resume}` starts one again **under the same id**, so every saved tab and `?term=` url comes back to it, with the old history and `restoredBanner` sent ahead of the session's own through `history(..., ahead)` and dropped once `historySize` says the new session has scrolled a screen of its own, and `resume` types `resumeLine` in after a beat when the record has a `session`, else `continueLine` (404 for a name nothing was kept under, 409 for one that is running, 400 for a repo that left the scan); `DELETE /api/terms/kept?term=` forgets one, `POST /api/keep {on}` writes the config switch and takes a first pass; the snapshot timer is `KEEP_EVERY` and `keepPass` never stacks two; a `kept` event carries the list to every browser, and ending a shell forgets its record. `keepOne` is one shell's snapshot (the pass runs it over every held shell, it adds the card's `session`); `endTerm(state, id, { keep: true })`, the sweep's end, marks the shell `ending`, waits out a running pass, writes a `swept` record through `keepOne` whatever `keepShells` says, and only then kills the session and leaves the record (false and nothing killed when the snapshot fails); `LiveTerm.kept` stops an attached client's exit from forgetting that record. `sweepShell` (the hub's `endShell`) notes when it began, and a keystroke after that, read once the record is written, means someone is at the shell: it stays running, its swept record is forgotten, and the answer is `in use`. The keep-end's promise sits on `LiveTerm.keepEnd` while it runs, and a plain end (a user's DELETE) that arrives then waits for it instead of killing the shell under its snapshot; once the keep-end has kept and ended the shell, the plain end forgets the record and refreshes the kept list, so the user's end wins and the record never comes back after the forget. A session that is gone when a client exits is only a shell that exited when `serverUp` says canopy's tmux server is still answering: the shells container restarting under a live canopy kills every client at once, and forgetting the records there would throw away exactly what the restart is supposed to leave behind. `canopy ui --no-open` starts the server without opening a browser tab (a launchd-restarted server otherwise opens one per restart, each a window on the same shells). Search: `GET /api/repos/grep?id=&q=` is one repo, `POST /api/grep {q, ids}` is many (one `GrepRepoResult` per id in the order given, `GREP_CONCURRENCY` at a time, a failed repo carries `error` instead of failing the call); `grepQuery` refuses an empty, multi-line or over-long term with 400. `POST /api/repos/openfile?id=` `{file, line}` opens the file in VS Code through `openFile` in openers (`fileOpenArgs` is pure: `code -g path:line`, behind `--remote ssh-remote+host` for a remote repo). History: `GET /api/history` (the overview, memoized 5 min and per scan; `?refresh=1` forces), `GET /api/repos/sessions|session|search?id=` and `POST /api/repos/note?id=` (opens the vault note); all answer 503 with the reason when the CLI is missing and 404 when the repo has no project in the archive. Workflows and flows: `GET /api/repos/workflows?id=` is one repo's list (ok and broken entries both); `GET /api/verdict` is `{ ready: hasGatewayKey() }`, what the UI reads to know whether a `verdict` gate can evaluate or will fall back to `ask`; `POST /api/repos/run?id=` with `action=flow` starts a flow for one repo (400 for a `host` repo — flows are local-only — and for an unknown or broken workflow name; a body's `request` that already started a flow answers 200 with it, or 409 when that flow is on another repo); `GET /api/flows` lists every flow (including the ones restored from disk at startup), `DELETE /api/flows?id=` dismisses one, `POST /api/flows/resume` (`{id, choice}`, `continue|retry|stop`) and `POST /api/flows/stop` drive a gated or running one (a stop during a check answers with the flow still working; it ends once the check is killed). Fleets: `GET /api/fleets` lists them, `POST /api/fleet` (`{workflow, ids, note, request?}`, a known `request` answering 200 with its fleet) starts one over several repos found in `state.result.repos`, `POST /api/fleet/stop` and `DELETE /api/fleet?id=` drive and dismiss one. Every change broadcasts as one of four events: `flow`, `flow-gone`, `fleet`, `fleet-gone`, the whole Flow or Fleet each time (a flow's own step Runs still broadcast as `run`, so the console component needs no flow-awareness). Launcher: `GET /api/repos/releases|pulls|builds?id=`, `POST /api/repos/install?id=` `{tag, asset?}` and `POST /api/repos/build?id=` `{pr?}` each answer 201 with a `Job`, `POST /api/repos/launch?id=` `{build}` answers the `Build` after the launch, `halt` ends its process, `uninstall` removes it, `launcher` stores the repo's `LaunchSettings` and broadcasts the map as `launchers`; `GET /api/launchers`, `GET /api/jobs`, `POST /api/jobs/stop`, `DELETE /api/jobs?id=`. Jobs broadcast as `job`/`job-gone`; a `builds` event names the repo whose builds changed outside a job (launched, exited, removed) or that a job just installed or built one, and the panel re-reads the list on it. A `LauncherError` carries its own HTTP status. Releases work for any repo with a GitHub remote, wherever its checkout is (the download lands here); builds are local-only. Peers: `refreshPeers` runs `peerPass` on the same `REMOTE_REFRESH` timer right after `fetchLocal`, queued through `queuePass`/`withPeering`, a true FIFO over `state.peering`, so the timer's own pass, a manual `/api/peers/sync`, and every route action (sync, take, track and seed, all through `runPeerAction`) never touch the same repo at once, and a repeat call while one is pending or running joins it instead of enqueueing a second. `peerPass` reads the config (or `CANOPY_PEERS_JSON` under `peerSettingsFromEnv`, honored only when `NODE_ENV` is `test`), runs `initRepo` once per peerable repo not yet in `state.inited` (`peerable`: `LAUNCH_SOURCE`, no `host`, no `forge`, no scan error, the same test `ui/src/peers.ts`'s own `peerable` mirrors), then `syncAll` over the rest, folds each repo's `PeerState` in through `applyPeerState` (notifies once per new divergence through `ctl notify soft`, schedules a status re-read when something moved, broadcasts the repo only when its `PeerState` actually changed), and rescans when `cloneMissing` landed a real clone. `ownRemotesOf` keeps a repo's peer remotes out of its own-remotes memo through `isPeerRemote` (the no-push marker, or an exact `peerUrl` match), and `notePeerList`/`samePeerList` clear that memo only when the peer list itself changed. Both read `state.peerNamesSeen`, never the disk. `linkPeers` re-attaches `state.peerStates` in `rebuildResult` the way `linkPulls` attaches pull counts. `GET /api/peers` answers `{ self, peers, seen, sync }`; `POST /api/peers/sync` kicks the pass and answers 202; `POST /api/repos/peer?id=` `{ action: "sync" | "take" | "track" | "seed", peer?, branch? }` answers 400 for a repo `peerable` rejects or a malformed body, 404 for an unknown peer, and 409 when `peerSync` is off (dry still runs `sync`, `take` and `track`, which are explicit user actions regardless of mode) or the repo has an active run or flow; a `peers` event carries `seen`. The three routes and the peering queue (`withPeering`, `queuePass`, `runPeerAction`) live in `src/server/peers.ts`, under the approve list in `.canopy/review.json`; index.ts routes to them where it always did and hands over its state and the helpers the pass shares with them (`peerSettings`, `refreshPeers`, `peerable`, `applyPeerState`, `refreshAndBroadcast`) through `PeerDeps`, and a route refusal is a `PeerError` carrying its own HTTP status, the way a `LauncherError` does. The pass itself stays in index.ts. `CANOPY_ORIGINS` (comma-separated exact origins, `parseOrigins` in `core/cors.ts`) names the other canopy pages that may drive this backend: `libraryOriginAllowed` lets a listed Origin through even cross-site, and a request with no Origin whose Host is a listed origin's host (a listed page's own GET behind `tailscale serve`); every `/api/*` answer to a listed origin carries `corsHeaders` (that origin, credentials, `Vary: Origin`, never `*`), and an `OPTIONS` preflight is answered before any route (204 with `PREFLIGHT_HEADERS`, else 403). `GET /api/backends` is `{ self, backends }` off config `backends` (`normalizeBackends` and `selfName` in `core/backends.ts`); see `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-26-multi-backend-design.md` and its amendments 6-8, and the client side (the registry, qualified ids, the join, the machine strip and switcher, the backends chip) in the `ui/` bullet below.

Screen layout profiles (`src/server/layouts.ts`, `ScreenLayouts`, tested in `layouts.test.ts`) are kept in `screen-layouts.json` in the config dir (mode 0600, written through a temp file and a rename, one write at a time): the user's own `profiles` and a `presets` map holding only the presets the user changed. `GET /api/layouts` lists the presets (each as changed, else as `PRESETS` in `core/screenlayouts.ts` ships it) and then the user's own. `POST /api/layouts` with `{profile}` creates one (201, id `p-` and 12 hex, at most 200), with `{from}` copies any profile as the user's own, named "… copy". `PUT /api/layouts?id=` changes one, a preset included (`edited: true`); `DELETE` drops a user's own and refuses a preset (400); `POST /api/layouts/reset?id=` puts a preset back and refuses a user's own. Every body goes through `parseProfile`, and a kept file is read back through it too (`parseKept`), so a hand-edited entry that does not check out is dropped, not trusted.

Every request starts at `route()`, which first asks `callerOf` who is calling and runs the caller gate (`src/server/callers.ts`), then the Library hand-off, the origin gate and `authRoute`. A refusal never reaches a handler. Section "Callers and tokens".

## The in-app browser (preview)

The in-app browser (preview): `core/ports.ts` lists what listens on the backend's loopback (`parseProcNet` over `/proc/net/tcp{,6}` with little-endian hex addresses, socket inodes mapped to pids through `/proc/<pid>/fd` for `cwd`/`comm`; `parseLsofListen`/`parseLsofCwd` on a Mac; `mergeListeners` one per port, v4 first; `repoOfCwd` the deepest repo holding a cwd; all but the fs walk pure and tested). `core/preview.ts` is the proxy: `PreviewProxy` holds a pool of ports (`parsePortRange` over `CANOPY_PREVIEW_PORTS`, default `7860-7869`, `0` off), `pickSlot` puts a backend port on one (reuse, free, else least recently used), a lazy `Bun.serve` per slot answers only to `previewHostOk` hostnames and proxies HTTP (`upstreamHeaders` rewrites Host/Origin/Referer to `localhost:<port>` and asks for identity encoding; `downstreamHeaders` rewrites a `Location` on the dev server, drops `X-Frame-Options` and CSP `frame-ancestors` so the panel can frame it, and keeps every `Set-Cookie`) and websockets (the first requested subprotocol, `vite-hmr`, answered; frames queued until the upstream socket opens), trying the other loopback on a refused bodiless request. A preview is its own origin by port, which is why this is not a path prefix under canopy: dev servers' absolute paths and HMR need the root, and a service worker needs a secure context the tailnet's http does not give. The public https page cannot frame a slot's plain http, so `CANOPY_PREVIEW_PUBLIC` (`https://canopy-p{slot}.beric.ca`, read by the browser-safe `core/previewPublic.ts`: `parsePreviewPublic`, `publicPreviewOrigin`) gives each slot a name of its own on the tunnel, which the slot's listener lets in (and takes as the preview's origin for a rewritten redirect) beside `previewHostOk`; `docs/deploy.md` lists the DNS and ingress it needs. Routes: `GET /api/ports` (`PortsResult`, canopy's and the slots' ports left out, each port tagged with the local repo its cwd is in, and `public`, the template, when set; the panel reads it before it calls an https page blocked, and `previewUrl` takes the slot's public name on such a page; a checkout on another backend previews through that backend's own `/api/ports` and `/api/preview` and its own public names, `canopy-mac-p{slot}.beric.ca` for the Mac, and without them `previewBlocked` says so; `previewTarget` in `ui/src/preview.ts` takes the public names only on an https page of their own site, since the gate in front of them keeps a `SameSite=Lax` cookie a cross-site frame never carries, and on a plain http page frames another backend's slots at its `CANOPY_PREVIEW_HOST`, the tailnet IP compose fills from `CANOPY_LISTEN` and `/api/ports` hands out as `host`, an IP because `*.ts.net` is HSTS-preloaded). A task runs with `BROWSER=none` (`taskCommand`), so a dev server's open flag started from a phone puts no window on the backend's desktop and `POST /api/preview {port}` (`PreviewSlot`; 400 for canopy's own or a slot, 503 when off). In the container, canopy runs with `network_mode`/`pid: service:shells` so the shells' loopback and processes are its own; the ports are published on `shells`, which carries the network alias `canopy` for the tunnel. UI: `Preview.tsx`'s `PreviewSection` (section key `preview`, in `DEFAULT_CLOSED`, not shown for a `host` repo; ⧉ on its head brings the project's bench to the front with the frame as its main pane, ⤢ fills the panel, and in place a `TermGrip` under the frame drags its height, the per-browser `previewHeight` setting clamped by `previewHeightOf` to `PREVIEW_H`) and the pure `ui/src/preview.ts` (`previewTarget`, public name, http host or blocked, which `previewBlocked` and `previewUrl` read, `previewPath`, `portsFor`, and the per-browser choice under localStorage `canopy.previews`, `readChoices`); tests in `ports.test.ts`, `preview.test.ts` (with a real proxy against a stand-in dev server) and `ui/src/preview.test.ts`.

Design mode: each slot serves the element picker at `PICK_PATH` (`/__canopy/pick.js`, the script in `core/pickscript.ts`) after the host check and before the slot lookup, so it answers while the slot is idle, and `withPicker` (Bun's `HTMLRewriter`) appends its tag to the head, else the body, else the end of the document, of any response `injectable` passes (GET, 200, `text/html`, `sec-fetch-dest` document, iframe or absent, so fragments and JSON are left alone).
