The script stays inert unless framed and only reads messages from `window.parent`: the panel's ⌖ (`ModeButtons` in `.preview-bar` and `.preview-float`) sends `{canopy:"pick", on}`, and the page answers each enable with `{canopy:"pick-ready"}`, a click with `{canopy:"picked", selector, html, text, path, styles, rect, via}` and Esc with `{canopy:"pick-off"}`, always to the origin that enabled it. The parent also sends `{canopy:"marks", list}` (selectors, at most 50), and the page draws a numbered badge at each element's top right on a fixed layer that follows scroll and resize; an empty list clears it. `PreviewSection` re-sends the enable on every frame load (an HMR reload resets the script), says "this page blocked the picker (CSP?)" when no ack comes within `PICK_ACK_MS`, and takes a message only from the frame's own window at the preview origin while picking is on; the repo is the panel's, never the message's. The pure `ui/src/designmode.ts` checks and cuts a pick (`readPick`, `PICK_MAX`), A pick's form takes a note and an `Intent` (change or question). "add" pushes an `Annotation` onto the panel's list and turns picking back on; the list empties when the port changes, and `marksMessage` re-sends it to the frame after every change and load. `annotationsPrompt` numbers the notes, fences everything each page reported as data, addresses the dev server's own `devUrl(port, path)`, and closes with an ask that matches the intents (questions are answered, not acted on). "send now" hands it to the store's `sendToAgent` (an idle chat's next turn, else pasted into a shell of the repo running Claude Code or Codex with Enter left to the user, else a new chat; `ui/src/agentsend.ts` holds the shell search `askAgent` shares). The notes bar (`components/PickNotes.tsx`) has the same prompt behind "send ▾": the repo's agent as above, each of the repo's shells with a mounted view where tmux reports an agent (`agentCandidates` and `runsAgent`, pasted with `typeInto`), a new chat (`startRun` with `chat`), or a new panel shell per installed harness (`openTerm` with the prompt, greyed past the server's 6000-character `PROMPT_MAX`), plus "copy all".

Pictures and marks (FR-029): while picking, C and S act on the element under the pointer, in the page (its key handler) or in canopy (`PreviewSection` asks with `{canopy:"hovered", via}`); the page answers with the same `picked` message, `via` `"copy"` (picking goes on; the panel puts `pickText` on the clipboard) or `"shot"` (the page stops itself first, so its outline is out of the picture). The ⬚ clip and ✎ draw switches put a layer over the frame (`components/PreviewLayers.tsx`: `ClipLayer` drags a box, `DrawLayer` keeps pen, arrow, box and text `Shape`s from the pure `ui/src/markup.ts`, and `DrawBar` floats over the frame's top edge with the tool, four colours, undo, clear, "snap" and "done"). The framed page is another origin, so no DOM capture sees into it: `ui/src/shots.ts` (`Shooter`, browser-only) takes `getDisplayMedia` with `preferCurrentTab` once per design-mode session, holds the stream while anything is going (`idle` lets it go, so does unmount), and cuts a png of a viewport box from a frame of it (`canShoot` needs a secure page; a window or screen share is refused since the crop would not line up). `takeClip` in `PreviewSection` sets the `shooting` class first, which hides everything floating over the frame, then shoots the box (an element's own rect, the dragged box, or the whole frame with the marks on it), posts it to `POST /api/repos/clip?id=` (saved with `savePaste` as `pasteName("design", …)` beside the shells' pastes, 400 for a repo on another host, 415 for anything but an image) and opens the note form with the `Clip` (`kind`, the backend `path`, the viewport `rect`, `marks` as `markupWords`, and a `thumb` object URL for the page alone, revoked when the note goes). An `Annotation` is a `pick`, a `clip` or both; `marksMessage` sends a blank for a note without an element, which the page skips. `annotationsPrompt` puts a "Picture n: path, what it is of. Read that file to see it." line under the note (or that no picture could be taken, with the box) and the marks in words, and opens the ask with two steps when they apply: organize the notes by component and say the plan first (two or more notes), and read each picture file before acting on its note.

## The files section

The files section: `core/finder.ts` lists a checkout with `git ls-files -z --cached --others --exclude-standard` through `git()` (`parseFiles` folds repeats, sorts, cuts at `FILES_LIMIT`), so a remote repo and a seed list the same way and the listing leaves gitignored paths out (a raw read still serves any path inside the repo, `.env` included, as a shell there would). `readRaw` reads one file: `safeRel` refuses an empty, absolute, `..`, `.`, empty-part, backslash or NUL path first, then a local file must `realpath` inside the repo's own `realpath` (a symlink out is a 403) and stream as a `Bun.file`; a remote one is one ssh round trip (`rawCommand`: `cd`, `realpath` and a `case` on the prefix, `wc -c`, then `base64`, exit codes 3 to 6 for no folder, no file, outside and too big) decoded here, since `exec` reads stdout as text. Caps: `RAW_MAX` (200 MB local, 25 MB remote) and the request's own `max`, past which it is a 413 that names the size. The routes are `GET /api/repos/ls` and `GET /api/repos/raw?path=&max=` (a seed's raw read is refused: its files may live with its stage). Every raw answer carries `x-content-type-options: nosniff` and `content-security-policy: sandbox; default-src 'none'`, and `core/filekind.ts` (`mimeOf`) sends anything that is not a known image, video, audio or PDF as `text/plain` or `application/octet-stream`, so a repo's HTML or SVG opened at that address can never run as a page of canopy's origin. The UI (`components/Finder.tsx`, pure parts in `ui/src/finder.ts`) builds folders from the flat list (`entriesAt`), finds by name (`findFiles`), fetches bytes with `api.rawFile` (a credentialed fetch, so another backend's gate cookie rides along) and shows text decoded (`looksBinary` turns a NUL in the first 8000 bytes into "no preview", `LINE_CAP` lines drawn) and media from an object URL revoked on change. A PDF goes in an iframe only as a blob URL whose bytes are a PDF. HTML's page view is `HtmlView`: an iframe with `sandbox=""` (no scripts, no forms, an opaque origin) whose `srcdoc` is the file behind `HTML_POLICY`, a CSP meta that lets it fetch nothing, so its relative images and stylesheets do not load either. `ui/src/rich.ts` loads highlight.js (its common languages), marked and DOMPurify on first use, so they sit in chunks of their own: source is coloured by extension (or a few names, `BY_NAME`) up to `HIGHLIGHT_MAX` characters, and markdown is parsed, its code blocks coloured, then sanitized, with a DOMPurify hook moving a relative image's address to `data-src` so the page never asks canopy's origin for it; `MarkdownView` resolves it (`resolveRel`) and fetches it as a file, and turns a relative link into opening that file in the section. `useFile` tags what it read with the file it read, so the render that names a new file never shows the old one's bytes under the new name. The section's **ignored** switch adds `GET /api/repos/dir?path=` (`listDir`: one sh script here or over ssh, the folder resolved inside the repo like a raw read, `.git` left out, NUL-ended `d/` and `f/` entries) and merges what only the disk has (`withIgnored`), marked and with no count. The listing reloads when the repo's last commit or changed paths move, a changed file's preview when its `mtime` does, and `canopy.finder` in localStorage keeps each repo's folder, pick and its dotfiles and ignored switches (`FinderPlace`).

## Review notes

Review notes: `parseDiff` (`ui/src/reviewnotes.ts`) numbers each line of a unified diff from its hunk headers, starting over at each `diff --git` so a partly staged file's two joined diffs stay apart, and leaves anything outside a hunk (git's stderr included) unnumbered. `DiffView`, given a `note` prop by `FileRow` (the working tree) and `FileLine` (a commit), puts a gutter button on each numbered line (`NotedDiff`, `NotedLines`) that opens a note box (⌘/Ctrl-Enter saves, Esc cancels); a note is pinned by side, number and line text (`anchorOf`, `noteOn`) and kept per browser under `canopy.reviewNotes` (`loadNotes`, `saveNotes`). Without the prop the diff renders as before, and a worktree's or a landing's diff takes none, since its notes would go to the wrong checkout's agent; seeds, forge and remote repos take none either (`takesNotes`). The panel's `ReviewNotesStrip` calls the store's `sendNotes`, which builds one message with `notesPrompt` (each quoted line fenced as data, each note as the user's words, and a note on a commit asks for new changes rather than an amend, since a rewrite diverges a peer), hands it to `sendToAgent`, and drops only the notes it sent.

## tailchan

tailchan (`docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-25-tailchan-design.md`): the tailnet broker in `homelab/services/tailchan`. `core/tailchan.ts` is browser-safe and pure (tested): `chanTarget` ("#x"/bare is a channel, "@h" a DM, a `dm.a+b` name refused as a target), `readQuery`, `dmPeer`, `shellHandle(repoName, termId)` (slug cut to 30, then 4 hex of the id), `parseSse`, `asChanMessage`, and `runNotice`/`flowNotice`/`fleetNotice` (a prompt or gate is a DM to the human, an end a channel line, each only on a transition). `core/chan.ts` is the client over an injected fetch: `chanConfig(env, file)` reads `CANOPY_TAILCHAN_URL`, `TAILCHAN_URL`, then the CLI's `~/.config/tailchan/env` (not under `bun test`), the UI's handle from `CANOPY_TAILCHAN_AS`/`TAILCHAN_HUMAN`, the bot `canopy` and channel `canopy`; `Chan.follow` reconnects at doubling waits and resumes after the last id. `server/tailchan.ts`'s `ChanHub` (`state.chan`) owns `/api/tailchan*` (503 without a broker: GET info, `read`, `send`, `put` raw body, `blob` proxy, `notify`), one stream as the UI's handle broadcast as `chan` events, an echo of what the UI sends (the broker never returns a sender's own post), and canopy's posts while config `tailchanNotify` is on (hooked on the runner's and flows' onChange; a flow's step runs are skipped; the ranger's gave-up DM goes out either way). `startServer({ chan })` overrides the config, `null` turns it off; the tests run a stand-in broker (`server/tailchan.test.ts`). A new local shell gets `TAILCHAN_AS=shellHandle(...)` through `handleOf` (tmux `new-session -e` plus an `@canopy_handle` option `LIST_FORMAT` reads back, or the pty's env) and `TermInfo.handle`; none without a broker or for an ssh shell. UI: `ui/src/chan.ts` (pure, tested: `convTarget`/`convOf`, `convOrder`, `mergeMessages` capped at `CHAN_CAP`, `isUnread`, `chanLine`, `latestClip`), the store's `chan`/`chanAs`/`chanMsgs`/`chanUnread`/`chanOpen`/`chanConv` with `loadChan`, `openChan(target?)`, `showConv`, `sendChan`, `putChan`, `setChanNotify`, `Chan.tsx`'s `ChanChip` (the top bar's `✉ n` and popover, nothing when not ready), a `chan` feed kind, and the shells picker's handle, live dot and "message".

## The agent registry

The agent registry (agents spec, phase 3; the broker's side is `homelab/services/tailchan`, its API in the `tailchan` skill): every Claude Code and Codex on the tailnet as the broker's `AgentCard` (`types.ts`, field for field, with `AgentState`, `isLiveAgent`, `RegistryInfo`, `ScanProc`/`ScanBody` and the `registry` event `{cards, gone?}`; the settings event stays `agents`). Every new local shell (tmux `-e` through `TmuxMeta.env`, the pty's env, restored and resumed ones too; not an ssh line) gets `CANOPY_TERM` (its id), `CANOPY_BACKEND` (`state.backendName`, `selfName` read at start), `CANOPY_REPO` (the plain repo id) and `CANOPY_API` (`apiUrlFor` the bind, loopback for a wildcard, set once listening) beside `TAILCHAN_AS`, all through `shellEnv`/`canopyEnv` in `server/index.ts`, so codex gets them as its `-c shell_environment_policy.set.*` flags; a new tmux session also gets canopy's own `TZ` when it has one (`sessionEnv`, not in the agent flags), since the tmux server in the shells container has none and Bun, Claude Code included, reads the zone's name, which the image's `/etc/localtime` gives as `Etc/UTC`; `startServer` deletes those names (`INHERITED_ENV` in `core/term.ts`) from its own env first, so a canopy started inside a canopy shell hands the outer shell's on to nothing it starts. `core/agentscan.ts` (pure but for `scanProcs`, tested): Linux reads procs.ts's `linuxProcs` (comm, state and start off the one stat read through `parseStat`, `parseBootTime`, `tickTime`; `procCwd`/`procComm` are shared with ports.ts), a Mac `ps -axww -o pid=,ppid=,lstart=,args=` plus `ps -ax -o pid=,comm=` (two calls: a Mac comm is a path that may hold spaces; `parsePsLong`, `parsePsComm`, `parseLstart` in the C locale) and `lsof` cwds; `classify` (comm `claude`/`codex`, a version-file comm with claude in its path or argv, else keep.ts's `argvAgent` over the program and, after an interpreter only, its script; never the harnesses' own helpers, Claude's Chrome native host, its `daemon` and the daemon's `bg-pty-host`/`bg-spare` processes, and Codex's shared `app-server`, so a session the daemon hosts counts by its own version-file process and a Codex session by its TUI; a hooked Codex card names the app server's pid, so the broker drops a scan proc whose harness and folder match a live hooked card whose pid the scan does not name), `pickAgents` (no zombies; a child of the same harness, codex's native binary under its node wrapper, counted by the parent), `treeCpu` and `scanStates` (a scan card has no hooks, so its state is a guess: working when the agent's process tree (stopping at Claude's helpers, so a session that started the daemon is not charged for the sessions it hosts) used `BUSY_SHARE`, 3% of a core, between two scans, added up process by process so a child that exits never reads as CPU given back, else idle; `RegistryHub` keeps the last scan's `CpuSample` (each tree member's CPU by pid) per agent, and the Mac's `ps` reads `time=` for it, Linux utime and stime off the stat line), `scanBody` (repo as the checkout's `link` and branch, the deepest local repo by `repoOfCwd`, and the state when there is one), `inContainer`. `server/registry.ts`'s `RegistryHub` (`state.registry`, both jobs off with no broker config, `chanCfg` shared with `ChanHub`): the scan posts `/v1/agents/scan` as the bot handle every `SCAN_EVERY` (30s, first after 3s, one at a time, a failure logged once per streak; off under `bun test` and `CANOPY_AGENT_SCAN=0`, `startServer({ registry: { scanEvery, relistEvery, lister, container } })` for tests); the follow is `Chan.follow` pinned to `agents` (the new `channels` argument) as the bot, reading `registryCard` (`core/tailchan.ts`, with the tolerant `asAgentCard`), re-listing `/v1/agents?state=all` on every (re)connect and every `RELIST_EVERY` (5 min; the broker posts its own `lost` transitions, the re-list only catches its week-old sweeps as `gone`), a lagging reading never beating a newer `seenAt`, a list passing over every card an event told of while it was on its way (the broker's `lost` keeps the last beat, so a tie is no proof; events are numbered, `heard`), and a beat alone never broadcast; `GET /api/registry` is `{ready, cards}`, 503 without a broker, 502 when the broker fails the first list. Every backend with a broker scans and follows; the page reads only home's (amendment 8, like tailchan): `api.registry()` at init and on home's resync, `applyEvent` drops a non-home `registry` event, `qEvent` passes it through. UI: `ui/src/agentcards.ts` (pure, tested; the spec's `ui/src/registry.ts` is taken by the backend registry): `urlKey`/`repoKeys`/`cardOnRepo`/`cardsFor`/`cardsByRepoCard` join cards to repo cards by the remote's web url (every remote and the link, lowercase host/path like `remoteKey`), else a card with no repo by cwd under a local checkout on the card's canopy backend; `orderCards` (by `tierOf`, then state: tier 0 a session, which `isSession` calls any card whose origin is not canopy-run plus a canopy chat; tier 1 a canopy run that is not a chat, a job, a flow's step or a coordinator, working or waiting; tier 2 the rest, an idle run, lost and ended; inside a tier waiting, working, idle, lost, ended, the newest start first), `splitRecent` (live, and the day's ended and lost), `groupCards` by machine (`machineOf`: the canopy backend's name, else the node) or repo, the three taking an optional `IsChat` (without one every canopy run reads as headless; `chatOf` in `allagents.ts` says whether a card names one of the chats' qualified run ids, a made `run:` card or a registry card alike, and the hook `useIsChat` in `Registry.tsx` gives the views the chats among the store's runs through it), `stateWord`, `whereWord`, `repoWord`, `ageWord`, `cardName` (a scan card is `<harness> pid n`), `joinTarget` (`qual(backend, term)` on a shown backend), `transcriptTarget` (a home canopy card's transcript as a path relative to its home checkout, for `api.openFile`), `mergeCards`/`replaceCards` (by `seenAt`, and `replaceCards` keeps what an event said of a card while the list was on its way, the store numbering the events it applies). The store keeps `registry` (cards by id), `registryReady`, `loadRegistry` (a failure other than the no-broker 503, which `req` errors carry as `status`, is tried again at doubling waits from 5 s to 5 min, and the first `registry` event while not ready loads the whole list), `showAgents`, and `agentsOn(s, repoId)` off the memoized `agentsByCard`, both over every repo (`everyCard`), not the board's workspace- and archive-scoped cards, so a panel on a repo the board leaves out still shows its agents. `components/Registry.tsx`: `AgentRow` (glyph, handle, repo · branch, state rust while waiting and dim once lost, model, age, where, cap chips, offers; message → `openChan("@handle")`, join → `bringTerm` then the git view for a panel shell, transcript), `RegistryTab` (the agents view's first tab when home has a broker, with a `ChanChip` in its bar since the top bar is the git view's), `AgentChip` (`✦ n` in a repo card's state row, rust while one waits, the list as its tooltip, a click is `showAgents`), `AgentsSection` (panel section `agents`, in `SECTION_KEYS` and `DEFAULT_CLOSED`; its header is `agentsHead`, the running agents by state, waiting first, or the day's stopped ones). The changes section's header is `changesHead` (`ui/src/elsewhere.ts`): files, `+added −deleted`, ahead and behind, each peer's WIP, then the card's own `elsewhereChips`. The feed's `registry` kind (word "agents") comes from `registryLines`: started, waiting, ended, lost, back; a turn is a quiet line. Tests: `agentscan.test.ts` (`/proc` and `ps` fixtures), `server/registry.test.ts` (a stand-in broker: list, follow, a stale event, a sweep, the 503, the scan post, a shell's env), `agentcards.test.ts`, the feed, qualify and store tests.

Activity, run time and the drill-down. `core/activity.ts` reads what a card's session did from its transcript on the backend's own machine, found by harness and session id alone (`findTranscript`: Claude Code's `<claudeHome>/projects/<projectFolder(cwd)>/<session>.jsonl`, else that name under any project folder since a long path gets a shortened folder; Codex's rollout whose name ends in `-<session>.jsonl` over the last `CODEX_DAYS`), so no path from the page reaches the disk and a session reads the same on the host and in a container mounting the same home. The tally is pure and tested (`newTally`, `feedLine`, `activityOf`): typed prompts (not tool results, meta lines, tag-wrapped blocks, the caveat or an interruption note; the same words twice within 5 s are one message, since codex says a prompt as an event and again as a completed item), replies, tool calls by name with a line each (`toolText`: a patch's files, a shell's command with codex's `bash -lc` and its 0.159 `exec` script unwrapped, a file tool's path, a search's pattern), failed results named by their tool, the files an edit tool or an `apply_patch` wrote (`patchFiles`), tokens (Claude's usage counted once per message id, its latest block; codex's running total, cached input apart), models, Claude Code's own `ai-title`/`agent-name`/`summary`, `last-prompt` and `cost-state` records (cost and lines as the harness counted them; canopy keeps no price list), busy time (gaps under `ACTIVE_GAP`, 5 min) and the newest `ACTIVITY_EVENTS` (40). `readActivity` keeps a reading per session (`HELD_MAX` 32, least lately asked dropped) and reads on from where it stopped, in `CHUNK`s (4 MB; a record past one is passed over), only up to the last whole line, anew when the file got shorter, one reading at a time per session; it counts a Claude session's `<session>/subagents/*.jsonl`. `GET /api/agents/activity?harness=&session=&cwd=` is `AgentActivity` (`types.ts`, with `ActivityEvent`), 400 for an unknown harness or a malformed id, 404 when the file is not on that machine. UI: `agentcards.ts` adds `durationWord` (to the minute, "1h 12m"), `runEnd`/`runMs` (its end, its last beat once lost, else now), `ageWord` now "ran 42m, ended 3h ago", `dayStartOf`, `todayMs`, `registrySummary` (live by state, machines, repos, the day's starts, ends and losses, every agent's time since midnight added up, the longest running, harness counts), `timelineOf` (a `Lane` per live card and per card that stopped today, `TIMELINE_LANES` 24 with the live ones kept first, from the earliest start but never before midnight and an hour at least, ticks on the local clock, eight at most), `readerOf` (the backend that reads a card's transcript: its canopy backend when shown, else a shown backend on the same node, learned from another card that ran under one there, or named as the node is; null for a scan card or another harness) and `markTrail`/`stateSince` (`TrailMark`: a mark on each change of state or new wait, one for a card met within 2 min of its start, none for a card met later; `TRAIL_CAP` 24). The store keeps `registryTrail`, marked on every `registry` event and on `loadRegistry` (which drops the trail of a card the list no longer names). `RegistryTab` shows `Summary` (numbers on the ground; a waiting count opens the inbox), `DayTimeline` (a lane per run in its state's colour, lost hatched, a run from before the axis cut flat, hairline ticks, a legend; a lane is a button that drills into its row and brings it into view, opening the past fold when it is there), then the groups; one row is drilled into at a time. `AgentRow`'s name is the toggle (a panel's rows each keep their own), its state carries "for 4m" when this page saw it change, and its run time is `ageWord`. `AgentDetail`: the session's title or first prompt, the facts (started, run time and busy time, last beat, what it waits on, harness, model and mode, where with os, pid and container, repo link and branch, folder, session), the activity off `readerOf`'s backend (`useActivity`: again every 10 s while it runs and the page is in view, a failed reading kept beside the last good one; why not when there is no reader), `ActivityView` (the tallies, the tools as thin bars, the files written with the folder giving way before the name, the last thing asked and said on rails, the latest events newest first in a well, twelve until "show all") and `Trail` (the states seen here). An ended or lost row dims only its head. The `rs-*` state colours are plain classes, shared by a row, the summary, the timeline and the trail. Tests: `activity.test.ts` (both formats, the lookups, reading on, a cut line, a rewrite, two readings at once), `server/agents.test.ts` (the route), `agentcards.test.ts`.

## The agents board

The agents view's "board" tab (`components/Board.tsx`, plans in pure `ui/src/board.ts`, tested) shows every agent at once in one of six layouts: rail and focus, wall, spotlight, dashboard, triage board and lanes. It is the view's first tab and the one it opens on, and it needs no broker: with none, the registry tab is gone.

Every agent, not only the registry's. `ui/src/allagents.ts` (pure, tested) adds a card, shaped like the broker's `AgentCard`, for each canopy run or chat (`run:<run id>`, state from `runState`: a chat between turns and a run's prompt are both "waiting") and each canopy shell with Claude or Codex in it (`shell:<shell id>`, state "idle" since no hook says more) that no registry card names through `where.canopy.run` or a live card's `where.canopy.term`. Task sessions never count. What runs in a shell comes from `/api/terms/agent`, asked by the store's `probeShells` for `shellsToProbe` into `shellAgents`; `useShellProbe` (`AgentFocus.tsx`) runs one timer every `SHELL_PROBE_EVERY` while any board, agent window or agent panel is up, and asks again when the shell list changes. A live process-scan card with the same harness and folder as a shell card, started since the shell, is that shell's agent and is dropped. `useAllAgents` and `useAgentCard` are how the board, the agent window and agent dock panels read cards; `agentName` names a made card by its folder when its repo has no web link.

Plans. `planBoard(layout, groups, opts)` returns a `BoardPlan`: grid columns and rows, a `Slot` per card (`grid-area`, a mode of term, row, chip, lane or hidden, and whether it answers in place) and `Furn` (summary, heads, group labels, the lanes' time axis). Places follow `boardOrder(cards, trail, chat?)`: `tierOf`, then state, then the longest in its state first, and `boardGroups(cards, by, trail, chat?)` keeps that order inside each group, `BoardTab` passing `useIsChat`. Tiles render in a stable order (by id) and a layout or focus change moves them only by `grid-area`, inside a view transition, so a joined shell is never remounted. Rows are `max-content`, not `auto`: a tile clips, so its automatic minimum is 0 and beside a `1fr` track auto rows collapsed. The dashboard stacks its waits at their own height beside one band of work. A phone gets `planNarrow`: the rail is a list and the focus fills the board, Escape or the back button closing it.

What a tile shows (`AgentScreen`): a canopy run's or chat's `RunPane` (the run sheet's timeline, prompt forms and a chat's message box, exported from `RunSheet.tsx`); else in the focus slot the agent's canopy shell, live (`TermView quiet`, so it never takes the keyboard, on a `joinOnly` tab with `attach=1`, so a gone shell is not started again); else a read-only peek; else its facts and transcript (`AgentDetail`). The peek is `GET /api/terms/screen?term=` (`paneScreen`, `tmux capture-pane -p -e`; `{text: null}` on a plain pty, 404 for no such shell) read every `PEEK_EVERY` while on screen and the page is visible, drawn by `ui/src/ansi.ts` (SGR to runs, the 16 colours mapped to the terminal theme's variables). Peeks exist because tmux sizes a window to its latest client: a live xterm in a small tile would shrink the agent's terminal for whoever is using it. `KEEP_SHELLS` recently focused shells stay joined, hidden.

Ideas that work in any layout. `w` takes the focus to the next agent waiting in the board's order (`nextWaiting`: a session's wait before a run's, the longest wait first in each), 1 to 9 an agent by place (`nthCard`, the same order), `[` and `]` step layouts; none fire from a field or a terminal. Follow mode (`agentsFollow`) takes the focus to an agent the moment it starts waiting, held while a key went down in the last `TYPING_HOLD`. Waiting rows answer in place (`QuickAnswer`: allow and deny, or a single question's options, through the inbox item for the ask or the run's prompt, a run's needing no broker answer key; anything bigger opens the inbox), and the focus shows the ask's whole inbox form. Grouping (`agentsGrouping`: none, machine, repo) is a modifier on every layout. The layout is a per-screen setting (`agentsLayout` in `SCREEN_SETTINGS`, `ScreenLayout.agents`, recommended "wall" on every screen, `recommendBoard` reading `recommendLayout` so the board with no setting and the applied profile agree). Any agent can become a dock panel of its own (`/agent/<card id>`, `ui/src/agentpanel.ts`, a reserved panel id) and the focus can pop out into a window of its own through `panes.ts`, as a dock panel pops out: the pane `FOCUS_PANE` (`/agent-focus`, `popOutFocus` in routes, `?view=agent&follow=1&card=`, window kind "agent", `AgentSolo.tsx`) says hello, bye and answers "who" on `PANES_CHANNEL`, the board names its agent with the one message added for it ("show") and either side's "return" hands the focus back (`focusAwayAfter`). While it is out the main window keeps the rail. The dock's listener passes that pane over, since it is no dock panel.

## The sessions view

`?view=sessions` (`components/Sessions.tsx`, the pure half in `ui/src/sessions.ts`, tested) lays every shell and agent out like a terminal multiplexer. The left column holds the machines tree over the agents list, and a "new ·" box between them opens a shell at the first repo whose name matches (`pickRepos`). The strip's shells (`place: "strip"`) run as tabs across the top through `TermTabs`, now exported from `TermDock.tsx`, and the one showing fills the rest at the "full" text size. A status line along the foot shows the keys and the health badge's count, which links to the health view. `machinesOf` lists the home backend and then the shown ones. Each gets its repos with a held shell, a tab not yet reported, a live agent, a star, or the showing tab's repo, sorted by name. A dot shows the most pressing state: waiting, working, live, dirty, quiet. A canopy card matches only its own backend's checkouts. Any other card matches by folder, since a web url would put it on every machine with a checkout. The agents list is `agentRows(cards, chat?)`, the live cards from `useAllAgents` in `orderCards` order, sessions first and then runs at work, an idle run last, with the chat predicate from `useIsChat`, and a waiting row answers in place with `QuickAnswer`. Picking a repo shows its newest tab. With no tab, it brings up a shell the backend already holds there (`heldShellAt`): a strip shell through `joinTerm`, a panel shell joined in the main pane. Only a repo with neither starts a new shell. Picking an agent whose shell is one of the tabs shows that tab. Any other agent shows in the main pane through `AgentScreen` until a tab is picked again. App does not mount `TermDock` while this view is up, because both would mount an xterm on the same strip shells. The keys `[` and `]` step the tabs, `n` opens the new-tab box and `w` jumps to the next waiting agent. `sessionsKey` keeps all of them away from fields and terminals. `w` steps from the agent showing, which is the one picked or the one in the showing tab. The status line also carries `SignInChips`, exported from `TopBar.tsx`, since this view has no top bar. Below `NARROW` the left column becomes a drawer. The tab row shows only the tabs in the left column's pick (`TabScope`, `tabsIn`): a repo row, a machine's name, or "machines" for every tab. A chip at the row's start names the scope, and its × goes back to every tab. When a scope leaves the showing tab out, `showingIn` shows its last tab instead. The row also offers the scope's strip shells that have no tab here (`untabbed`: hidden here, or held by another device) as dim tabs, and picking one joins it. `+` or `n` opens the new-tab box (`NewTab`) over the main pane for the scope's repo, or the "new ·" box when the scope is not a repo. The box takes a name, blank for the repo's, and what the tab runs: a plain shell, or a shell with a harness started in it (`openTerm` with `start: "agent"` and the harness as the launch pick). A harness the machine lacks is greyed out, and the box remembers the last choice. Enter opens the tab, Escape or a press outside cancels, and Ctrl+C clears the name (`newTabKey`). Tabs are renamed in place by a double-click or F2 (`RenameBox` in `TermDock.tsx`, `renameTerm` with `renameTab`), and moved, hidden or closed from the menu a right-click or the `⋯` button opens. `swapTerms` with `swapTabs` swaps a tab with its neighbour in the filtered row. A hand-given name is `TermTab.label` (`cleanLabel` in `src/core/types.ts`, one line with no control characters, at most `LABEL_MAX` characters). The shell carries it too, as `TermInfo.label`. A new tab's socket sends it as `label=` and the server sets it on the session it starts. A rename of a held shell posts it to `POST /api/terms/label`, where an empty name takes it off. On tmux the name is the session option `@canopy_label`, so it outlives the server and `listSessions` reads it back. Every terms broadcast passes through `adoptLabels`, so a rename or a cleared name on one device reaches the others' tabs, and a hidden shell brought back keeps its name. A backend that keeps names says so with `Backend.labels`. On one older than that a shell never has a name, so its tab keeps its own. A kept shell's record carries the name (`KeptShell.label`), so a restore puts it back on the new session and the tab, and a moved shell's tab posts its name to the new backend (`landMove`).


Split panes (FR-026, after Orca's terminal tabs; pure layout in `ui/src/splitpanes.ts`, tested, components in `components/SessionPanes.tsx`). A group is a `PaneTree`: a pane holds a strip tab by id, a split holds two side by side (`row`) or stacked (`col`), the first taking `at` (held to 0.1 to 0.9). The groups are the per-browser setting `sessionSplits` (`splitsOf` repairs a saved list: whole trees of two or more panes, no tab in two), read through `pruneGroups` against the strip's tabs, so a closed tab drops out and a group left with one pane goes; `writeSplits` in `Sessions.tsx` reads the store fresh, prunes, applies a change and saves. The row shows a group once (`rowOf`: its first tab in the row's order, with a pane count on the tab) and `headOf` names the row tab for the active one; `[` and `]` step that row, and a group's tab goes back to the pane last focused in it. A group any of whose panes is in the left column's scope brings its other panes along (`reach`, which `showingIn` reads). Every strip shell is an `.ss-pane` keyed child of `.term-body`, split or not, placed by `boxesOf`'s fractions as inline percentages, since a moved xterm would remount and rejoin its shell; the panes of the active tab's group are all `active` TermViews and only the focused one is not `quiet`, so a split never takes the keyboard from the pane you are in, and `refocus` looks in `.ss-pane.on`. Each pane in view has a `PaneHead`: a grip (in a group), its name, and Orca's three icons, drawn inline: hand-off (the focused pane while `useShellAgent`, exported from `AgentButtons.tsx`, sees Claude Code or Codex; `handOff` takes a `place` now and opens a strip shell here), split right, and ×, which ends the shell as the tab's × does after moving the focus to `neighbourOf`. A split opens a plain strip shell at the same repo; `openTerm` makes it the active tab at once, so the active tab changing is the new shell, which `splitIn` puts on the pane's edge. `Seams` draws `seamsOf` as `role="separator"` handles: a drag redraws a draft tree (`atFrom`, `setAt`) and saves on release, a double-click puts halves back, the arrow keys, Home and End move one. A press on a tab (TermTabs' `onGrab`) or a grip becomes a drag after 6 px: `edgeAt` picks the nearest edge of the pane under the pointer, `.ss-drop` shows the half it would take (`dropBox`), a release lands it there through `splitIn` (moving it out of any other group), Escape or a release off every pane cancels, and the click that ends a drag is swallowed. The tab menu adds split right, split down and "out of the split" (`leaveGroup`). Ctrl+Shift+D and Alt+Shift+D (`splitKey` in `sessions.ts`, read by `code`) are taken on the way down, before xterm, since Ctrl+Shift+D would reach the shell as Ctrl+D. Below `NARROW` the focused pane shows alone. A repo panel's shells (`PanelShells` in `TermDock.tsx`) split the same way, with their groups in their own setting, `panelSplits`, pruned against panel shells only, so neither view's pruning drops the other's groups. The shared parts live in `SessionPanes.tsx`: `useSplits(place)` (the pruned groups and the fresh-read writer), `usePaneDrag` (the grab, the drop and the `.ss-drop` preview), `openedSince` and `termIds` (a split or a hand-off finds the shell it opened as the one not there before, since `openTerm` does not make a panel shell the store's active one), and `TabMenu`, which leaves out the sessions-only items (new tab, move) when it is not handed them. In a panel the focused pane is the panel's own `chosen`, a split's new shell shows because it is the newest, every tab has a right-click menu, and the split keys act only while the focus is inside that panel, since every open panel listens.

Section tabs (FR-030; pure half in `ui/src/sectiontabs.ts`, tested). A card's section can leave the card and sit as a tab in a shell window: `SectionTab` is `{ id, repoId, key, place }`, `place` one of `panel` (the card's `PanelShells`), `strip` (the strip's `TermDock` and the sessions view, which share it) or `window` (`ShellSolo`). The id is `sec:<place>:<key>:<repo id>`, which no shell's name can be, so a `PaneTree` holds a section beside shells; `useSplits(place, also)` counts the section tabs at its place as live, and `also` names a shell window's own shell, which is not in the store, so its groups live in a third setting, `windowSplits`. The tabs are the setting `sectionTabs` (`sectionTabsOf` repairs it; a section is a tab in one place at most, and `addSectionTab` moves it), and `sectionTabs`, `panelSplits`, `sessionSplits` and `windowSplits` are all `SCREEN_SETTINGS`, so each kind of screen keeps its own arrangement. `RepoPanel` leaves out `tabbedKeys`, so a tabbed section is gone from its card until `untabSection` closes its tab. `sectionApplies` holds which sections a repo has nothing for (`PanelSection` reads it first), and `tabbableKeys` is what the menus offer: applies, not tabbed, not hidden in every panel. `SectionTabView` (`components/SectionTab.tsx`) shows the section inside a `SectionWindow` provider and a `.panel` box, so it is always open and its gear has no panel lines. `TermTabs` takes `sections` after its shells (rename and the agent buttons are shell-only), each surface renders a `.sec-pane` keyed child beside its shells with a `SectionPaneHead` (split opens a shell beside it; in a shell window it puts the window's shell beside it instead), and `TabMenu` gives a section split right, split down, out of the split and back to the card, and a shell "a section as a tab". The strip's active shell (`activeTerm`) is checked against shells, so a section showing there is the store's `stripSection`, which `showTerm`, and any change of `activeTerm` (a store subscription), clears; with no shell showing the strip shows its last section. `tabSection` unfolds a folded shell section so the new tab is seen, and `ShellSolo` keeps one body shape with or without section tabs so the first never remounts its shell.

Tabs between shell windows (FR-031; pure half in `ui/src/panemove.ts`, tested). `usePaneDrag` registers each shell window that calls it (the card's `PanelShells`, the strip's `TermDock`, the sessions view, `ShellSolo`) in a module-level `ZONES` set. A drag finds the innermost window under the pointer with `elementFromPoint`, so a window under an overlay or folded away is never hit. Its own window lands a pane as before; another window lands it only when its `receive.accepts` says yes, and the drag state is shared (`useSyncExternalStore`) so the `.ss-drop` preview shows in the window it would land in. `body.ss-grabbing` holds the grabbing cursor across windows. `canMove` holds what a window takes: the strip takes any tab, a card only its own repo's shells, sections and runs (never a popup), a shell window only its repo's sections; a task's, the ranger's, a joined view's and an ended shell never move. `moveTo` carries a move out in order: a shell is moved on its backend first (`placeTerm`, `POST /api/terms/place`, which sets tmux's `@canopy_place` and `live.info.place` and broadcasts), then leaves its old window's groups; a page tab leaves its old groups before it moves, since `movedId` gives it a new id naming its new place, and joins the new window's groups after (`splitIn` beside the drop target). Every terms broadcast passes through `adoptPlaces`, so another device's tab follows the shell. The tab menus offer the same move as "move to the shells strip" or "move to its card's shells" (`moveOut`), landing as a tab of its own, which works when the other window has nothing to drop on. The strip's `TermDock` splits from `sessionSplits` as the sessions view does, with pane heads only on the panes of a split. Its gear's "split tabs" group sets `stripSplits` (a `SCREEN_SETTINGS` key, default on). Off, the strip reads no groups, so it shows one tab at a time, and its zone is `whole`: its own tabs do not split there, and a tab from elsewhere previews the whole pane and lands through `moveTo` with no `beside`, as a tab of its own. The groups are left alone, so the sessions view still splits them, and the split keys are still caught (so they never reach the shell as Ctrl+D) but do nothing. `ShellSolo` is a browser window of its own, which a pointer drag cannot leave, so it takes no tab from elsewhere.

Chats and popups ride the same rows as item tabs: `ItemTab` is `{ id: tab:<place>:<kind>:<ref>, kind, ref, label, place, repoId? }`, `kind` a `run` (an agent's chat or a coordinator, by the store's run key), an `agent` (an `AgentCard` id) or a `popup` (one of `POPUP_NAMES`), kept in the per-screen setting `itemTabs`; `PageTab` is either kind and `isPageTabId` is what the surfaces test for "not a shell". `PageTabView` draws a run as the sheet's `Console` (its ✕ closes the tab through `onClose`), an agent through `AgentScreen`, and a popup through `PopupTabView` (`components/PopupTab.tsx`, each popup's body lifted out of its popover). A popup's or a run sheet's gear offers "a tab in the shells strip" (and for a run "a tab in its card's shells") when its `PopSpec` carries a `tab`; the line calls `tabItem` and closes the popup with an Escape, which every popup and sheet closes on. The board's tile has a "tab" button and the sessions view's picked agent "keep as a tab". The card's shells hold its runs, the strip and the sessions view hold everything, a tab with no repo shows in every sessions scope, and its split opens no shell. The strip's showing page is `stripPage` (it was `stripSection`) and closing either kind is `untab`.
## Notifications

Notifications: the per-browser `notify` setting (off by default; `NotifyRow` in the device tab asks for permission and says when the page is plain http or the browser has none) turns on system notifications and a waiting count in the title. `ui/src/notify.ts` is pure: `snapshot` keys everything the waiting panel lists plus each run's status, `transitions` returns new waiting items and runs that went working to done (a failed run comes once, through its failed row), and `watch` is the burst guard, which re-seeds without firing on the first state and whenever the connection signature changes (page load, a backend connecting, a stream that dropped and resynced), then stays quiet for `QUIET` (10 s) while the wholesale reads land. A thin `useStore.subscribe` in `store.ts` fires a `Notification` only outside `dockless()` windows, with the setting on, permission granted and the tab hidden, `tag` the item's key, and a click focuses the window and opens the repo's panel. While it is on, every window prefixes its title with `waitingCount` through `setTitleCount`; `setTitleBase` (`ui/src/title.ts`) is how the solo, shell and agent windows name themselves, so the two compose through `titled`.

## Asks and the inbox

Asks and the inbox (agents spec, phase 4 and amendment 4; the broker's side and its API as above): an agent's hook posts a permission, a question or a guard hit as an `Ask` (`types.ts` field for field with the broker: `AskKind`, `AskState`, `AskAnswer`, `Presence`, `AsksInfo`, `GuardsInfo`, and the `asks` event `{asks, gone?, presence?}`), which waits for the human before it falls back to the agent's terminal. `core/tailchan.ts` reads them (`ASKS_CHANNEL`, `asAsk` tolerant like `asAgentCard`, `askOf` the `{"type":"ask"}` event, read like `registryCard` only off the broker's own handle and node, `BROKER_HANDLE` `tailchan`, though the broker refuses a client's post to `#asks` and `#agents` already, `asPresence`). canopy holds no answer token (amendment 5): each browser keeps its own answer key, one device's secret out of the broker's `ANSWER_TOKENS` `name:secret` pairs, and sends it as `X-Canopy-Answer-Key` to its home backend on the four writes alone, which `AskHub` forwards as the broker's Bearer token without keeping or logging it; a server-held token answered for every process on the machine (the loopback API with no Origin, which every shell's `CANOPY_API` reaches, and on the mini `/proc/<canopy>/environ` through the shared pid namespace). `CANOPY_TAILCHAN_ANSWER_TOKEN` is no longer read; `startServer` still deletes it and `termEnv` drops `PRIVATE_ENV`, for a deploy that still sets it, and `exec` always passes the live env. `Chan` gained `listAsks`/`getAsk`/`answerAsk`/`presence`/`setPresence`/`beatPresence`/`guards`/`setGuards` (the key as Bearer on the writes). `server/asks.ts`'s `AskHub` (`state.asks`; every backend with a broker follows, the page reads home's, like the registry): `Chan.follow` pinned to `asks` as the bot, the open list re-read on every (re)connect and every 5 min (an ask held open that the list no longer names is read alone to learn how it ended, dropped only on the broker's 404 for it, kept as it was when the read fails any other way), an open reading never undoing a close, closed asks kept `CLOSED_KEEP` (10 min) then sent as `gone`; `GET /api/asks` (`AsksInfo`, presence read fresh since it decays at the broker without an event), `GET /api/asks/one?id=`, `POST /api/asks/answer {id, behavior, message?, answers?, always?, client?}` (`by` is `answerer(deviceNameOf(client))`, else `canopy`; the broker writes `<by>@<token name>`), `GET`/`POST /api/presence {away}` (away pins, false is here now), `POST /api/presence/beat` (forwarded every time: the page throttles, and a key's test is one), `GET`/`PUT /api/guards` (checked by `normalizeGuards`); 503 without a broker, 403 on a write without a key (`answerKeyOf`: also when `Sec-Fetch-Site` is present and not `same-origin`, a cheap check curl can forge, the key being the gate) and for a key the broker refuses (its 401 too, so the page never reads it as a sign-in), the broker's 409 passed through; `canAnswer`/`canEdit` in the reads only say the broker answers; `startServer({ asks: { closedKeep, relistEvery, sweepEvery } })` for tests (`server/asks.test.ts`, a stand-in broker). A binary frame on a shell socket that `isKeystroke` (`core/term.ts`: not only a terminal's own replies, DA, CPR and its DEC form DECXCPR, the `ESC[0n` status report, DECRPM, focus, OSC, DCS) stamps `LiveTerm.lastInput` (presence is the page's own beat, which sees the xterm's keys too); `GET /api/terms/watched?term=` is `{watched}`, true within `WATCH_SPAN` (2 min) of one, false (not 404) for an unknown shell or a task, which the hook asks before it routes an ask. UI: `ui/src/inbox.ts` (pure, tested) merges client-side, since runs and flows are already per backend in the store: `mergeInbox(asks, runs, flows, now, ctx)` is every open ask, every run on a prompt (a flow's step run named by its flow) and every gated flow as an `InboxItem` (`key` `source:id`, qualified id, repo, who, where off the registry card, `left`/`until`), oldest first; `askWord`, `endingWord` (allowed by, answered at the terminal, expired to the terminal, session ended), `leftWord`, `detailText`, `recentAsks`, `asksOf`, `mergeAsks`, `replaceAsks` (what `loadAsks` lays a whole list in with: never reopening a close, and keeping what an event said of an ask while the list was on its way, the store numbering the asks events as it does the registry's), `toRunAnswer`/`toAskAnswer` (an `InboxAnswer`), `inboxTitle`. The store keeps `asks`, `asksReady`, `answerKey` (`ui/src/answerKey.ts`, pure and tested: localStorage `canopy.answerKey` apart from the settings, `cleanKey`, `keyHeaders`, `maskKey`, `keyTestOf`; `setAnswerKey`, `testAnswerKey` a presence beat that says ok or refused, Settings' masked "answer key" row; `canAnswer(s)` is `asksReady` and a key), `presence`, `inboxOpen`/`inboxFocus`, `loadAsks` (at init and home's resync), `openInbox(key?)`, `answerInbox(item, answer)` (a run through `answerRun`, a flow through `resumeFlow`, an ask through `api.answerAsk` on home with `clientId()` and the key, the answered ask then applied as an `asks` event so the feed's "allowed by" line shows even when the answer beats the broker's event), `setAway`, `pagePresence` (App's pointerdown/keydown anywhere, once a minute, home only, with a key) and the memoized `inboxItems(s)`; `applyEvent` drops a non-home `asks` event. `components/Inbox.tsx`'s `InboxChip` (`? n`, rust while anything waits, in `TopBar`'s chips and the agents view's bar on both tabs): each item folds open to its form (`components/Prompts.tsx`, shared with `RunSheet`: `PermissionForm` allow / allow always or all for this run / deny with an optional reason, `Questions` with decline for an ask, continue/retry/stop for a gate), a live countdown (the clocks tick whenever the inbox is open, by the second while an ask counts down, else every 15 s, `inboxTick`), join its shell or open the run; then how the last asks ended, the away switch, and "add your answer key in Settings" without a key. `?view=agents&ask=<id>` (`Route.ask`) opens the inbox on that ask once loaded, then `dropAskHere`; an ask that has closed says how (`GET /api/asks/one`). The feed's `ask` kind (word "asks") is `askLines`; a registry row with an open ask gets "? answer" into the inbox.

Plain words and remembered rules. A run's permission is a `PermissionAsk` (`types.ts`): beside `tool`, `title` and `detail` it carries the agent's own `description` (Claude's Bash `description`, from `permissionAsk` in `claudedrive.ts`, which also resolves a leading `~` in a file tool's path against the home folder; Codex's `reason` unless it is the stock escalation line, from `approvalPrompt`), the shell `command`, the `cwd` it runs in, the `paths` a file tool touches, and `noRule` when no remembered rule may answer it: every Codex approval canopy does not positively read as a plain request inside the sandbox (`plainInSandbox` in `codexrun.ts`: only the fields `PLAIN_FIELDS` lists from the 0.159 schema are set, so a `reason`, a network context or amendment, an `approvalId`, a `grantRoot` or any field a later Codex adds makes it not plain, and a command's `kind` is `command`; Codex 0.159's protocol has no escalation field, so this fails closed and the "retry without sandbox" line only picks the friendlier message), an older `execCommandApproval`/`applyPatchApproval`, and every permission of an incubator stage run (`RunCtx.ask` stamps it when `stage` is set, and the Runner gives a stage run no `RememberHook`). A workflow's own allowed tools still answer an escalation they cover, as before. An ask gets the same from its hook's JSON through `askPermission` (`ui/src/inbox.ts`). `core/explain.ts` (pure, browser-safe, tested, no LLM) reads the rest: `splitShell` cuts a command line into simple commands (quotes, `$()` and backticks, heredocs, redirects, comments), `explainCommand` names each one plainly (folded by kind, at most five steps, `cd` followed for the outside check, `cd ~` landing outside any project; every word with a slash is normalised against the cwd, with an option's value after `=` or attached to a short flag (`-DPREFIX=/etc` too), an operand's `key=` value, an assignment's value, a curl `@file` and each part of a colon or comma list included (`reaches`), and a dot glob counts as outside) and sets the flags in `EXPLAIN_FLAGS` order (deletes, pushes or publishes, git commit, outside the project, network, writes files, runs code), `explainPrompt` does a file tool by its files, and `startsOutside` says whether a request runs from or touches a file outside the project. Deletes include `git branch -d`/`tag -d`/`stash drop`, `find -exec rm`, `shred`, `truncate`, `crontab -r`, `gh ... delete`, `aws s3 rm`, `docker rm` and `kubectl delete`; `npm publish` and the deploy commands (`firebase deploy`, `vercel --prod`, `wrangler deploy` and the like) flag push and network; `git -c` (other than user.name and user.email) flags runs code. Beside the flags it marks `opaque` (code canopy cannot read: an interpreter's inline snippet, `sh -c`, a heredoc or a pipe into an interpreter, `eval`, `awk`, a sed script other than plain print, delete and substitute, a wrapper like `sudo`, `ssh` or `xargs`, any `$`, backtick or brace list, an environment assignment (leading, after `env`, or through `export`/`declare`/`typeset`/`readonly`/`local`) of anything but the git identity, `CI`, `NODE_ENV`, `TZ`, `LANG`/`LC_*`, `NO_COLOR` and `FORCE_COLOR`, `alias`, `trap` and `hash`, and every `execOption`) and `guarded` (a step that is not a known reader, by its reading's key in `READ_KEYS` and no other flag than outside, names a place code runs from, so `sort -o .git/hooks/x` counts and `cat package.json` does not, and git's diff, show and log readers with `--output` read as writes (`git output`): `.git`, `.canopy`, `.claude`, `.codex`, `.vscode`, `.husky`, `.githooks`, `.devcontainer`, `.idea`, `package.json`, `.npmrc`, `.yarnrc(.yml)`, `bunfig.toml`, `.envrc`, `.gitmodules`; `guardedPath` judges below the project's own folder and folds case). Program names are looked up by `progName`, the basename in lower case, since APFS runs `Python3` as python3. A reading with nothing specific to say (an unknown program, an unknown git subcommand, a generic package script) is `vague`, and the raw command then stays unfolded. `PermissionForm` (`Prompts.tsx`) shows the agent's line, canopy's reading and the flag chips over the raw command, which folds under them (`RawCommand`). "allow and remember…" opens `RememberPanel` only when `rememberOffer` (`core/offer.ts`, shared by the page and the server) returns rules: a run's permission (an ask's hook keeps its own "allow always"), no `noRule`, nothing `startsOutside`, no Bash command `explainCommand` flags outside, and a file tool only with paths. The rules come from `ruleOffer` (`core/shellwords.ts`, where `shellWords`, `commandWords` and `parseRule` moved out of `codexrun.ts` so the page and the server read commands with the same code). A plain command offers its first words as `Bash(<words>:*)` up to three and never past its first flag, then itself exactly, broadest first; a tool with subcommands (`SUBCOMMANDS`: git, npm, docker, aws and the rest) starts at two words. The offer is the exact command alone when the first word is a `VAR=` assignment, a path, or in `RUNS_ANYTHING` (interpreters and shells, `eval`, `command`, `!`, `sudo`, `env`, `xargs`, `ssh`, `awk`, `sed`, `find`, `bunx`, `npx` and the like), when the second word is a flag (`git -C sub status`), or when the pair is in `RUNS_ANYTHING_SUB` (`docker run`, `kubectl exec`, `npm exec`, `bun x`, `uv run`, `gh api`, ...), or when `execOption` finds a way the program runs another (`rg --pre`; tar's `--to-command`, `--use-compress-program`, `-I` and `--checkpoint-action`; git's `-c` other than an identity, `--upload-pack`/`-u`, `--receive-pack`, `--template`, `grep -O`, `rebase -x` and `git config`; make's `VAR=` overrides, `-f` and `--eval`; `find -exec`/`-ok`/`-fprint`; `fd -x`; npm's `--script-shell`; `go test -exec`; `cargo --config`; and the installs that run package scripts: npm, pnpm, yarn and bun install, add, ci and kin, pip install, uv pip/add/sync, cargo install, go run/generate/install). `runsOther` is those together. An `opaque` simple command is offered exactly or not at all, and nothing is offered for a command that is `guarded` or a chain that is `opaque`. A chain offers a bare `Bash` alone with a warning, since a prefix rule never matches one; a file tool (`FILE_TOOLS` in `shellwords.ts`, the one list the offer, the server and `ruleWords` read, with `EDIT_TOOLS` its editing part) offers its name; `Permissions`, `AskUserQuestion`, `ExitPlanMode`, `NotebookEdit` and `Network` are never offered. The scopes (`scopeOffers`, default first) are the workflow step, the whole workflow and the repo for a flow step's run (`Run.flowStep`, set from the `ActionSpec` by `stepSpec`, with the workflow's `source`), else the repo; step and workflow scopes carry the source and exist only for a bundled or user workflow, since a repo-local one with the same name in another repo is a different file. `POST /api/runs/answer` with `{kind: "allow", remember: {rule, scope}}` goes through `Runner.rememberScope`, which refuses (409) a stage run, a prompt id that is not the one waiting (the console keys its form by prompt id and answers with the id it was shown), a rule not in `rememberOffer` for that prompt, and a rule that does not cover it. It resolves the scope from the run (`scopeOf`: step and workflow apply in every repo with the same source, repo is the run's path, so a reused incubator slug would see an old slug's repo rules if any existed, which stage runs never write; 400 when the run has none), keeps it in `RememberedRules` (`core/remember.ts`, `<config dir>/remembered.json`, written atomically through a temp file and a rename, 0600, and taken into memory only once the write succeeds; a failed forget keeps the rule), broadcasts `remembered`, answers the prompt and `recheck`s every waiting run. `RunCtx.ask` consults the `RememberHook` after `allowAll` and `unattended`, so a retro run and an unattended workflow behave as before. `ruleCovers` refuses a `noRule` prompt and any Bash command `explainCommand` flags outside or `guarded` (it reads the prompt's own command, so codex's `sh -lc` wrapper is gone and a Claude `sh -c` stays opaque); a prefix rule then covers only a simple command it matches (codexrun's `autoAnswer`) that is neither `opaque` nor `runsOther`, an exact rule exactly its command, a bare `Bash` any command that is not `opaque` and whose cwd is inside, an edit tool a change inside that touches no guarded path, and another file tool needs at least one path, every one inside. A matched rule still passes the hook's `inside` (`runsInside` for the command, `pathsInside` for the paths, which real-paths the nearest existing ancestor so a symlink out of the project does not count as inside, and for an edit tool also refuses a real path that is guarded, so `tools -> .git/hooks` does not make `tools/pre-commit` an ordinary file) before the run goes on. A question is never answered, and the step notes `allowed by a remembered rule (<rule>)`. `GET /api/remembered` lists the rules and `POST /api/remembered/forget {id}` drops one; the store keeps them per backend (`remembered`, `loadRemembered` when the inbox opens, the `remembered` event).

Remembered rules for agents canopy did not start (`core/sessionrules.ts`, pure but for the disk checks, tested). A Claude or Codex session in a terminal follows the same repo rules a run does: its tailchan hook asks `POST /api/remembered/check {tool, input, cwd}` of this machine's canopy (`CANOPY_API`, else `TAILCHAN_CANOPY_API` or `http://127.0.0.1:7850`) before it raises a permission ask, and a rule answers the call at once with no ask. `repoHolding` finds the deepest local repo (no `host`, no forge) holding the session's folder, `sessionAsk` reads the call as a run's `PermissionAsk` with that folder as its `cwd`, and `sessionRule` takes a repo-scoped rule that `ruleCovers` and then `runsInside` and `pathsInside` pass on disk, so a link out of the repo stops it; a step's or a workflow's rule never holds for a session. The route is open on the loopback like the rest of the API, since it only says whether a rule covers a call. Only a `keyed` rule (`RememberedRule.keyed`) answers a session: one kept by a write that carried an answer key the broker took. The loopback API that keeps rules is open to every shell, so an agent could start a run, wait for its prompt and remember `Bash` for itself; such a rule answers canopy's runs as before and never a terminal. A run's remember is keyed when the page sends its key with it (`answerRun` does, to the home backend only) and `AskHub.keyHolds` gets a presence beat through with it, and a keyed keep of a rule held unkeyed marks it keyed. For an agent's ask: the inbox's "allow and remember" on an agent's permission ask sends `remember: <rule>` with the allow to `POST /api/asks/answer`, where `AskHub` asks `rememberForAsk` (server/index.ts) first, which needs the agent's registry card to work in a local repo and the rule to be one `rememberOffer` gives and `ruleCovers` holds for (`sessionRemember`); a refusal is a 400 and nothing is answered. The broker hears a plain allow, and the rule is kept for that repo, keyed, only once the broker took it.

"Waiting on you" is a dock panel under the reserved id `/waiting` (`WAITING_PANEL` in pure `ui/src/waiting.ts`; a leading slash never starts a repo id, and the id has no `|` or `:`, so it reads as home's). It lists, top to bottom: whose turn it is (`turnRows`: canopy's chats between turns, status "idle" less a flow's steps, each opening its sheet; and the registry's cards in tailchan's "waiting" state, which Claude's idle prompt sets, less a canopy run's card and any card with an open ask, each jumping into its canopy shell (`joinTarget`, then `bringTerm`) when it runs in one on a backend the page shows, with an **agents** button beside it for the repo's agents section, and otherwise opening that section when `repoOfCard` places it); the inbox's items through the exported `InboxRow`; the repos with a conflict, an error or changes (`attentionRepos`, each row opening its panel, and a repo with commits ahead and no conflict or error getting a **push ↑N** beside it through `api.push`, whose route re-reads and broadcasts the repo so the row goes; a push the tracked upstream rejects for being behind is first rebased onto it and pushed again (`rebaseAndPush` in `core/git.ts`: fetch, `rebase --autostash @{u}`, which drops a commit already upstream and names it in the output; a conflict aborts the rebase and names the files, and a branch with merge commits of its own is left alone), a failed push or pull shows its error under the row, and when git refused over `index.lock` the error offers **clear lock and retry**, which calls `POST /api/repos/unlock` (`clearStaleLock` in `core/git.ts`: the lock git names through `rev-parse --git-path`, removed only when `find -mmin` says it is over `STALE_LOCK_MIN` minutes old and no git process is at work in the repo, since a `git commit -a` waiting on its editor holds an old lock (`/proc` on Linux, `lsof` on macOS, refused when neither can tell), by one `find -mmin -delete` so a fresh lock that replaced it stays, never in a seed) and then repeats the move (a repo panel's pull, push and commit notes do the same through `GitNote` in `Dock.tsx`: the pull and push row retries its last move, the commit box only clears the lock and says to try again); `IdLabel` alone names another backend's); the failed runs not owned by a flow and the tasks that failed or gave up (`failedRows`, newest first; a run opens its sheet, a task its panel with tasks unfolded); then the repos with nothing but unpushed commits (`isUnpushed`); and last the clean checkouts behind their upstream (`behindRepos`: no changes, nothing unpushed, no error, so a pull cannot lose anything), each with a **pull ↓N** through `api.pull`. Those two groups stay folded until opened, since `sectionsFor` defaults this panel to `WAITING_FOLDED` where a repo's defaults to `DEFAULT_CLOSED`. The memoized selectors are `turnItems`, `attentionItems`, `behindItems` and `failedItems` in the store; `waitingCount`, on the panel's head and the ⚑ button, leaves the unpushed and the behind out. The TopBar's ⚑ button beside the inbox chip, counting `waitingCount` as the chip counts the inbox, and the key `w` open it (never close it). The id is kept through every prune by one exemption in `mineOf`, so the scan, the load, `closedSections` and `popped` all keep it; `popOut` and `bringProject` refuse it; the Dock loop and `CellStrip` branch on it instead of rendering a `RepoPanel`. Its gear (`WaitingGear` in `Dock.tsx`) has zoom (`ZOOM_KINDS` "waiting"), the layout moves and close buttons, and folding its groups (kept under its id in `closedSections`); there is no pop out, new tab or solo window for it.

## Guards, hand-off and repo channels

Guards, hand-off and repo channels (agents spec, phase 5): `core/guards.ts` (browser-safe, pure, tested) is the broker's `RULE_RE` and limits, `parseGuardRule` (every/prefix/glob/exact, a non-Bash rule kept but `inert`), `describeGuard`, `normalizeGuards`, and `guardHit`/`commandParts`, the CLI hook's `guard_jq` in TS (checked case by case against the jq); the agents view's routing tab ends with `Guards` (the home broker's rules, tailnet-wide: edit, add, remove, save/revert, a box that tries a command; read-only without a key). Hand-off: `ui/src/handoff.ts` (pure, tested: `otherHarness`, `handoffPrompt(from, transcript)` ("Continue the work of the <harness> session whose transcript is <path>. Read its last part first.", empty without a path), `handoffFrom(card, shown)`, `handoffRepo`, `cardOfShell`) and `components/Handoff.tsx` (`handOff` opens a new panel shell on the same repo and backend with `{harness: other}` and that first message; `HandoffButton` greyed where the backend lacks the harness): on a registry row for a live canopy shell or run on a shown backend, and in `AgentButtons` while the showing shell runs an agent (its registry card's harness, else `termAgent` polled every 15s). The path is the card's `transcript`, else `GET /api/terms/transcript?term=&harness=` (the pane's own agent on tmux, else `harness`; `newestTranscript` in `core/sessions.ts`: the newest `~/.claude/projects/<folder>/*.jsonl` with something typed, or the newest codex rollout whose `session_meta` cwd is the repo; 404 for none, an ssh shell or a plain pty with no harness named). Repo channels: `repoChannel(webUrl)` in `core/tailchan.ts` is the CLI hook's `repo.<owner>-<name>` slug byte for byte (tested against its own pipeline's output); `MessageAll` in `Registry.tsx` ("message all" → `openChan("#repo.…")`) sits on the registry tab's repo group headings and at the top of a panel's agents section (off `repo.link`, origin first like the CLI).

## Tasks

Tasks (`docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-28-dev-cycle-tasks-design.md`): a repo's named processes (dev, test, build) run and supervised on canopy's tmux. `core/tasks.ts` is browser-safe and pure (tested): `mergeTasks(detected, repoFile, canopy, own)` layers detected tasks (`parseScripts` off package.json, `parseCargo`, `parseMakeTargets`, chosen by `detectTasks` over the `TASK_FILES_SCRIPT` read), `.canopy/tasks.json` (`parseTaskFile`, `normalizeTaskPatch`) and config `tasks`, with the own-repo rule applied there (`keep` and `withPanel` only take effect on a repo `own` says is the user's, so a clone of someone else's repo never starts a process on its own) and `dev` given to the task the highest layer names, `overrideOf` (canopy's layer stores only the fields that differ from the detected and repo-file merge, so the sheet, accept and hide send the whole merged task plus their change; `withChange` and `renameOld` in `ui/src/tasks.ts`), `nextDelay` (1, 2, 4 ... capped at 60 s), `taskStatus` (`stopped` is a stop by hand with its exit on record, grey and never counted bad), `listedTask` (not idle: what `/api/tasks` and the store's `tasks` handler both list), `paneReading` (a "no server" right after live panes is doubted for `noServerGrace`, 30 s, before it is believed), `startMark`, `plainLines`/`logPage` (ANSI stripped, carriage-return rewrites collapsed, each line tagged with the local time of the start marker it sits under), `parseTaskState` (the record keeps `fails` and `gaveUp` too, so a backoff and a gave-up survive a canopy restart), and the sweep's decisions `expiredTaskLogs`, `reapable` and `staleWants`; `TASK_TIMINGS` holds every interval. `core/taskrun.ts` (Bun): `taskTermId` hashes repo path and name into a shell-shaped id, logs sit under `tasks/logs/<id>.log` in the config dir (rotated past the cap to `.log.1` by `rotateIfBig`), `tasks/state.json` keeps what each task wants and how it last exited, and `startTaskSession` makes the session with a placeholder command, appends the start mark, pipes the pane into the log and only then `respawn-pane`s the real command with canopy's `TZ` in its environment for a local repo, as a shell gets it (`sh -c 'cd -- <dir> && exec sh -lc <cmd>'`, so a missing folder fails the task instead of tmux starting it at home), so the log gets the first output and the pane's exit status is the task's; the tmux builders (`taskSessionArgs`, `taskPanesArgs`/`parseTaskPanes`, `taskCommand`) are pure and tested in `tmux.ts`. `server/tasks.ts`'s `TaskHub` (`state.tasks`) owns `GET/POST /api/repos/tasks`, `/def`, `/log` and `GET /api/tasks`, a per-task lock, a 2 s supervisor over one `list-sessions` (not `list-panes -a`, which fails with "no current target" on the shells container's server once its last session is gone, and would read as tmux not answering) that broadcasts the `tasks` event (`{repoId, tasks}`) only for repos that changed, keep running with backoff and `gave-up` after five quick failures, recovery once the first listing answers (after the server listens: `start()` awaits nothing but the record; every wanted `keep` task except a clean exit 0 or a gave-up, started at once with no session, back to its backoff when its death is on record from before), start with panel, and the sweep at startup and hourly; it never kills a running process and never starts a session while tmux is not answering. A stop for a task whose repo left the scan is found by repo id (`stopGhost`); the repo-file write refuses a symlinked `.canopy` or `tasks.json` with 409. A task session sits in `state.terms` with `info.task`, so `/api/term?attach=1` joins it like a shell, while `listTerms`, `tellTerms`, `snapshotShells`, the shells chip and `DELETE /api/terms` (by `state.tasks.knows`, so a task only on record too) pass over it, every tick holds each live task pane again, and a non-attach socket on a task id is refused. `startServer({ tasks })` overrides `TASK_TIMINGS` for tests. UI: `Tasks.tsx` (the panel section, `TaskChip` on a card, `TasksChip` in the top bar, the edit sheet); the section's ⧉, or a task picked in `TasksChip` (which a phone keeps on the top bar's first row), brings the project's bench to the front through `bringTask` with that task in the bench's `Front`; a tasks section that folds or unmounts only gives the task back (`dropBenchTask`), never the bench; in the bench the section shows the repo's tasks as tabs along its side and `TaskOutput` in `Section`'s `below` slot, outside its css zoom, which the bench places as its log pane: the picked task's live terminal (a `TermView` that only joins, keyed by `startedAt` so a restart remounts it) or its log, picked through the pure `frontTask`, pure `ui/src/tasks.ts` (`taskChip`, `taskWhen`, `taskLines` for the feed, `devTask`, `markTime`), `TermTab.task` (a task's terminal as a tab among the panel's shells, revived with a new generation on the next click once it ended, never ended by closing the tab), `taskShellUrl` (`view=shell&term=&task=` for a window of its own), the preview section's pairing with a running dev task, and `qTask` in `qualify.ts`; the store re-reads the running list after a scan so a task whose repo left the scan shows "not in scan", and one subscription posts start with panel for every panel newly in the dock, by any path, and once per page load for restored ones (`panelsStarted`). Tests in `tasks.test.ts` (core, server, ui), `taskrun.test.ts`, `tasks-keep.test.ts`, `tasks-sweep.test.ts` and `tasks-hub.test.ts` (the hub with fake deps: restarts, gave-up, the no-server grace, a gone repo's stop).

## The ranger

The ranger (`docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-10-ranger-design.md`): canopy's always-on agent, one interactive Claude Code session per backend, kept like a keep task. `core/ranger.ts` is browser-safe and pure (tested): `normalizeRanger`/`patchRanger` for config `ranger` (`RangerSettings`: `on`, `profile`, `handle`, `telegram`, `fresh {daily, maxMb}`, `home` (`own` or `root`), defaults off, `04:00`, 25, `own`), `rangerHandle` (`ranger` unless set), `rangerArgv` (claude with the profile's model and effort through `agentArgs` but yolo forced off and the extra box filtered by `rangerExtra`, which drops every flag that skips permissions or that canopy sets itself and keeps a `--permission-mode` other than bypass; then `--add-dir <root>` when it runs in a folder of its own (ahead of `--name`, since claude's flag takes every plain word after it), `--name ranger`, `--append-system-prompt-file <config>/ranger/brief.md`, the telegram plugin through `--settings` and `--channels` when it owns the bot, and `--session-id` for a conversation with no transcript yet or `--resume`, then the opening prompt `rangerHello`, since tailchan's Stop waiter arms only when a turn ends and a session that has had none would leave every DM unread), `rangerBrief` (`{{handle}}`-style slots and `{{#previous}}`/`{{#telegram}}` blocks over `lib/ranger.md`, which `<config>/ranger.md` replaces), `TRUST_RE` (Claude's trust dialog as 2.1.296 words it), `freshDue` (size first, then the daily hour passed since `sessionAt`) and `isQuiet` (an idle card or none, and no pane output or keystroke for `RANGER_QUIET`, 10 min; `cardIdle` counts waiting "your turn" as idle, since that is tailchan's word for Claude's own notice after a minute at the prompt, while a permission is not), and the wakes: `readWake` (a prompt and one of `at`, `when`, `cron`, `run`), `parseWake`, `wakeLine` (`[cron <id>]` for Eric's crons, `[wake <id>]` otherwise, a run's end and status ahead of the prompt), `cronAfter`. Local time is the canopy process's: in the container it comes from `TZ` in `.env` (the image's `/etc/localtime` names `Etc/UTC`, which is all Bun reads; docs/deploy.md). `core/cron.ts` (pure, tested): `parseCron` (five fields, lists, ranges, steps, the `@` macros, and Vixie cron's day rule: with either day field starting with `*` both must match, with both restricted either one does; at most `CRON_MAX` (100) characters, on the way in and read back), `nextFire` (the next local minute, skipping whole months, days and hours, null past eight years; Vixie's clock-change rules: a line at fixed times runs a time the clock skips as soon as it jumps and a repeated time once, one with `*` in its minute or hour field (`onTheClock`) runs on the clock, through the repeated hour; hours and minutes step in real time, since a setter would put a time in the repeated hour back on its first pass, before the time asked from), `parseWhen` (`in 30m`, `14:00`, an ISO time), `lastClock`. `core/tmux.ts` adds `rangerSessionArgs` (a placeholder `sleep` with `@canopy_ranger 1`, `@canopy_path` and `remain-on-exit`, and no `@canopy_repo`, so `parseSessions` and `parseTaskPanes` both pass over it), `RANGER_FORMAT`/`parseRangerPanes` (dead, exit status, created, and `window_activity` as the last output) and an `env` on `respawnArgs`. `server/ranger.ts`'s `RangerHub` (`state.ranger`, deps injected, tested over a stand-in `RangerTmux`): its session id is `rangerTermId(backend)` (sha256 of `ranger\0<backend>`, 32 hex, so `isTermId` passes and a tab or url finds it every start); a 2 s tick lists the ranger's panes through `paneReading`'s no-server grace, holds the session in `state.terms` as a `TermInfo` with `ranger: true`, no repo and the root as path, believes no list read before a launch began (`launchedAt`), and treats every exit as a death unless canopy ended it (`end()`), any exit code, since `/exit` is a clean 0: `nextDelay` backoff, `gave-up` at `giveUp` (5) with a DM to Eric through `ChanHub.onRangerGaveUp` (whatever the notify switch says), a good `uptime` stretch forgetting the deaths, and a gave-up kept on `ranger/state.json` across a canopy restart. A start (`doLaunch`) refuses with a 30 s `recheck` rather than a death when Claude Code is missing, the profile is codex, or a live canopy card on another backend has the handle (`handle-taken`); a restart or fresh start refused while the old session runs is `held`, its state and reason ("it still runs as before") standing over `running` until the same kind goes through at a recheck, and one refused while nothing runs is launched again as the same kind, so it still clears a gave-up and a fresh one still starts anew; a fresh start or restart asked for while another launch is in flight runs after it rather than being folded into it; its folder is `<config>/ranger/home` for `home: own` (made on start; Claude's trust in a folder covers every folder under it, so this keeps the one trust prompt off the scan root, and `CLAUDE_CODE_ADDITIONAL_DIRECTORIES_CLAUDE_MD=1` in its env loads the root's `CLAUDE.md` through `--add-dir`) or the scan root for `root`; it mints a conversation (`randomUUID`) when there is none, on `fresh`, or when its folder moved (a conversation is filed under the folder it ran in, `rec.home`), keeping the old transcript as `previous` when it exists, writes the brief file, and respawns the pane with `<login shell> -lic 'cd -- <its folder> && exec claude …'`, since tailchan is on PATH only through the interactive rc files and `exec` passes over a `claude` wrapper function, with `canopyEnv` less `CANOPY_REPO`, canopy's `TZ` when it has one, and `TAILCHAN_AS=<handle>`. While running, a pane with no live card for its conversation is read for `TRUST_RE` (`trust`, since no hook runs before that dialog), only within `trustWindow` (2 min) of a start or while the dialog shows; the transcript is the one under the folder the conversation started in (`rec.home`, a record from before that field counting as the scan root, so moving it starts a new conversation); the transcript's size is read each tick, and every `freshEvery` (5 min) a due and quiet fresh conversation starts. Wakes live in `ranger/wakes.json` (at most `WAKES_MAX`); every `wakeEvery` (15 s) the due ones go out as a DM from canopy's bot through `ChanHub.dm` (whatever the notify switch says), held while there is no broker or the ranger is not running, a one-shot removed once sent, a cron moved to its next fire (one that came due while it was down fires once), a run wake fired once the run leaves `isRunActive` (the runner's `onChange` calls `onRun`) or with "is gone" when the runner no longer knows it; a send that fails keeps the wake for the next pass.
