Routes wait for `start()` to read the settings, record and wakes off disk (503 when those reads failed), so a call between the bind and the reads never writes the defaults over them: `GET`/`POST /api/ranger` (`RangerInfo`; a POST is a settings patch, applied one at a time so two in flight both land: on and off start and end it, a profile, handle, telegram or home change restarts a running one, 400 for a field that does not read or a profile whose harness is not Claude and 409 for a handle live on another backend, both checked before anything is saved, 503 for on without tmux), `POST /api/ranger/restart|fresh` (409 while off), `GET`/`POST`/`DELETE /api/ranger/wakes` (`by` is `eric` only when the body says so, a run wake 404s for a run this backend does not hold); the `ranger` event carries `RangerInfo` on every change (`tell` compares the JSON), and `tellTerms` calls `refresh` for its viewers. In the server: `/api/term` takes the ranger's id with `attach=1` and no `id`, on a stand-in repo at the root; a plain socket on it closes `TERM_GONE`; `listTerms` never deletes its entry (the list cannot name it; the hub holds and drops it) and leaves it out of every shell list with `tellTerms`, `snapshotShells` and `DELETE /api/terms` (400). `startServer({ ranger: { tmux, timings } })` for tests. CLI: `canopy ranger [status] | on | off | restart | fresh | say | wake | unwake` over `--backend` or `CANOPY_API` (`say` posts through `/api/tailchan/send`, a wake from the CLI is Eric's), and the UI command line answers `ranger` with a pointer to the chip. UI: `ui/src/ranger.ts` (pure, tested: `RANGER_WORD`, `rangerTone`/`worstTone`, `shownRangers` (each backend's that is on, in backend order), `rangerCard` (its live registry card by conversation), `rangerLine`, `sizeWord`, `wakeWhen`, `ericsCrons`), the store's `rangers` by backend (loaded beside the incubator at init, on a backend's connect and on a resync, numbered events like `stages`, dropped with the backend) with `loadRanger`, `setRanger`, `rangerAct`, `addRangerWake`, `removeRangerWake` and `openRanger` (a strip tab `ranger: true` under `qual(backend, term)` with the backend's own empty repo id, joined only through `joinsOnly`, never ended by `closeTerm`, no agent buttons or pop-out), and `components/Ranger.tsx`: `RangerChip` (`✦ ranger` in the top bar, phone's first row too, moss running, lichen on its way, rust when it needs someone; the popover's `RangerBlock` per backend: state and why, the conversation's age and size, restarts, viewers, a contested Telegram warning, open, message (`openChan`), restart, fresh conversation, turn off, and its wakes with remove) and `RangerRow` in Settings for the scoped backend (on, profile, its folder, handle, fresh hour and size, Telegram, and Eric's crons with add and remove). The chip's tone and line read "your turn" as idle through `cardIdle`, and `turnItems` leaves every ranger's card out of "waiting on you" (`rangerSessions`), since it is reached by DM and sits at its prompt by design. A ranger that gave up is a `ranger` row in "waiting on you"'s failed group (`failedRows` takes the rangers; the row opens its session, which shows what it printed last).

Turning the ranger off invalidates queued and in-flight launches. Launches
recheck that state after asynchronous work, and a start already handed to
tmux is cleaned up before the off request completes. Stopping the hub
prevents queued launches but preserves a session already submitted to tmux,
including its conversation metadata, for the next backend to adopt.
Wake delivery checks the live wake list
before each send, so deleting a later wake while an earlier DM is pending
prevents that later wake from being sent.

## The incubator

The incubator (`docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-01-incubator-design.md`, phase 2 plan `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-01-incubator-phase-2-intake-clarify.md`): a new project from an idea, links, files, a voice memo or a repo, as a `Sprout` (`types.ts`) carried one workflow at a time through the existing `Flows`. `core/sprout.ts` (browser-safe, pure, tested) holds slugs, titles, what an upload is taken as (`inputType`, MIME parameters dropped, `.md` by extension), the inputs index and the summaries clarify writes back, `parseQuestions` (at most `QUESTIONS_MAX`), `answersText`, `holdsSlot` (`SPROUT_CONCURRENCY` 2; a sprout waiting on questions holds none, one parked by a live gated flow keeps its slot) and `nextWorkflow`. `core/incubator.ts`'s `Incubator` (deps injected, tested with fakes) answers an intake before anything slow: `prepare` transcribes (`core/transcribe.ts`, an OpenAI-style endpoint; `CANOPY_TRANSCRIBE_URL` is an origin and the code appends `/v1/audio/transcriptions`, `CANOPY_TRANSCRIBE_KEY`, `CANOPY_TRANSCRIBE_MODEL` default `transcribe`), makes the seed at `<root>/_incubator/<slug>` through `core/seed.ts` (built in `<root>/.canopy-making/<slug>.<sprout id>`, a dot folder the scan skips, outside the seeds folder so the clone's url and token never sit where a stage can read them, on the same disk so the move into place is one rename, keyed by the sprout so a dismissed attempt still cloning and a new intake on the same slug never clear each other's, and renamed into place only once whole, so a restart mid-clone leaves no seed a later prepare takes as made; a clone only of a `networkOrigin` url, the remote renamed `upstream` and set to the url without its userinfo, then the cloned `.claude/settings.json`, `.claude/settings.local.json` and `.mcp.json` removed in a commit; `commitSeed` commits the `.canopy/` files alone as `canopy@<self>`, with no hooks and no fsmonitor on seed.ts's own git calls (the scan, fetch and peer pass still use plain `git()`), `--no-verify`, `add -f` and literal pathspecs; a token in a repo url or link is stripped (`urlWithoutSecret`, a string scan, so a password with a raw `/`, `?` or `#` goes too) from everything stored or shown, the raw url kept in memory for the clone alone; `readSeed` and `writeSeed` refuse a symlinked path component and anything over `SEED_READ_MAX`), rescans, then `pump` starts the next stage, always with yolo off and the route's extra flags dropped, whatever the routes say (`stageAgent` in the server's start wrapper, and in the flows' restore hook for any repo under `_incubator/`); `onFlow` follows each owned flow's status on a microtask, and clarify's end reads `questions.json`, the summaries and the brief's title. Stages load workflows from the bundled and user sources only, never a seed's own `.canopy/workflows/`. `SproutFiles` (`core/sproutstore.ts`) keeps records under `$CANOPY_CONFIG_DIR/incubator/`, stamps each with the launch root and lists only its own; restore runs only under the flows lock (`flowsLock.owner`, after the flows' own restore), and a server without it lists read-only and answers intake 503. Each change rewrites the vault note `02 - Dev/incubator/<slug>.md` and, for a start or park, appends to the daily note (`core/sproutnote.ts`), through the memory gateway (`core/vault.ts`, `CANOPY_VAULT_TOKEN`, `CANOPY_VAULT_URL` default `https://mem.beric.ca`, a failure logged and tried at the next change). The vault token, `CANOPY_TRANSCRIBE_KEY` and `VERCEL_TOKEN` are read once at start, then deleted from canopy's env and listed in `PRIVATE_ENV` (`SECRET_ENV`, both in `core/envnames.ts` and re-exported by `core/term.ts`), and `VaultNotes` holds its token in private fields. The `clarify` workflow reads its inputs by full path with Read (a transcribed recording's index line points at its transcript, `a voice memo; its words are in [n]`), checks `questions.json` with one retry, and has `listed: false`: out of every repo's workflow list and refused with 400 by the flow and fleet routes. `scout` (Research reads only devhub's `manifest.json` and `references.json` and the workspace's READMEs, through `withWorkspaceRead`, and writes `.canopy/pick.json`, which its check, `@pick-check`, validates in canopy's own process (`core/builtincheck.ts`: a check line starting with `@` names a built-in that reads the seed only through `readSeed`, so no bun, `bunfig.toml` preload or `.env` an agent wrote is ever loaded; clarify's `@questions` works the same; any other check line is shell, run by `runCheck` in `core/check.ts`, which sets `CANOPY_CLI` to `bin/canopy-check` for a check run here (an unisolated backend or a repo that is not a seed), while a seed's shell check through the stage runner gets only the runner's env); Eval is the judge gate over it; the Incubator reads it through `parsePick`, `pickRefusal` and `phaseRefusal`, which in phase 3 parked anything but a `new` pick on `vercel` (phase 4 below widens it); a judge rejection at Eval is `rejected`) and `build-new` (Scaffold, Test, Accept, the status following the step through `statusFor`) are unlisted too, and after Accept the Incubator's `ship` (canopy's code, never an agent's step: `core/deploy.ts` pure, `core/shipper.ts` over `gh`, git with hooks off, the pinned `vercel` CLI with `VERCEL_TOKEN` in its env alone and the Vercel API, error text built from vercel output having the token replaced by `***`) makes the private repo, the Vercel project (with `framework` `vite` or `nextjs` when `package.json` makes it clear, through the pure `frameworkOf`), the push (from a bare clone of a bundle of the seed in a scratch folder, pushing the bundle's HEAD commit, so the seed's own `.git/config` never steers it) and the production deploy (which first sets the project's Vercel Authentication to previews only, from a fresh clone of the seed's bundle checked out at its HEAD commit, detached, with `.vercel/` removed, `vercelConfigRefusal` refusing a `vercel.json` with `alias` or a key off `VERCEL_JSON_KEYS` and any other Vercel config, `withCanopyIgnored` writing `.canopy/` last into `.vercelignore`, and `VERCEL_ORG_ID`/`VERCEL_PROJECT_ID` read from `GET /v9/projects/<name>` pinned in the vercel children's env), each put on record as it exists so a resume makes nothing twice, parks when an alias is off `vercel.app` (`strangeAliases`) or the API will not describe the project or the deployment, and goes `live` only on a `vercel.app` url a smoke GET answers and whose `/.canopy/intent.md` is not the file itself (`servesFile`), the GETs following same-host https redirects by hand (at most 5) so an agent-built app cannot point canopy's request elsewhere; a run or check on a seed (`isSeedPath`, through `RunnerOptions.stage` and `DriveCtx.stage` into `spawnEnv` for both drivers, and the server's check hook) starts from `stageEnv` in `core/envnames.ts`, without `GH_TOKEN`, `GITHUB_TOKEN`, `GH_ENTERPRISE_TOKEN`, the `GIT_CONFIG_*` pairs, `SSH_AUTH_SOCK`, `CANOPY_API`, `TAILCHAN_*` or `PRIVATE_ENV`, which is the whole guard only on a backend that runs stages unisolated: there an agent that runs code (build-new's `bun run`) shares canopy's uid and pid namespace and can still read `/proc/<canopy>/environ`, which holds the starting env, while under the stage runner it runs in another container with no token (amendment 3); a park with no gated flow behind it is a sprout `park` item in the inbox. Token-free stages (`docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-02-incubator-token-free-stages-design.md`, amendment 3): canopy's git in a seed goes through `core/seedgit.ts` (`guardSeed` in `git()`, which `seed.ts` and the bundle use: an allowlist of config keys read with `--no-includes`, `core.hooksPath` among them since the flags lay `/dev/null` over it (ruling 27), no gitfile, `commondir` or `config.worktree`, `GIT_CEILING_DIRECTORIES` and `SEED_GIT_FLAGS` on every call, no git while the seed is busy; on an unisolated backend every seed is busy while any stage is alive: a seed check, an active seed run, or `Runner.liveAny`, held through `holdQuiet` until the stage runner says no, and canopy's own seed commit and the ship's bundle wait for quiet through `inQuietSeed`; on an isolated one a seed is busy only while its own check or stage is, `seedBusyFor`), a stage reads only the user's settings (`--setting-sources user`, and `core/codextrust.ts` drops codex's trust in every seed), `core/builtincheck.ts` runs `@pick-check` and `@questions` in canopy over `readSeed`, `core/stagewire.ts` (pure: frames, `requestRefusal`, `childEnv`), `src/stage/runner.ts` (the daemon, `src/stage/main.ts` its entry) and `core/stageclient.ts` carry one process per connection (only `claude`, `codex` or `sh`, only in a direct child of the stage root, the env the runner's own base plus `CANOPY_RUN`, `CANOPY_REPO` and `CANOPY_BACKEND`, and `CANOPY_API` and `CANOPY_TOKEN` too on a runner whose `CANOPY_STAGE_PASS_ENV` names them (`passEnvNames`, which refuses any other name at start; the coordinator's runner), the tree, process group and every process with its cwd in the seed killed when the run ends or the connection closes, and every orphan in the runner's own pid namespace killed at each run's end and every 30 s by `sweepOrphans` (sparing pid 1, itself, live connections' trees and `docker exec` trees); `hello` names the harnesses, `busy` says whether anything runs in a seed), reaching the drivers as `DriveCtx.spawn` through `RunnerOptions.stageExec` and the checks as `StageClient.exec`, `core/stageshare.ts` copies a sprout's inputs to `_incubator/.shared/inputs/<id>` and a workspace snapshot to `.shared/workspace/<id>` (read-only in the stages container), and `CANOPY_STAGE_SOCKET` makes every stage go through the runner while `CANOPY_INCUBATOR_UNISOLATED=1` runs them here, neither leaving `isolation()` to hold every sprout queued with the env to set (a runner that is away or unfenced queues sprouts and parks flows with `parkedFor` `stage`, which resume on their own at the next good hello that says fenced, and `GET /api/incubator/stages` plus the `stages` event say `{isolated, mode, waiting, unfenced}`); the `stages` compose service (the Dockerfile's `stages` stage: no gh, vercel or token, its own pid namespace and `stages-net` on the bridge `br-canopy-stg`, public `dns:`) is fenced by `scripts/stages-fence.sh` (raw-table PREROUTING drops of every private, tailnet, link-local, multicast and broadcast range and all IPv6 on that bridge and on the coordinator's `br-canopy-crd`, where two ACCEPTs on its gateway, canopy's port 10.250.14.1:7850 and the egress proxy's 3128, are kept above the drops by `--apply` and a catch-all appended below them drops the rest of that bridge's traffic, `--install` writing a root-owned copy and a boot unit ordered before docker) and checked by `src/stage/fencecheck.ts` (`/app/fence-check.js` in the image; only a timeout counts as blocked); the runner itself probes `CANOPY_FENCE_PROBE` (a URL past the host, the LAN router's page, set in `.env`) through that file's `reach` at start and every 5 min and refuses every spawn until a probe times out (`fenceOf`; unset, answered, or any other failure all refuse), its hello carrying `fenced` (`true`, `false` or `"unchecked"`, which an older runner's silence reads as) and a reason, and a fence refusal carrying `fenced` too, so `StageClient.fenceRefusal`/`exec`'s `unfenced` park the step (`Run.away` with `awayWhy`) rather than fail it; the server's `isolation()` and `stageFor()` treat an answering runner that is not fenced as away, the stages event's `unfenced` says why ("stages unfenced"), and stage parks resume only on a hello that says fenced. The runner also refuses a program whose file, realpath or any folder above either its own uid can write, and before every spawn drops the seeds' codex trust and refuses while the stage claude settings or codex config hold a key off its allowlist (`src/stage/settingscheck.ts`); a stage's shell check is `sh -c`, never a login shell. The stages image keeps claude and codex root-owned under `/opt/stage-tools`, a PATH of root-owned folders only and `DISABLE_AUTOUPDATER=1`, and its build fails on a PATH folder, stage tool or runner file the `bun` user can write (pinned in `server/compose.test.ts`). `server/incubator.ts` is the routes (`/api/incubator`, `/api/incubator/stages`, multipart intake, 415 and 413 by type and size), and `detach()` runs before the flows stop. UI: `ui/src/sprouts.ts` (pure: the stage strip, words, order, feed lines, `sproutBackend`, where a new project goes by default, and `sproutActivity`, which sets a card's motion: sap along the top while a stage or the retro runs, a breathing rust edge while it waits on the user, a ripple on each change, all under `prefers-reduced-motion: no-preference` with a still sky edge otherwise; the page merges each backend's list read through `replaceSprouts` into that backend's slice only, with numbered incubator events, like asks), the inbox's `sprout` source with kind `clarify` and "go on assumptions" as `{skip: true}`, `components/Incubator.tsx` (the `incubator` view, the + project sheet with a recorder on a secure page, the project's sheet), the bare `n` key; CLI `canopy new` and `canopy incubator list|show`. The incubator is every shown backend's: `qSprout` qualifies a sprout's id, seed repo id, flow ids, retro flow and an extend's target, each sprout call goes to the backend its id names, the store keeps `stages` by backend name and reads each backend's list when it connects or resyncs, a card and the sheet name the backend and the bar says each backend's stages when the page shows several, and + project offers a `runs on` choice that defaults to home while its stages can start (mode not `off`), else the first online backend whose can. The retro lessons on offer (`advice`) stay home's alone. `CANOPY_INCUBATOR_AUTOSTART=0` keeps every sprout queued. Hardening (amendment 4, plan `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-03-incubator-hardening.md`): on an isolated backend canopy runs no git in a seed. `setSeedGit` (`core/exec.ts`) hands every `git()` call on a seed path, after the busy check and the guard, to `seedGitThrough` (`core/stageclient.ts`), which sends the stage runner a `git` request (`{seed, args, env}`, the env names limited to `SEED_GIT_ENV` and refused otherwise, in canopy and again in the runner); the runner runs it in the seed's top folder only, behind the fence, with `SEED_GIT_FLAGS` and `gitEnv` (no global or system config, `HOME` at `/nonexistent`, the ceiling at the stage root), tracks it as a run its sweeps spare and ends it by its own tree and group. A runner that is away or unfenced answers `SEED_AWAY`, which `seedHeld` treats like `SEED_BUSY`: the card keeps its last status (`refreshHeld`) and a commit waits; nothing falls back to local git. `gitToFile` streams stdout into a file for `bundleSeed` (`core/seedmirror.ts`: `git bundle create - --all HEAD`, its HEAD read with `git bundle list-heads`), which the ship clones. `SeedMirrors` keeps a bare mirror of each seed at `<root>/.canopy-mirrors/<slug>/.git` (a dot folder the scan skips, named `.git` so the Mac's sync to the mini leaves it alone), fetched with prune from a fresh bundle and its HEAD set detached, synced after canopy's commit in a seed (`seedOps`' `committed` hook), after a run's outcome read (`freshStatus`) and at the top of each activity pass, and only when the seed's refs or HEAD moved. The peer gate never opens a seed, on any backend: `gateCommand` answers `_incubator/<slug>` and its `.git` with the mirror (run with `--strict`, refused by `mirrorRefusal` when it is missing or reached through a link) and refuses any other path that is in the seeds dir or leads into it by a link, a suffix, a gitfile or a commondir (`candidateReach`); `serveList` names a seed with no origin and no git, and `serveSeeds`/`serveSeed` refuse one. On an isolated backend (`seedsStayHome`) seeds leave the peer pass, the peer route, the background fetch and `cloneMissing` (`PassOptions.skip`), so seeds flow one way: peers read them from the mirrors. The stages image runs the runner as root: `rootStart` reads `CANOPY_STAGE_UID`, `CANOPY_STAGE_GID` and `CANOPY_STAGE_CALLER_GID` (baked from the build args), every child starts through `setpriv --reuid --regid --clear-groups --no-new-privs` (`RunnerOptions.as`), writability is judged for the stage uid by `writableBy`, `socketModes` sets the socket folder to `root:stagecaller 0750` and the socket to 0660 at every start, canopy's service holds the group through `group_add` (`STAGECALLER_GID`, 7850 by default), and the codex trust sweep and the settings check run in a child dropped to the stage user (`stageChores`, `main.ts --stage-chores`, `RunnerOptions.as.self`); `sweepCodexTrust` follows no link (lstat, `O_NOFOLLOW`, a random exclusive temp name). Every child is spawned in `/` and enters its seed only after the drop, through `sh` (`enterSeed`), which runs nothing unless `pwd -P` is the seed's checked realpath. The stages container is `read_only` with a tmpfs home (the stage uid, 0700, 2 GB) and `/tmp`, and `BUN_RUNTIME_TRANSPILER_CACHE_PATH=0` keeps the root runner's cache out of that home. The stage uid stays the host uid. Behind the runner, every step of build-new, renovate and extend gets a bare `Bash` at launch (`withShell`, `SHELL_STAGES`, amendment 4 ruling 16), and Codex's `autoAnswer` reads a bare `Bash` as every command inside the seed; unisolated, every list stays as written.

Retro (amendment 5 in the spec, plan `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-03-incubator-phase-5-retro.md`): a sprout's look back once it is live, handed off, rejected or stopped, or once a park has waited 24h with no live flow behind it. `core/retro.ts` is the pure part: when a retro comes due (`endRetroDue`, `parkRetroDue`), the record (`retroRecord`, passed through `withoutSecrets`, with each flow's `FlowDigest` that `flowMoved` and `reject` keep on its `SproutFlow`, the last 20 `parks`, and each input as a summary or note, never its raw text), `parseAdvice` and its caps, and the improvements fold, offer and render. The Incubator marks a retro `due` at the transition, so a deploy never starts retros for old sprouts. `pump` starts due retros after the stage slots, one at a time (`RETRO_CONCURRENCY`), and a retro holds no slot; a queued sprout waits while its own retro runs, and the server's timer calls `tick()` every 10 minutes so a park's day is noticed. The retro flow lives in `s.retro.flowId`, never in `s.flows`, so it is never read as the current stage, and `retroMoved` follows it apart from `flowMoved`. `startRetro` shares the record through `StageShare.record` (`stageshare.shareRecord`, to `_incubator/.shared/record/<id>/record.json`) and runs the bundled, unlisted `lib/workflows/retro.md` (one step, `tools: Edit, Write` plus the read set the stage allowlist gives, checked by the `@advice` built-in in `builtincheck.ts`, which needs `.canopy/retro.md` and a valid `.canopy/advice.json`). When it is done canopy commits those two files and folds the advice; a gate, a failure or a stop marks the retro `failed` with a reason, and nothing about a retro ever changes the sprout's status. Retro runs are unattended: `startRetro` sets `Workflow.unattended` (`RETRO_UNATTENDED`, never read from a file), `stepSpec` passes it to each step's run, and `RunCtx.ask` then denies every prompt at once with that message (`RunAnswer` deny's optional `message`, which the claude and codex drivers pass on). A retro flow stage-parked for an absent runner stays gated for `resumeStageParks`; one that waits on a prompt for `RETRO_WAIT_MAX` (15 minutes) fails on the tick. Resuming a parked sprout stops its running park retro. A restart follows a running retro's flow, else marks it due again, and fails it after 3 cut-short tries; dismissing a sprout stops its retro. `core/improvements.ts` (`AdviceFiles`) keeps `improvements.json` and `improvements.md` in the incubator's config dir at 0600, writes serialized through a tmp file and a rename, counts each key by distinct sprouts, and offers a dismissed or accepted key again only after 3 more. Routes: `GET /api/incubator/advice` is the offers, and `POST {key, accept}` answers one (404 when it is not on offer); accepting a user workflow opens its file on this backend's desktop or answers its path, and anything else opens a chat on canopy's own checkout (`canopyRepoOf`: by realpath, else by a remote naming the homepage's GitHub repo; never a remote host) opened idle with yolo off and no extra flags; nothing is sent, and the answer's `draft` (from `adviceMessage`, which fences the edit as data) fills the chat's message box (`chatDrafts` in the store) for the user to read and send. The `advice` event carries the offers. The vault note gains a `## Retro` section (`sproutNote`'s third argument, clipped at 4000), the detail carries `retro.md`, and tailchan posts a silent channel line when a retro leaves lessons (`retroNotice`), never a DM. UI: the strip's retro mark follows `s.retro.state`, the feed says when a retro starts, leaves lessons or fails, the inbox's `advice` source is one item with accept and dismiss per key (a chat opens its run sheet, a file shows its path), and the project sheet has a retro section.

Renovate, extend and Firebase (amendment 6 in the spec, plan `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-03-incubator-phase-4-renovate-extend.md`). The chain is `nextWorkflow`: clarify, scout, then the build workflow `BUILD_WORKFLOW` names for the pick's kind (`build-new`, `renovate`, `extend`), then `ship`, or `hand-off` for an extend. Ship and hand-off are canopy's own steps (`isOwnStep`) and need no runner. `core/seedsource.ts` is what turns a scouted pick into a seed. `extendTarget` resolves a manifest id or a unique basename to a repo on the launch source, refuses a repo with a host or forge, a seed, a dot folder or the root, reads its origin, and asks `gh api repos/o/n` for a repo that is not archived, that the `gh` login can push, and whose owner is the login (`gh api user`) or one of `extendOwners` in canopy's config; the remote it returns is the owner and name GitHub answers with, so a renamed repo resolves to where it is now. `rebuild` runs in canopy's process under `<root>/.canopy-making/<slug>.<id>.rework`. It clones the source, fetches the old seed's history from its bundle into `refs/heads/incubator/notes`, copies the notes over with `readSeed`/`writeSeed`, then swaps the work dir in under `inQuietSeed` (old seed renamed to `.old` first) and syncs the mirror. Just before the swap it hands `RebuildSpec.swapping` the work and the rebuilt HEAD, which the Incubator saves as `s.rework`; a rerun passes it back as `pending`, and `settle` finishes or undoes a half-done swap, then keeps a seed whose bundle HEAD is the recorded one instead of cloning again. An extend seed sits on `new/<slug>` at the remote's default branch, with no origin and `/.canopy/` in `.git/info/exclude`; the rebuild refuses a target that already tracks any of `NOTE_FILES`. `extend.md` opens with `Baseline`, a check-only step that always passes: once per seed, and only while HEAD is still the commit the branch's reflog began at, it installs and runs each gate script on that untouched base, writes the names of the ones that fail to `.git/canopy-baseline` and what they printed to `.canopy/baseline.md`. Build's and Test's checks report a failing gate named in `.git/canopy-baseline` rather than fail on it, so a suite already red on the default branch no longer spends Build's retries on tests the change never touched. The record sits in `.git` because a stage can write `.canopy/`: the notes file is for the agents and the judge (Accept's evidence), and editing it waives nothing. The waiver is per gate, not per test, so Accept is told to name any new failure inside a waived gate. A record of nothing (no `package.json`, a failed install, or a branch already past its start) leaves every gate counting. A renovate seed renames origin to `upstream` with a clean url, runs `dropAgentSettings` and `dropUpstreamNotes` (any `.canopy` the upstream tracks goes in canopy's own commit), and commits the notes; `Incubator.rebuild` first checks GitHub's license against the one scout read and parks on a mismatch or none. `Sprout.work` records what was rebuilt, so a retry never rebuilds twice, and `workRefusal` parks a later pick of another kind or source. Every commit canopy makes in a seed goes through `commitFiles`, which commits nothing on an extend seed (the branch carries the agent's commits only); `built()` reads `seeds.headOf` for `builtHead` there. `handOff` makes the one bundle, parks with the moved-seed reason when the head moved after accept, and resolves the target again with `extendTarget`. Without the user's yes for this head and remote it makes `shipper.branchReview` (commits, a `-z --numstat` stat, and the CI, deploy config, hook config and `package.json` scripts changes from every commit, flagged by `handOffFlag` and `scriptsFlag` in `sprout.ts`), stores it as `s.handOff`, and sets the status `approving`, which holds no slot and waits in the inbox as a `hand-off` item (`handOffText` is its detail) and on the sheet. `Incubator.approveHandOff` (route `POST /api/incubator/handoff {approve, head, reason?}`) takes a yes only for the head the review showed and queues the sprout; a no parks it. With the yes, `handOff` calls `shipper.pushBranch` with the recorded remote, the one just resolved, which must agree, and the approved head, which the bundle's must be. That clones the bundle bare, checks the branch tip is the bundle head, that the remote's base is an ancestor, that no commit in `base..head` touches `NOTE_FILES` (`--full-history`, so a `-s ours` merge cannot hide one), and that `base..head` holds no merge at all, then pushes `head:refs/heads/new/<slug>` alone with `push.followTags=false`, `push.recurseSubmodules=no` and `--no-verify`. The sprout ends `handed-off` with `branch` set to the GitHub tree url; the vault note, tailchan and the retro all say so. Firebase (`core/firebase.ts` pure, the rest in `shipper.ts`): for a `vercel+firebase` pick, `ship()` runs `firebaseMade` before the bundle (project id from `firebaseProjectId`, recorded before the create; the default Firestore database at `FIREBASE_LOCATION`; a web app reused by display name; the SDK config upserted to the Vercel project's env through the API), and `firebaseDeploy` after the push and before the Vercel deploy. The CLI runs from `CANOPY_FIREBASE_PATH` with `--non-interactive --json`, a scratch `HOME` it removes after, and `FIREBASE_TOKEN`, which is masked in any error. The deploy clones the bundle, removes `.firebaserc` and top-level `.env*`, refuses a `firebase.json` with anything but `firestore.rules`/`firestore.indexes` (`firebaseConfigRefusal`, so no hooks), refuses a named file whose link leads out of the clone, and runs `deploy --only firestore`. `phaseRefusal` never refuses an extend; `convex` and the `mini` host park with `CONVEX_PARKED` and `MINI_PARKED`, the same words `deployReady` gives. An answer given inside a run (`Incubator.runAnswered`, called from the server's answer hook) is an input. `logAnswers` keeps it whole (`runAnswerRecord`: each question, the labels offered, the labels picked, told apart from the user's own words by `splitAnswer`) in the store's `run-answers.json` beside the record, never under `inputs/`, which every stage reads a copy of. The seed's `.canopy/answers.md` (`seedAnswersText`), the input file, the inputs index and the vault note get only a summary: each question quoted on one line and the labels picked, never the user's own words. The seed's file is rewritten whole from the store on every answer. The judge never reads it: the server's `evidence` hook puts `judgeAnswersText`, built from the store at the gate, in its place (`Incubator.answersEvidence`, `withStoredAnswers`). It survives a rewind.

## The Library

`src/core/library.ts` + `lib/devhub/`: the bundled stdlib Python workspace manager, served lazily through Canopy at `/library/*`. `Library` owns an authenticated loopback worker; `canopy.py` canonicalizes the launch root, copies durable `_devhub` metadata once into `$CANOPY_CONFIG_DIR/library/<root-hash>`, and serializes CLI/HTTP/background writes. The Library/Ports views embed the existing dashboard editors inside the React shell; `bridge.js` handles same-origin repo navigation and theme updates. `canopy library --root <dir> <command>` shares the same state. Do not import generated snapshots, live PIDs, credentials, or personal deployment/DNS infrastructure into the repository. Regression coverage is in `src/core/library.test.ts` (real Python subprocess and HTTP integration, fixture workspace only).

The Library sync (`docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-24-library-sync-design.md`, FR-016) shares what Eric records in the Library between backends: categories, overrides, links, notes, flags, ignore, three plain settings, references, tags, relations and health, never the host keys. `core/libsync.ts` (pure) flattens the five files into keyed entries (`cat:`, `ovr:`, `link:`, `note:`, `arch:`, `fav:`, `pin:`, `ign:`, `set:`, `ref:`, `tag:`, `rel:`, `health:`), stamps each change with `{at, by}` against `sync.json` (always after the stamp it replaces), and merges a peer's doc key by key, newer stamp first and the greater backend name on a tie. Absence of a key a peer has is unknown, never a deletion; only a tombstone deletes. Two holds: a mass deletion (`massDeletion`) holds the whole merge until `--accept`, and a category tombstone waits while its folder here has projects. `unflatten` writes the entries back in each file's own order, keeping everything not shared. `server/libsync.ts`'s `LibrarySync` owns one queue for every pass: a stamping pass on `fs.watch` of the state dir and before answering or merging, a pull of every other `backends` entry's `tailnet` URL at start and every minute when `librarySync` is `dry` or `on`, and the joins (`--seed` stamps at file times, `--adopt` takes a peer's whole). A backend with no `sync.json` answers `{ready: false}` and merges nothing. Merged files go through the adapter's `__sync-write` (`lib/devhub/canopy.py`): under the engine's lock, compare-and-swap on each file's sha256 (exit 3 when one changed), a backup into `sync-backups/`, atomic renames, `sync.json` last. Then `Library.refresh` rebuilds the manifest if the worker is up. Routes: `GET /api/library/sync` (the doc) and `/api/library/sync/status` are reads in `LIBRARY_READS`; `POST /api/library/sync` is admin. The health row is `canopy:library-sync`; the CLI is `src/cli/libsync.ts` (`canopy library sync`, caught before the rest of `canopy library` goes to the engine). Phase 2 (folder moves) is not built.

## The shared repo spec

`spec/` (manifest, SPEC.md template, DESIGN.md, `blocks/`) and `core/spec.ts`: `loadSpec` reads it (`SPEC_DIR`, cached once per process for the scan), `planSync` is pure (the bytes a sync writes, the files it removes, and the `.canopy/spec.json` record: version, halves, sha256 of each owned text; dropping the visual half removes a DESIGN.md whose hash still matches the old record, and leaves one edited since as the repo's own), `specState` compares a record with the files (drift wins over behind), and `syncRepo`/`repoSpecState` are the local fs half. The scan sets `Repo.spec` for local checkouts only, at scan time like the description, so a file event does not re-read it. UI facets `spec-drift` and `no-spec` in `ui/src/filters.ts`; CLI `canopy spec status|sync|check`; the bundled `spec` workflow syncs in a check-only step through `$CANOPY_CLI`, has the agent fill SPEC.md behind an `ask` gate, then commits, each later step checked with `spec check`. The UI's sync is `POST /api/repos/spec?id=` with `halves` (`["doc"]` for `--doc`, `["doc", "visual"]` for `--visual`, absent to keep the repo's), refused for a repo on another host; it re-reads that repo's spec state and status and broadcasts the repo, since a file event does not re-read the spec (tested in `src/server/spec.test.ts`). The panel's **spec** section (`SpecSection` in `Dock.tsx`, `spec` in `SECTION_KEYS`, folded by default and folded for saved layouts through `knownSections`) shows the state and offers the three syncs; it is absent where `Repo.spec` is.

## Health

Health (`docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-12-health-design.md`): what is broken on a backend and what canopy is fixing. `core/health.ts` is the model, pure and browser-safe (tested): a `HealthCheck` (`types.ts`, with `HealthState` ok, down, healing, gave-up, off or unknown, and `HealMode` auto, button, self or none) moves through `observe(check, ProbeResult, now)`, where `ProbeResult` is a union on `state` (`ok` with a detail, `down` with why, `off`, `healing` for a piece restarting itself, `gave-up` for one that stopped on its own). A failure on an `auto` check is `healing` with `nextHeal` on `healDelay` (1 s doubling, capped at 60 s) and asks for a heal unless one is pending; `healFailed` counts a heal that did not hold and at `HEAL_MAX` (5) gives `gave-up` and `escalate`, once; on any other mode a failed heal is plain `down`, so a button never loops. A pass clears the fails once well for `RESET_AFTER` (5 min), at once after a give-up. History keeps the last `HISTORY_MAX` (20) changes of state, and `lastOk` moves only on a change, so a check that stays well sends nothing. `summary` is the chip's word (`off` left out; `unknown` warns after a minute).

`server/health.ts`'s `HealthHub` (`state.health`) owns the checks: `add` (a def with `probe` and `every` is polled at once and then on the interval, never overlapping, each probe cut at its `timeout`, 10 s by default), `report` for a piece that says when it changes, `heal` for the button (404 unknown, 409 without a `doHeal` or on `none`), `remove`, `stop`. An auto heal runs at `nextHeal`, then the probe again; a fed check with no probe treats its next failing report as the heal's answer (`awaitingReport`). A give-up calls `onGaveUp`, which is `ChanHub.onHealthGaveUp`, a DM whatever the notify switch says. Every change is debounced 250 ms and sent as a `health` event only when the list's JSON differs. Routes: `GET /api/health` (`HealthInfo`), `POST /api/health/heal?id=`.

Canopy's own checks (`addCanopyChecks` in server/index.ts, plus the flows and stages ones where those start): `canopy:tailchan`, `canopy:registry` and `canopy:asks` from each hub's `onStream` (`streamResult`), all `self`, `off` without a broker; `canopy:ranger` from every `ranger` event through `rangerResult`, `self` with a button that restarts it; `canopy:tmux` polled every 30 s with `list-sessions` (no server with held shells is down, with none it is ok, "starts with the first shell"), `none`; `canopy:stages` from the stage watch's down and answer callbacks (`unfencedWhy` makes an answering runner down), `off` with no runner; `canopy:watch:<source>` per local source, polled every 30 s, `auto`, healed by closing and starting the watcher again (`startWatcher` now keeps `rt.watchError` and drops a watcher on its `error` event); `canopy:flows`, polled every minute (`pidAlive` on the holder, so the words say when the keeper is gone), `button`: the heal takes the lock and runs `adoptFlows`, the restore the startup does; `canopy:activity`, `canopy:remote` and `canopy:keep` from the end of each pass through `passReport`, which adds the check on its first report; `canopy:gone`, `self`: a refresh whose local repo errors with no `.git` left at its path (`goneRepo`: the folder moved, was renamed or deleted) reports `healing` and rescans the repo's source (`healGone`), which drops the card and finds the checkout wherever it went, and the end of that rescan reports ok, or down naming any card still there (`reportGone`); a corrupt `.git` or a permission error keeps its checkout and stays the card's error. `Chan.follow` drops a stream silent for `FOLLOW_SILENCE` (60 s; the broker pings every 20 s, and comments count) and redials, which is the streams' heal; `ChanHub.start` is guarded against a second follow.

The page: `ui/src/health.ts` (pure, tested) has `HEALTH_PANEL` (`/health`, one of `isReservedPanel`'s), `healthRows` (each shown backend's checks in `backendOrder`, a backend that is not online one synthetic row), `groupRows` (by backend, then canopy and each repo, the bad first) and the words and tones. The store keeps `health` by backend with `healthEvents` counting events so a late `loadHealth` never undoes a newer one, loads it where it loads the ranger, and `healCheck` posts the button. `qCheck` qualifies a repo check's `scope.repoId` like any id. `components/Health.tsx` has `HealthList` (rows with the state's word coloured, why, since, detail, fails, the heal button while down, healing or given up, and the history on a click of the label; `open` opens every history, `only` keeps one repo's group), `HealthChip` in the top bar's chips (and the phone's first row; a drawn pulse, `ok`, `warn` or `bad`, the popover with "open as panel" and "open page", the latter through `openView` in clirun.ts) and `HealthView`, the `health` entry in `VIEWS`. `HealthPanel` in Dock.tsx is the dock panel, built like the command line's. Waiting's `failedRows` takes the health rows and adds every check that gave up but the ranger's, which has its row already. The CLI is `src/cli/health.ts` (`healthCommand`; its parsing and lines are pure and tested): the listing (`--bad`, `--repo` an id or a path the backend scans, matched through `/api/tree`, `--canopy`, `--json`) exits 1 when a check shown is down or gave up; `show` prints one check with its heal mode, fails, next heal and history; `heal` runs the button; `watch` reads `/api/events` and prints what changed on each `health` event (`changeLines`); `checks`, `add` (one kind flag, checked by `normalizeCheck` before the post) and `rm` (canopy's layer only; a check from the file says to edit it) speak to `/api/repos/health`; `lint` and `init` work on the local files alone, `init` writing the dev task's check when the task files have a `dev`. The listing, `show`, `heal` and `watch` span every backend the home one lists at `/api/backends` (`targetsOf`, tested: home at the address the CLI reached, the others at their tailnet address and then their public one, a sign-in answer (401 or a redirect) moving on to the next address); the listing groups by backend under its name, an unreachable backend is a rust line and exit 1, and `--json` over several is `[{backend, checks | error}]`. `heal` refuses an id two backends hold until `--on NAME` picks one; `watch` follows each stream with the backend's name ahead of every line. `checks`, `add` and `rm` speak to the home backend or `--on`'s. `--backend URL` asks that address alone, and a home with no `/api/backends` is the only one.

Repos' own checks (phase 2). `core/repohealth.ts` (pure, tested) reads a repo's `.canopy/health.json` (`{ "checks": [...] }`; `normalizeCheck` holds each to its kind: `http` with an http(s) `url` and an optional `expect`, `port` 1 to 65535 with an optional `host`, `task` by a task's name, `compose` by `service` and an optional `file`, `command` by `run`; every one may carry `fix`, `heal` (auto, button, none), `while` (a task's name: `probeFor` asks the task hub first and answers `off`, "only while dev runs", unless that task is running or in backoff; not on a `task` check) and `every`, at least 5 s, 30 by default). canopy's layer is `health` in the config, by repo path (`setHealthCheck`, `removeHealthCheck`, `healthFor` in store.ts). `mergeChecks` lays canopy's over the repo's by name and marks what may not run here with `blocked`: a seed runs none of the ones that run in the checkout (`task`, `compose`, `command`, a `fix`), a repo on another host or a forge runs only `http` and `port`, and not those aimed at a loopback address (that would be this machine), and from the repo's own file `compose`, `command` and `fix` run only where `own` holds (the rule tasks use, now `ownRepo` in server/index.ts), and `auto` there is a button. canopy's layer is the user's own words, so ownership does not hold it back.

`server/repohealth.ts`'s `RepoHealth` (`state.repoHealth`) keeps them in the hub as `repo:<repo id>:<name>` with a `repo` scope. `sync` reads every repo's file (a remote one over ssh, at most every 5 min; a forge one never), merges and adds a def only when it changed, so a check keeps its timer and history; a blocked check is added without a probe and reported `off` with why (`ProbeResult`'s `off` takes an optional why now), so it shows without turning the chip red. Probes: `http` (`fetch`, redirects manual, 5 s, ok on `expect` or 200 to 399, detail `200 in 42ms`), `port` (`Bun.connect`, 5 s), `task` (the task hub's status: running ok, backoff healing, gave-up gave-up, idle and stopped off so a heal never starts a task the user stopped, exited (a clean exit) and failed (any other) down), `compose` (`docker compose ps --format json <service>`, running and healthy or no healthcheck), `command` (`sh -lc` in the checkout with the check env, 30 s, the last line of output as why or detail). Heals: a `fix` first, else a task restarts through the task hub and a compose service through `docker compose restart`; `http` and `port` without a fix heal by no hand. `rebuildResult` asks for a sync a second after the tree settles (`syncRepoHealthSoon`), so does the watcher when a repo's `.canopy/health.json` or its `.canopy` folder changes, and the class syncs every minute on its own. Routes: `GET /api/repos/health?id=` (`RepoHealthInfo`: the merged checks and the file's errors), `POST` with a check stores it in canopy's layer, `DELETE ?id=&name=` takes one out.

In the page, `ui/src/health.ts` adds `repoChecks`, `badRepoIds` and `repoWord`, the card's word (`up · 42ms`, `down 3m`, `2 of 3 up`, coloured by tone; nothing when every check is off). `RepoHealthChip` sits on the card beside the task chip and opens the panel on the `health` section (`showHealth`); `HealthSection` is that section (folded by default, in `SECTION_KEYS` after tasks): `HealthList only={repo.id}`, the file's errors, and under "add" canopy's checks with remove and a form whose fields go through `normalizeCheck` before the post (a repo on another host offers `http` and `port` only). The `health` filter facet keeps the repos with a check down; `matchesFilter`, `countFacets` and `pickWhere` take a `FilterContext` with `healthBad`, which the store memoizes on `health` (`healthBad(s)`), and `visibleCards` re-filters on it only while that facet is lit.

## Keep awake

Keep awake: `Awake` (`core/awake.ts`) holds the machine out of idle sleep through the child `awakeCommand` names, `caffeinate -i -w <pid>` on a Mac and `systemd-inhibit --what=idle:sleep … tail --pid=<pid> -f /dev/null` on Linux, both tied to canopy's pid so a crash never leaves a hold behind; it is unavailable in a container (`inContainer`), on another platform, or when the binary is missing. The mode is config `awake` (`off`, `always`, `working`; `setAwake`, a bad value reads as off) through `GET/POST /api/awake` (POST is admin). The server calls `recountAwake` on every runner `onChange`/`onGone` and every registry event, which hands `workingCount` (this backend's working runs plus the working cards `ownCard` takes for this machine's: its own canopy backend, a node its canopy cards ran on, or the backend's name or short hostname, since the registry spans the tailnet and a Claude on the mini must not hold the Mac awake) to `Awake.setWorking`. `Awake.start`/`stop` sit beside the Library's and only run outside tests, and the `awake` event carries `status()` to the Settings tab "keep awake" (`AwakeRow`) and to `canopy awake [off|always|working]`, both worded by `awakeWords`.

## The landing queue

FR-012 (`docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-15-landing-queue-design.md`, plan 1). A run can start on a branch of its own: the run route's `branch` (on by default for `propose` when the repo has a main or master to cut from, off for every other action, refused for a workspace run or a seed) makes `startBranchRun` in `server/index.ts` cut `run/<slug>-<id>` from the default branch's tip (`defaultBranch`: `origin/HEAD`, else `main`, else `master`) into `<configDir>/worktrees/<repo key>/<id>` (`core/worktree.ts`; `repoKey` is the folder name and a hash of the path) and hand the runner a `BranchStart`: the worktree as cwd, `branchPrompt` (the repo's framing around the worktree, then "commit on this branch, do not push"), and a `prepare` that runs the gates' `setup` line there before the agent starts (a failure fails the run). Branch runs do not count for `activeFor`, nor do coordinate runs, which hold no checkout (one per repo, `coordinatorFor`; their end reads no status), nor a chat ended between turns (`ctx.ending`), which takes no more turns while its process runs its async hooks and exits, so a checkout run, a workflow and up to `BRANCH_RUNS_PER_REPO` branch runs go side by side. A branch run settles from its worktree (`LandingHub.branchState`): commits ahead of its target and clean is `ready`, else `uncommitted` or `nothing`. The card's elsewhere list leaves these worktrees and their branches to the landing section.

`core/gates.ts` reads a repo's gates from `.canopy/gates.json` in the main checkout (`{gates: [{name, run}], setup?}`), else the `package.json` scripts typecheck, lint, test and build that exist (`bun run <name>`); a broken file throws and holds the queue rather than falling back. A workflow step whose check is `@gates` (the bundled ship's Gates step) runs the same list: `gatesCheck` reads `.canopy/gates.json` as HEAD holds it, so the step before cannot loosen it, else the working tree's package.json scripts, and `gatesScript` makes them one script that names each gate and stops at the first red one with its exit; it skips `setup`, fails on a broken file, and passes with a line saying so when there are none. The step's agent may run each gate's line without asking: the flow's `checkTools` hook adds a `Bash(<line>)` rule per gate (`gateTools`) before the step's run starts, and only for `@gates`, so every other step starts without waiting. A gate and the setup a branch run starts with go through `runGate` (`core/check.ts`), whose env is `gateEnv` (`core/envnames.ts`): what a stage goes without, every `CANOPY_` variable and `NODE_ENV`, so code an agent wrote on the branch runs with no login of canopy's and never sees the server's config dir, tmux server or sockets. Both, and a local workflow check, run as `sh -c 'sh -lc "$1"; exit $?'` (`ledLine`), so the shell that waits leads the session the timeout's spawn starts: a gate command leading it would take the first pty it opened as its controlling terminal and be hung up when that closed, which is how canopy's own suite died at its shell tests as a gate (exit 129, 2026-10-16). `core/landing.ts` is the pure model: `LandEntry` states (`queued`, `rebasing`, `setup`, `gate`, `landing`, `landed`, `waiting`, and the sent-back `conflict`, `red`, `moved`), `advance` for each outcome, `enqueue` (a ready run only, which a whole stopped run joins as; a sent-back entry rejoins at the end), `holdAll`, `retryWaiting`, `restartWorking`, the words (`landWord`: "queued 2nd", "gate: test") and `fixPrompt`. `server/landing.ts`'s `LandingHub` keeps a `LandingRun` per branch (keyed by `RunBranch.origin`, the branch's own id, which every run on it carries) from the runner's changes, and works one loop per repo: the main checkout must be on the target (else every queued entry waits as "checkout not on main"), then for the first queued entry `rebaseOnto` the main checkout's HEAD (a conflict is aborted and sent back with its files), `setup` again when the rebase brought in a lockfile change, each gate through `runGate` in the worktree (a red one sent back with the last 4000 characters), a recheck that HEAD has not moved (else round again, sent back as `moved` on the third), then `ffMain` (`merge --ff-only`; a checkout whose own changes touch the same files waits as "main checkout busy" and is retried every 30 s), and the worktree and branch removed. Nothing is pushed. "fix it" starts a follow-up run on the same worktree, resuming the branch's last Claude session (`--resume`, `DriveSpec.resume`) with the reason, and a fix that settles ready rejoins at the end; "drop" removes the branch and worktree. Records live in `<configDir>/landing/` (`runs/<id>.json`, `queue/<repo key>.json`), written in order per file and only after `adopt`, which the server calls from `adoptFlows` while it holds the flows lock: a run that was working reads `stopped`, an entry cut off mid-turn starts again, and a run worktree no record names is an orphan offered "remove" or, when clean and ahead, "queue anyway". A `stopped` run lands with the same sort, approval and queue as a ready one when its work is whole (`wholeOf`: the worktree there, nothing in `status -uall` so untracked files count and ignored ones do not, and commits its target lacks). That is asked when it is wanted, never kept from adopt, since the worktree can change after: `land` looks again and refuses a dirty one or one with no commits with the reason, and `lookStopped` looks at every stopped run with no entry after each publish (one look at a time, sending the info again only when an answer changed; `GET /api/landing` waits for it), carried on the info as `LandingRun.whole` and never saved. A whole one with no sort is sorted then; it never joins on its own, even when it sorts `auto`, and "land all" leaves it, since the restart cut the agent off. Routes: `GET /api/landing` (`LandingInfo`, also the `landing` event), `POST /api/landing/land {run}`, `/fix {id}`, `/drop {id}`, `/orphan {dir, action}`. The page (`ui/src/landing.ts`, `components/Landing.tsx`) shows a landing section per repo (`SectionKey` `landing`; a stopped row offers land beside drop when it is whole, else its reason), a chip on the card, a branch line with land and fix it in the run sheet (land too for a whole stopped run), the pre-flight's "on a branch of its own" toggle, and a `sent-back` inbox row per branch with no fix going. `canopy land <run>` and `canopy queue [repo]` (`cli/land.ts`) do the same over the API; the queue lists a whole stopped run as one that can land. A run that opens a PR never lands here: its record keeps `pr` and the PR watch takes it from there (next section).

Review rules (FR-013, plan 2). `core/review.ts` reads `.canopy/review.json` from the main checkout like the gates (`{autoLand, approve}`; `logic` in `autoLand`, an unknown key, or a glob that leaves a bracket open, starts with `/` or climbs out with `..` is a parse error, and a broken file makes every run `approve` with the error as its why). `sortBranch(dir, target, rules, hasGates, fold)` takes `git diff --raw -z --find-renames` from the merge base to the branch's head and sorts each file: a delete, rename, copy, mode change, symlink, submodule, new executable, binary or a file over 512 KB is logic from its raw entry; `.md`, `.markdown`, `.rst` and `.adoc` are docs anywhere; a `.txt` is docs only in a folder named `docs` or under a README, LICENSE, CHANGELOG, NOTICE or AUTHORS name; any other file in a docs folder is docs unless its extension is code, config, or markup or styles a site builds from; a JS or TS file is logic when a changed line holds a directive (`DIRECTIVE`: ts, eslint and oxlint switches, coverage and formatter ignores, JSX pragmas, bundler magic comments, pure annotations, source maps, triple-slash references, `use client`/`use server`, a shebang), logic when either side holds a macro import attribute, and otherwise `format` when the two sides match with all whitespace out and `comments` when every changed line is blank or a comment line, each only when `Bun.Transpiler` gives both sides the same output. The transpiler runs with `macro: false`: Bun runs a macro import while transpiling by default, and the sort reads text a run wrote, inside the server, so the attribute check comes first and the setting backs it up (an escaped attribute the check misses makes the transpiler throw, which is logic). Every other language is logic, as is anything past 300 files. `sensitiveWhy` checks canopy's own list (agent instruction files and folders: `CLAUDE.md`, `AGENTS.md`, `GEMINI.md`, `SKILL.md`, `SPEC.md`, `DESIGN.md`, `.cursor/`, `.agents/`, Copilot's instructions, `.windsurfrules`, `.clinerules`; `guardedWhy` from `explain.ts`; `handOffFlag` from `sprout.ts`; lockfiles) and then the repo's globs, case-folded when the checkout's `core.ignorecase` is true; `fileSensitive` checks both paths of a rename or copy. The verdict is `approve` when a file is sensitive or the rules are broken, `auto` when the rules file exists, the repo has gates and every class is in `autoLand`, else `land`. An approval names `sensitiveId`, `git patch-id --verbatim` over the sensitive files' diff (the whole diff when the rules are broken), which a rebase leaves alone and any edit to those files moves; without patch-id it falls back to SHA-256 over the diff with the hunk numbers out. `LandingHub` sorts a run once it settles ready (`settled`; `auto` joins with `entry.auto`), again on every `land` (`canLand` wants the id the sheet showed for `approve`, and the entry keeps it as `approved`), on `land-all` (which skips and names each `approve` run), after every clean rebase in `turn` with main's rules then (`recheck`: an `auto` entry that no longer sorts `auto`, or one whose sensitive id no longer matches its approval, leaves the queue with a held notice), and when a "fix it" run settles (`rejoin`, through `rejoins`). One sort runs at a time per branch (`sorting`), and `idle` waits for them. Notices (`LandNotice`: landed on its own, held back) are kept 50, newest first, in `landing/notices.json`; the inbox lists them at its foot outside its count, and `POST /api/landing/seen` clears them. `GET /api/landing/diff?id=&path=` serves one file's diff between the sort's base and head, and only for a path the sort listed. The page shows each ready run's verdict, "look and land" opens `ApproveSheet` for an `approve` run, and "land all" shows with two or more ready; `canopy land <run> --approve <id>` and `canopy land --all [repo]` do the same from the shell. Canopy's own `.canopy/review.json` lets docs, comments and format land on their own and holds the secret store, the peer gate's files (`src/cli/index.ts`, `src/core/peersync.ts`, `src/core/peers.ts`), `scripts/`, `lib/`, `spec/` and the docker files for approval.

Issue start: a branch run whose note opens with `#123` or a `github.com/<o>/<r>/issues/<n>` URL starts from that issue. `parseIssueRef` (`core/issue.ts`, browser-safe) matches only a ref at the start, so a chat that mentions an issue keeps its words, and the run sheet's `Plan` shows a "from issue #n" chip from it. `issueNoteFor` in `startRepoRun` runs after every refusal and only on the branch path (propose's default included), calling `resolveIssueNote`: the slug from `githubRemote`, then `fetchIssue` (`gh issue view -R <slug> --json …`), `parseIssueView`, and `issueNote`, which opens "GitHub issue #n: title" so `runBranchName` names the branch after it, with the body, labels and the latest 10 comments fenced as data and the user's own words after. A gh failure answers 400 with gh's last line, a URL naming another repo is refused, and a repo with no GitHub remote keeps its note.

## The PR watch

FR-011 (`docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-16-pr-watch-design.md`, plan `2026-10-16-pr-watch.md`). A branch run lands through the queue or opens a PR, never both. The run route's `pr` (refused off a branch) puts `openPr: true` on the run's branch; the landing hub copies it to its record and, once the run is ready and sorted, calls `openPr` in place of queueing it (a failure posts a held notice). "open PR" on a ready run, or a sent-back one, does the same by hand through `POST /api/prs/open {run, draft?}`. Once a record carries `pr`, `landable` refuses it, `landAll` skips it and `runLanding` (ui) shows `PR #n` with no land. The landing section lists such records apart (`RepoLanding.opened`, in any state, out of ready and the rest) through `openedRow`: `PR #n` and never land, linking to the PR while a watch has it (matched by id and repo), and offering a drop (`POST /api/landing/drop`) by the server's own rule, `watchHolds` in `core/pr.ts`: none while a watch holds the branch (its PR neither swept nor read as closed or merged), since the watch keeps the worktree; one once the watch is swept, read closed or merged, or gone (as when a swept PR stays closed past its 30 days), once no run on it is working. `LandingHub.drop` refuses a record carrying `pr` while `prHeld` (`PrHub.holds`, the same `watchHolds`) says the watch still holds it, and says to close the PR from its run's sheet first.

`server/prs.ts` holds `PrHub`. Each PR is a `PrWatch` in `<configDir>/prs/<branch id>.json`, written only by the holder of the flows lock, the same `adopt`/`detach` handshake as the landing hub, and every change goes through one serial queue. Opening pushes the branch with `pushBranch` (`push --porcelain`, never force) to `pushRemote` (the target's upstream remote, else `origin`, else the only remote), then `gh pr create`; the repo's default for auto-merge is `.canopy/prs.json` read at `HEAD` of the main checkout (`{autoMerge}`), auto-fix starts off. `tick` polls each open PR with `gh pr view --json` (`core/pr.ts` `parsePrView`), every 30 s while a check runs or a fix is going, and for 10 min after any push canopy makes (`FRESH_FOR`, since GitHub may not have queued the workflow on the first read), and every 5 min when quiet, and stops once it is merged or closed. The SSE event `{type: "prs", prs}` carries the whole list and the health check `canopy:prs` reports gh failures.

Auto-fix: a failed check on the head canopy pushed, or a new review comment from someone `gh api .../collaborators/<login>/permission` calls admin, maintain or write, starts a fix run through `startBranchFix` with `fixPromptForCheck` (the failed job's log tail) or `fixPromptForComment`. Each prompt fences the log or comment between `<<<` and `>>>` as data, not instructions, and tells the agent not to push; when the fix run ends canopy pushes its commits itself, but only when the agent finished (`runOk`): a fix run stopped, failed or cut off by a restart pushes nothing and sets `gaveUp` until auto-fix is turned on again. Three tries per check or comment (`MAX_ATTEMPTS`; a comment's failed fix rewinds `seenComment` so it is read again), then `gaveUp`; comments from anyone else land in `untrusted`, shown and never acted on. A permission lookup is kept only once GitHub answers (a 404 is no access); any other gh failure leaves that comment for the next read. A PR closed on GitHub takes `pr` and `openPr` off the landing record, so the run may land and never opens another on its own. Auto-merge: when `readyToMerge` holds (not a draft, no fix going, the head is the one canopy pushed and not one GitHub already queued or one held for approval, at least one check and every rollup check passed since `gh pr view` does not say which are required, no change request, `MERGEABLE`, and a merge state other than `BLOCKED`, `BEHIND` or `DIRTY`), `screen` sorts the head with `sortRun` as a land would: an `approve` verdict (or a head that cannot be sorted) sets `approve` and leaves the merge to Eric. Then `gh pr merge -R <slug> --match-head-commit <head> --<method>` with the method `mergeMethod` picks from the repo's settings, and a read after it: exit 0 can mean GitHub only queued it (a merge queue), which sets `queued` and polls every 30 s. A merged PR, by canopy or anyone, waits while a run on the branch is still going, then has its remote branch deleted, its worktree and local branch removed unless they hold commits past `pushed` or changes (kept as an orphan with an error saying so), the main checkout fetched and its landing record dropped; the watch stays for the list. The sweep's close (`sweepClose(id, comment)`, on the queue, refused while a fix runs) is `gh pr close <n> -R <slug> --comment <c>` and nothing else: the remote branch, the worktree, the local branch and the landing record's `pr` stay, so the branch does not slip back into the landing queue. The watch stays with `swept` set to when, its state marked closed, and is read once a day (`SWEPT_POLL`, also after a restart, and never at the busy pace); a read that finds it closed leaves `pr` on the landing record. A read that finds it open (or merged) drops `swept` and the watch goes back to its normal pace, so auto-fix, auto-merge and the cleanup after a merge work again. A swept watch is out of `prune`'s count. After 30 days (`SWEPT_FOR`) it is dropped by a read that still finds it closed, with the remote branch left and `pr` kept on the landing record; a read gh fails keeps it at the daily pace, but a swept or `closeUnread` watch 30 days old whose last read failed (a repo deleted or out of reach) is dropped before the next read, the same way, so no such watch is kept forever. `sweepClose` also refuses a PR with canopy's own auto-merge on or a run on its branch going. A PR the watch finds closed without `swept` (a close by hand, or the other backend's sweep) is asked who closed it (`whoClosed`): one `gh api graphql` call (`CLOSED_QUERY`) reads the gh login, the PR's `closedAt` and its newest 20 comments, so a long thread never pages. The check assumes the Mac and the mini run gh as the same login, since the comment it looks for is that login's own. `sweptAtOf` counts it as the sweep's close only for a comment by the gh login that starts with `SWEEP_COMMENT_PREFIX` ("Closed by canopy's sweep after") and was posted within 5 minutes of `closedAt` (`SWEEP_CLOSE_SKEW`), so another author's comment, or an old sweep comment before a reopen and a later close by hand, does not count. A `closedAt` missing or unreadable cannot be told either way, and is asked again like any answer that does not read, never freed. Such a close marks the watch `swept` from `closedAt` and keeps `pr`; any other frees the run as before (`markPr(null)`); an answer gh cannot give sets `closeUnread`, keeps `pr`, and is asked again once a day until it can be told. The page shows the PR under the run's branch line (`PrBlock` in `RunSheet.tsx`), a card chip per repo (`repoPrChip`), and an inbox row for a red check with auto-fix off or auto-fix giving up. The CLI is `canopy pr <run> [--draft]` and `canopy prs [repo]` (`src/cli/pr.ts`).

## The sweep

The sweep (FR-018, `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-22-sweep-design.md`) offers what went idle for closing. `core/sweep.ts` is the pure part: `normalizeSweep` and `patchSweep` for config `sweep` (`setSweep` in the store), and `planSweep(inputs, settings, now)`, the rules. A shell whose `command` is neither a shell at its prompt (`isShellCommand` in `core/shellname.ts`, import-free and shared with the keep's agent probe: sh, bash, zsh, fish, dash, ksh, tcsh, csh, nu, login, a leading `-` counting too) nor an agent is still offered, but with `checked` and `auto` false, `handOnly` true and a why that names it ("running vim, no input or output for 30h"): a dev server, an editor, ssh or a REPL can sit quiet for days while in use, so only a tick in the sheet closes it. `checked` is only the sheet's default tick (false for a PR too); what lets a close Eric did not tick take an item is `mayClose`, `closable && !handOnly`, which the schedule, the ask and the page's `askCloses` all use. A PR comes from `inputs.prs`, the `AuthoredPr` list `parseAuthoredPulls` in `core/github.ts` reads from `AUTHORED_QUERY` (`viewer.pullRequests(states: OPEN)`, paged like `PULLS_QUERY`; a node missing a field or with an `updatedAt` that does not read is skipped). Its id is `slug#number`, it is idle from GitHub's `updatedAt`, and it is left out when its repo is archived, it is set to auto-merge, it sits in a merge queue, or `inputs.fixing` holds it (a watched PR with a fix going). Its `repoId` is the first of `inputs.repos` with a GitHub remote on its slug (`githubSlug`; a forge listing is skipped, as `linkPulls` skips it, and a checkout on this machine beats one under an ssh source), else null with the slug as `where`. It is `checked: false`, and a draft is also `handOnly`, so it closes only from a tick in the sheet; a non-draft PR is closable from the ask, and by the schedule when `pr` is in `auto`. `core/github.ts` takes `parseRemote` and `isGitHub` from `core/remote.ts`, not `core/access.ts`, because the page loads `core/sweep.ts` and access pulls in exec. `core/sweepstore.ts` keeps `<config dir>/sweep/chats.json` (the chats it stopped, 14 days) and `log.json` (the newest 50 passes); each entry is read through a parser, and one that does not read (a hand edit, `null`) is left out rather than failing the routes, as is an outcome or item inside a log entry, while a log entry with no `outcomes` list is left out whole. `server/sweep.ts`'s `SweepHub` takes its deps from `startServer`: `facts()` is `sweepFacts` in index.ts, which reads tmux's activity with `listActivity` (keyed by `sessionName(id)`; a throw, no tmux or no tmux server gives `shells: null`, and a shell tmux does not list is left out), each shell's `lastInput`, label, task, ranger and move flags, and its agent from the newest live or lost registry card whose `where.canopy` names the shell on this backend (with no such card, `paneInfo` and `paneAgent` look at the pane, and an agent there counts as waiting, so it is kept; otherwise the pane's foreground command goes on as `command`; a pane tmux will not describe leaves the shell out); runs less the coordinator's and the incubator seeds'; `heldRuns` from the landing records (`id` and `runId`), active flows' steps, a running coordinator's children and each PR watch's fix run; the registry's cards as `outside`; `fixing` from `PrHub.busy()`, the watched PRs canopy works on (a fix going, canopy's own auto-merge on, or a run on the branch going); `repos` from the scan; and no `prs`: the hub adds those. `SweepHub` lists the authored PRs through `authoredPrs()` (`gh api graphql --paginate --slurp` with `AUTHORED_QUERY`, read by `parseAuthoredPulls`) only while the settings' `prs` is on and `holdsFlowsLock()` says this backend holds its flows lock (`PrHub.keeps`, true from `adopt` to `detach`, since the lock itself is taken after the hubs are built). The lock is per config dir, so it keeps two servers on one dir apart but not the Mac and the mini, which each hold their own; the PR list is login-wide, so `prs` (off by default, `normalizeSweep` reads only `true`, `patchSweep` takes only a boolean) is meant to be on for one backend, and the PR watch's comment check below covers a PR the other backend swept. A backend sees only its own PR watches' fixes, auto-merge and runs (`fixing`), so a PR the other backend is fixing is not spared by that rule here; the window is narrow (the PR must also be idle past its span by GitHub's `updatedAt`, which a fix's push moves), and the other backend's watch recovers its landing record through `whoClosed`. A backend that does not sweep PRs plans none, never asks GitHub, and answers a close of a `pr:` key with `failed`. The list is kept for a minute as a promise, so the plan reads that a `sweep` event sets off on every open page share one query, and a list gh cannot give is logged and planned as none for that minute rather than failing the route, with gh's last line as `SweepInfo.prsDown` (only on a backend that sweeps PRs, the one that asks), which the sheet shows where the PR group would be (`prsDownNote`, `prPlace`) and the settings row under its "sweep PRs from this backend" switch, so "no idle PRs" and "gh failed" do not look the same. Such a failure is no news about the PRs, as tmux not answering is about the shells: a fire keeps the previous ask's PR items, the prune after a close keeps the ask's PR keys, and a close of a `pr:` key is `failed`, not `gone`. A close that closed a PR lets the list go, so the next plan asks again. `serverStart` is taken at the top of `startServer`. `close(keys, by)` runs on one serial queue like `PrHub.queue`, and plans again from fresh facts before each key: a key the new plan has and may close is closed (`sweepShell` for a shell, handed `seen`, the last keystroke the plan saw for it, 0 for none: any newer keystroke, even one that landed while the facts were read or the plan made, makes it `in use now`, before the snapshot or after it, and `endTerm(..., { keep: true })` without a plan counts only keystrokes from the call on; a swept-chat record, a last look that the run is still idle (`runStatus`; otherwise the record is taken back and the answer is `in use now`) and then `runner.stop` for a chat, `runner.dismiss` for a run, and for a PR a read of that one PR through `prState` (`PrHub.readState`, one `gh api graphql` query, `STATE_QUERY`, for `state`, `updatedAt`, `isDraft`, `autoMergeRequest` and `mergeQueueEntry`, which `gh pr view` lacks; `prNowOf` reads it): a gh failure is `failed` with gh's last line, a state other than `OPEN` is `gone`, an `updatedAt` newer than the list's, auto-merge on, a merge queue, or a draft the list did not have as one is `in use now`, else `closePr` with `sweepComment(spans.pr)` (the span in whole days, hours under a day, minutes under an hour, each rounded down), which is `PrHub.closeAuthored`: `sweepClose` for a watched PR (`watchFor`, the slug in any case), else `gh pr close <n> -R <slug> --comment <c>` with the branch kept, and a gh failure is `failed` with gh's last line, the PR still offered); the settings are read again before each `pr:` key, on Eric's close as on the schedule's, so `prs` switched off mid-close stops the rest; the authored list is read once per `close`, not per key, since each PR is read again on its own, and not at all for a close that names no PR (its plan then has no news about the PRs, so the keys of PRs it closed before stay remembered); a key it lacks is `in use now` while the thing is still there and `gone` once it is not (`failed` for a shell while tmux does not answer). The hub remembers the keys it closed until the plan stops showing them, since a stopped chat reads as idle until its process exits: such a key is left out of the plan and a second close of it is `gone`. Each call writes one log entry and broadcasts a `sweep` event. The event is `{type: "sweep"}` and nothing more: every event stream hears every event, and a page's `EventSource` sends no token, so its stream is a `none` caller's, while the plan, the swept records and the ask are what the `admin` routes guard. There was no admin-only event to copy (the `auth` event with the pending pairs goes to every stream too), and filtering by stream would have cut the page off, so the page reads `GET /api/sweep` on the event instead. `POST /api/sweep/resume {runId}` starts a chat on the record's session (`StartExtras.resume`, the record's harness) and drops the record, at once even while the stopped chat's process is still exiting: a chat ended between turns takes no more turns, and `Runner.activeFor` no longer counts it as holding the repo, since its async hooks (tailchan's `--wake` waiter) can keep it alive a long while; settings patches run on a queue of their own. Routes: `GET`/`POST /api/sweep`, `POST /api/sweep/resume`, `GET`/`POST /api/sweep/settings`, `POST`/`DELETE /api/sweep/ask`, all `admin`.

The schedule: `startServer` calls `SweepHub.start()` (not under `bun test`), which arms a 60 s timer and then runs `tick(now)` once at once: the timer goes first, so a catch-up that hangs (tmux not answering) never leaves the schedule off, `ticking` keeps the two apart, and a `stop()` during the catch-up stays a stop (the `stopped` flag); the hub keeps its own timer off under `bun test` too, and tests call `tick` directly. A tick does nothing while `settings.on` is false or the cron does not parse, and touches no sweep file then. Otherwise it reads `lastFire` from `<config dir>/sweep/state.json`: with none, or one later than `now` (a clock set back, a hand edit), it writes `now` and stops, so a first switch-on never fires at once and a future `lastFire` never holds the schedule off; when `nextFire(cron, lastFire)` has come it writes `now` as the new `lastFire` before it fires (a crash in the middle never fires twice), so any number of missed slots fire once. After a restart the catch-up at start finds nothing to close: runs and chats are not restored across a restart, and a shell's idle time counts from the server's start. Where catching up matters is the Mac sleeping through a slot: the server keeps running, idle time counts the sleep, and the schedule fires within a minute of waking. Turning the schedule on, or changing its cron while on, writes `lastFire` before the settings, so it counts from then. A fire runs on the close queue and reads the settings again there, so a schedule switched off while the fire waited behind a slow close closes nothing and writes no ask. It reads them again before each key it closes: once the schedule is off it stops, and a key whose kind has left `settings.auto` is skipped. Neither gets an outcome, since nothing looked at it, and a skipped key goes into the ask. After its closes it reads them once more and writes no ask if the schedule went off meanwhile. It plans, closes the items whose kind is in `settings.auto` and that `mayClose` lets go (a shell running a foreground program is `handOnly`, so the schedule never closes it), and writes the rest `mayClose` lets go to `<config dir>/sweep/ask.json` as the pending ask, replacing one not yet answered (an empty rest clears it). A busy shell is in neither: it waits in the sheet. With the default kinds (shell, chat, run) the rest is normally empty, so no inbox item appears. With `facts().shells` null (tmux not answering) the fire keeps the previous ask's shell items rather than dropping them. A failed ask write is logged, as a failed log write is, and the event for the fire's closes still goes out. The schedule's recheck before each close asks the same of the fresh plan, so a shell that started a program since the fire planned is `in use now`. A fire with nothing to close writes a log entry with no outcomes. `SweepInfo.ask` is the current plan's items whose keys the stored ask holds and that `mayClose` lets go, so `why` and `idleSince` are fresh and a shell that has started a program since the fire drops out (its key stays stored, so it comes back if it goes idle at its prompt again); null when none are left. `POST /api/sweep/ask {keys}` closes those keys, each after the usual recheck and only while `mayClose` holds for the fresh plan's item (otherwise `in use now`, so an answer never closes a shell that has started a program since), logged as `eric`, and clears the ask (404 with no ask, 400 for a key it does not hold); `DELETE /api/sweep/ask` dismisses it. A settings patch that turns the schedule off clears the ask. A close through `POST /api/sweep` drops from it the keys the fresh plan no longer has (a shell key stays while tmux does not answer) and clears it once none is left, so a restored shell that goes idle again under its old id never turns up in an ask Eric saw emptied. That runs on the close queue rather than in `info()`, so a GET never races a fire's write. Both files are read entry by entry like the others: a malformed ask item is left out, and a state file whose `lastFire` is not a number reads as no fire.
