The page (`ui/src/sweep.ts` pure and tested, `components/Sweep.tsx`): the store holds the home backend's `SweepInfo` as `sweep` (`loadSweep` at init and on the home stream's reconnect, only when the page holds a home token, since the routes need `admin` and a refused read would mark the backend as wanting a sign-in; another backend's `sweep` event is dropped, since its ids would read as home's; on home's, the store reads `api.sweep()` only when `readsAdmin` in `callerToken.ts` says home's last `/api/auth` answer lets it land (the mode is not `enforce`, or `you` is a kind holding `admin`), so a read-only page is never refused on every event and marked as wanting a sign-in, and a tokenless page on a backend that does not enforce still follows along; an event that sends a read numbers it, and only the newest such read is taken, which also outranks a `loadSweep` in flight, while an event that sends none leaves earlier reads to land), and `openSweep` puts up the `{kind: "sweep"}` sheet through `RunSheet`'s frame. The command line answers `sweep` (any words after it) by opening it, and a broom (`SweepButton`, an inline SVG with the count of closable items) sits on the sessions view's status line and the agents board's bar. The sheet reads the sweep afresh as it opens and says so when it cannot (an older backend, or a page without `admin`). `groupItems` lays the rows out a kind at a time in `SWEEP_KINDS` order, outside agents last with no box; `defaultPicks` ticks the plan's `checked` items that are `closable`, and never a PR (draft or not; the group's note says why), though any PR can be ticked by hand; a kind's head box ticks only the rows the plan still has that `mayClose` lets go (`headKeys`), so a draft or a shell running a program always needs its own tick; each row shows where, `durationWord` of its idle time and why (a draft's why starts "draft"). A PR row's title links to it on GitHub (`prUrl`, built from the id `owner/name#number`, null for an id that does not read as one), in a new tab; a click on the link does not toggle the row's box. "Close N" posts the ticked keys (`closeSweep`, with no read after it: the event comes on another connection, and a read that landed first would hold the close as seen) and, once the close is back, drops them from the picks (`dropPicks`), so a second close never counts them again; since the hub's new plan no longer has what it closed, `sheetRows` keeps the items that close sent, each with its outcome in place, under a `sweepSummary` line ("closed 7 shells, 2 chats, dismissed 11 runs; 1 in use now"). A log entry's outcomes are read through `outcomesOf` (the sheet's last-close line, the feed's line and `endedCards`), which treats a missing list as empty. The "swept" fold lists `swept.shells` (restore through `restoreShell`, with `resume` when an agent ran in it, so it comes back on its own conversation; the sheet then reads the sweep again, since a restore goes through the shells' routes; `restoreShell` throws when the record is gone or its repo has left the scan, and the row that asked shows why) and `swept.chats` (`resumeSwept`, which opens the new chat's run sheet waiting for a message: it reaches its driver on the first one). `RunSheet` skips anything under `.no-autofocus` when it focuses, so Enter never closes or restores. The hub sends the same `lastRun` on every close, resume and settings change, and a restored shell keeps its id, so `freshEntry` passes a log entry on only when it is newer than the one the page holds (or, for a page that never read the sweep, from the last minute): `takeSweep` in the store, which takes an event's read, then adds one `sweep` line to the feed (`sweepLine` in `feed.ts`; `describeEvent` gives the event itself no line), and `endedCards` names the agent cards it ended (`shell:<id>` and `run:<id>`, and registry cards whose `where.canopy` names the shell or run on the home backend), whose `/agent/<card id>` dock panels close and whose agent window (`AgentSolo`, not the board's following focus pane) closes itself through `sweepEnded`. The CLI is `src/cli/sweep.ts`: `canopy sweep` prints the plan with a mark on what `--yes` would close (`planLines`, by the same rule), and `sweepKeys` picks, with no `--kind`, the items the sheet starts ticked, so never a PR; with kinds named, every item of those kinds that `mayClose` lets go, ticked by default or not, so `--kind pr` closes the PRs that are not drafts, and a draft or a shell running a program stays for a tick in the sheet. Its POST carries `unticked: true` (`sweepBody`), which `POST /api/sweep` honours by closing a key only while the fresh plan's item passes `mayClose`, as an answer to the ask does, so a PR turned into a draft between the CLI's read and its close stays open ("in use now"); the sheet's POST leaves it out, since its keys are ticks.

The schedule on the page. The ask is an inbox item (`ui/src/inbox.ts`, source and kind `sweep`, key `sweep:ask`), built from the store's `sweep` (`InboxContext.sweep`, part of `inboxItems`' memo) when `SweepInfo.ask` holds anything: `askTitle` names it a kind at a time with each kind's span from the settings through `spanWord` ("Sweep: 3 PRs idle 14+ days"; whole days from two days up, else hours, minutes under an hour), and its `at` is `lastRun.at`, since the ask records no time: the fire's own entry when nothing closed since (`by: "schedule"`), else a later close's, the nearest the data comes (the oldest item's `idleSince` with no log at all, as for an ask written by hand). `inboxTitle` lists its title alone, which names the sweep already. The row (`SweepAsk` in `Inbox.tsx`) lists each item's where, idle time and why, and "close all" sends `askCloses`, the items `mayClose` lets go whether the sheet would tick them or not (a PR starts unticked), to `POST /api/sweep/ask` through `answerInbox`, and is disabled with a line saying so when none are; the server clears the whole ask then. Since `info()` leaves `handOnly` items out of the ask, the row's "left for the sheet" marking and its `close N` label are only a guard. "pick" closes the inbox and runs `pickSweep(pickKeys(items))`, the same items as close all (a shell running a program starts unticked in the sheet too, for Eric to tick by hand), which opens the sheet as `{kind: "sweep", picks, pick}`: `SweepSheet` seeds its picks from them instead of `defaultPicks`, and `RunSheet` keys it by `pick`, a number each pick takes, so every pick remounts it, hand edits gone, and an empty pick is never taken for the broom's sheet, and its close goes through `POST /api/sweep` like any, since the ask route refuses `handOnly` items; such a close leaves the ask, whose closed items drop out of `info().ask` on the next event. "dismiss" is `DELETE /api/sweep/ask`. An error (a page without `admin`) shows in the row like any inbox answer's. A 404 from close all or dismiss (no ask pending: another device answered it first) counts as done (`askGone`), and the page reads the sweep again; a 400 (a newer fire replaced the ask) stays an error. A sign-in to home reads the sweep as soon as the token is kept (`signIn`), so the item and the Settings tab need no reload. Settings has a "sweep" tab beside the ranger's, shown only while it scopes to the home backend and the page holds its sweep (`SweepRow` in `components/Sweep.tsx`): the schedule's switch, the cron (saved on blur or Enter, kept as typed when the server refuses it), the five spans as a number and an hours-or-days select (`spanParts`, `spanMs`, which is null unless the ms come out finite and above nought; a number is saved only when edited and changed, and a unit change saves nothing: `switchUnit` shows the same span in the new unit, converting a number typed and not yet saved with it; `patchSweep` refuses a span under `SPAN_FLOOR`, 10 minutes, while `normalizeSweep` keeps a shorter one a config was given by hand), and the auto kinds shell, chat, run and pr (outside agents never close, so they are not offered), with "Closes public PRs on GitHub with no one looking." while pr is among them. A switch above the cron, "sweep PRs from this backend", saves `prs` through the same queue; its hint (`PRS_HINT`) says to turn it on for one backend only and that, with pr also among the auto kinds, the schedule closes public PRs with no one looking. Both start off. Each change posts `setSweepSettings` to `POST /api/sweep/settings`, one at a time: the row's inputs are disabled while a save is out, a ref turns away the blur that follows an Enter, and an auto toggle is computed by `nextAuto` over the store's newest settings rather than the render's, so two quick toggles keep both. A refusal's text shows in the row.

## Triggers

An outside event starts a fix with nobody typing (`docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-17-triggers-design.md`). The sources are Sentry and Linear (below). `core/trigger.ts` is pure and browser-safe. It holds the rules, the event record, `parseSentry` (the `issue` resource's `created`/`unresolved`, `event_alert` and `error`; anything else is null), `ignoreReason` (projects, levels, an ignore list matched against the title), `existing` (one event per issue under a rule: only a `failed`, `ignored` or `merged` event lets the same issue start again, so one issue never opens two PRs), `noteFor` (the issue fenced as data with `pr.ts`'s `fence`, led by the issue's id so the branch reads `run/sentry-issue-<id>-…`), and the event's state as its run (`fromRun`), its flow (`fromFlow`), its PR (`fromPr`) and the landing queue's held notices (`fromLanding`) move it.

The rules are `<config dir>/triggers.json`, written by hand and read again before each use: `{rules: [{name, source, repo, workflow, secretEnv, projects?, levels?, ignore?, tokenEnv?, org?, api?}]}`. The file never holds a secret. `secretEnv` names the environment variable with the Sentry integration's client secret, and `tokenEnv` the one with a token that may resolve issues. Both must start with `CANOPY_TRIGGER_` (`TRIGGER_ENV_PREFIX` in `core/envnames.ts`): the server takes every such variable out of its own env at start, next to `SECRET_ENV` (`takeTriggerSecrets`), and the hub reads them from that map. So no run, shell or gate inherits them, and the unattended run reading attacker-shaped error text never holds the token. A new secret needs a restart. A bad line is left out and named on the page, and the rest still fire.

`server/triggers.ts` holds `TriggerHub`. `POST /api/hooks/sentry/<rule>` is filed `open` in the callers table, since Sentry sends no token. The hub reads at most 1 MiB, checks `Sentry-Hook-Signature` (hex HMAC-SHA256 of the raw body under the rule's secret, compared in constant time) before it parses a word, and answers a resource that carries no new issue with a quiet 200, since Sentry retries anything else. The events live in `<config dir>/triggers/events.json` (the newest 200, plus any still queued or running). Only the holder of the flows lock writes them (`adopt`/`detach`, like the landing and PR records), so two servers on one config dir never both write. A server without the lock answers a hook 503, and Sentry retries the same address later. The lock is per machine; what keeps the Mac and the mini from both starting a run for one issue is that Sentry posts to one address. Every change goes through one serial queue.

An event starts work on a branch with `openPr` (`startTriggerRun` in `server/index.ts`), and `triggerPlan` picks how. A workflow of one plain step (a prompt, no check line, gate `continue`) is a branch run, the same run "open PR" makes: `startBranchRun` with an `ActionSpec` from the step. Anything more (several steps, a check line, a verdict or judge gate) is a flow on a branch: `cutBranch` makes the branch and worktree once, and `Flows.start` takes it as `branch`. A step that asks before the next one starts is refused, since no one answers. The workflow comes from the bundled and user sources only, like a fleet's, so a repo cannot rewrite what its own trigger runs. Every run is `unattended` (a flow's workflow is copied with `unattended` set, which `stepSpec` passes to each step): a tool outside the step's list is denied, never asked. It needs Claude Code on the repo's job route, and a flow's steps all take that route whatever profile they name. A repo whose branch slots are full (`BRANCH_RUNS_PER_REPO`, a flow counting as one from its start to its end) leaves the event `queued`.

A flow on a branch (`Flow.branch`) runs each step's run on that branch with `held` set on its `RunBranch`, so the landing queue keeps the record up to date (its session, for a later "fix it") but sorts nothing. The setup line runs before the first step only, and the steps' checks and judge evidence read the worktree. `Flows.activeFor` does not count such a flow, nor does the UI's `flowFor`, so it never blocks the checkout's own runs and flows or holds the repo card, and a fleet never starts one. When the flow ends, `settleBranch` reads the worktree (`branchState`, `LandingHub.stateOf`) into `Flow.branch.state` and the outcome. A done flow then hands the branch over (`onBranchEnd`): the server passes the last step's run, with the unheld branch, to `LandingHub.handOver`, which sorts it, opens the PR and leaves it to the PR watch as a branch run's end would. A stopped or failed flow hands nothing over. `Flows.pickUp` takes a stopped branch flow back up: its stopped step runs again, resuming that step's Claude session with `PICKED_UP_NOTE`, and the steps after it follow. A restart takes a branch flow back like any flow, its step rerun on the same branch with no setup. The hub follows a flow event through `onFlow`: the event carries `flowId`, its latest step run's id and the current step's name, and `fromRun` leaves it alone. A gated flow reads `waiting` with the gate's reason, and a done one reads `working` until its worktree has been read. The queue is tried again when any run ends and every 30 s. From there the landing queue opens the PR when the run settles ready, and the PR watch does auto-fix, auto-merge (per the repo's `.canopy/prs.json`) and cleanup. The hub follows the runner (`onRun`), the flows (`onFlow`), the PR watch's broadcast (`onPrs`) and the landing queue's (`onLanding`, for a PR that did not open). A merge calls Sentry's API to resolve the issue when the rule has a token: `PUT <api>/api/0/organizations/<org>/issues/<id>/` with an org, else the older `/api/0/issues/<id>/`. A test fire never calls it. After a restart, an event whose run is gone reads `stopped`; one following a flow the new server took back stays as it was.

Routes: `GET /api/triggers` returns the rules (with `secretSet`/`tokenSet`, never a value, the hook path and any problem), the events and `keeping`. `POST /api/triggers/fire {rule, title}` runs a made-up issue through a rule and skips its filters. `resume {id}` picks a stopped event's flow up when the server still keeps it (`Flows.pickUp`), else resumes the branch's last session through `startBranchFix`, `retry {id}` starts a failed, fixless or branchless event on a new branch, and `stop {id}` stops its run or takes it off the queue. All three need `operate`, as does `DELETE /api/triggers/events?id=`, which refuses a live event. The SSE event `{type: "triggers", events}` carries the whole list.

The bundled workflow is `lib/workflows/sentry-fix.md` (`listed: false`). Its tools are git read and commit, file reads and edits, `ls`, and `bun run`/`bun test` only: no `bun x`, `bunx` or `bun install`, so error text cannot talk the run into fetching and running a package in one command. It triages first, finds the root cause, makes the smallest fix with a test, runs the gates and commits on the branch, and never pushes. Where the right result is a product decision the code has not made, it makes the case fail with a named error and says which decision is needed, rather than inventing a default. `lib/workflows/sentry-triage-fix.md` is the same work as a flow: a read-only Triage step, then a Fix step checked by `@gates` with two retries back to itself. The page is the `triggers` view (`components/Triggers.tsx`, model in `ui/src/triggers.ts`). Each backend's rules show with their hook address to paste into Sentry, the secret and token state, and a "try it" box. The events sit in sections (Needs you, Working, In review, Fixed, Nothing to fix, Ignored). Each row has its state, a flow's current step, id, repo, time, count, the Sentry link, the PR link and the acts its state allows, and its title opens the run sheet. The tab's count is the events that need Eric, else the ones going. The edge gate in front of canopy.beric.ca must let `/api/hooks/*` through unauthenticated for Sentry to reach it.

Linear is the second source (`docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-23-linear-trigger-design.md`). A rule with `source: "linear"` takes `labels` (names or ids, default `canopy`), and its `projects` holds team keys; its workflow defaults to `linear-issue` and its `api` to `https://api.linear.app`, and `labels` on a Sentry rule is a bad line. `parseLinear` reads an `Issue` delivery only when one of the rule's labels is new on it: created with it, or an update whose `updatedFrom.labelIds` lacked it (any other edit is a quiet 200). The hook checks `Linear-Signature` with the same `signatureOk`, then refuses a `webhookTimestamp` more than `LINEAR_SKEW` (60 s) off (`linearFresh`), both 401. `ignoreReason` also passes over an issue already completed or canceled. The note leads with "Linear issue ENG-123 came in.", so the branch reads `run/linear-issue-eng-123-…` and Linear's GitHub integration, where installed, links the PR by itself; the description rides in the fence, cut to `MAX_DESCRIPTION`. `TriggerEvent.service` is the rule's source (absent on older events, which read as Sentry), so a test fire on a Linear rule still says Linear. With a `tokenEnv` (a Linear API key, sent bare when it starts `lin_api_`, else as Bearer), `server/linear.ts` writes back over GraphQL: `linearComment` posts the PR's link once the event has one (`linked`), and `linearDone` moves the issue to its team's lowest-position `completed` state on merge (`resolved`), skipping an issue already done. A 200 carrying `errors` counts as a failure. Both are queued from `onPrs` link first, and each writes its mark onto the event as it is after the call (`mark`), not as it was before. An issue with no agent session whose run ends with no fix or fails hears why: `set` asks `issueNoteFor(prev, next, origin)` for the comment (the closing message, or the failure's reason, and a pointer to Retry on the triggers page when `CANOPY_PUBLIC_ORIGIN` is set), and `noteIssue` posts it through `linearNote` with the same `writer`, logging a refusal rather than marking the event. The bundled `lib/workflows/linear-issue.md` (`listed: false`) has `sentry-fix`'s tools and checks first that the issue is clear and small enough for one run, committing nothing and saying what is missing when it is not. The agent (phase 2 of that spec): a linear rule with `clientId` and `clientSecretEnv` takes `AgentSessionEvent` deliveries on the same hook (`parseAgentEvent`; any other rule answers them with a quiet skip). `linearAppToken` mints the app's token with the client credentials grant and holds it in memory by client id until an hour before it ends; `writer` falls back to it when the rule has no `tokenEnv`, and `sayNow` mints again once after a `LinearError` 401. `agentHook` answers at once: it queues a thought and, with `CANOPY_PUBLIC_ORIGIN`, a link to `?view=triggers`, then starts `deliver` with the session, which a duplicate takes over and is told about. `set` asks `activityFor(prev, next)` for the activity each move tells the session and queues it on `telling`, a chain apart from the serial queue so a slow Linear call never holds a delivery up; `idle` waits on it. `fromRun` keeps a done run's closing text as `summary`, which the no-fix and PR responses carry, and holds a done run's event at working until the runner has read its worktree (the branch still says `working`), so a run that commits nothing never passes through `ready` and tells its session of a commit. `linkIssue` puts the PR on the session (`addedExternalUrls`) instead of commenting when the event has one. A message (`prompted`): the stop signal calls `stopEvent`; on a `followable` event (stopped or no-fix with a branch) `followUp` starts a run on the branch through the server's `followUp` dep (`startBranchFix` with the message fenced); on a failed one `retry`; otherwise the session is told why nothing happens. A linear rule may name its `organization`; `otherWorkspace` makes the hook skip a delivery whose `organizationId` differs, before either kind is read. The install: `GET /api/linear/install?rule=` (a GET, so `read`, which a browser redirect can reach with no token) keeps a one-time state in memory and sends the browser to `linearInstallUrl` (`actor=app`, `LINEAR_APP_SCOPES`, `prompt=consent`, the redirect `<CANOPY_PUBLIC_ORIGIN or the request's origin>/api/linear/callback`); `GET /api/linear/callback` takes that state once within `INSTALL_FOR` (10 min), and `linearInstall` trades the code and reads `viewer` and `organization` with the token it got, which is then dropped. The page it answers (`installPage`, every value escaped) names the workspace id to pin. Linear sends the browser back from linear.app, so it arrives marked `sec-fetch-site: cross-site`; the callback is a return leg (`RETURN_LEGS` in `core/library.ts`), which `returnLeg` strips of that mark before `libraryOriginAllowed` judges it, so the host, forwarded protocol and any Origin still decide and the one-time state is what stops a forged return. `TriggerRuleInfo.install` is the link the rule card shows.

## The coordinator

FR-010 (`docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-16-coordinator-design.md`, plan `2026-10-17-coordinator.md`). A `coordinate` run is a chat whose brief is `lib/coordinator.md` (or `<config dir>/coordinator.md`) with the goal as its first message. It starts through `startCoordinator` and reaches canopy over `CANOPY_API` with a `coordinator` token bound to its run (read and plan), held to its lane ("Callers and tokens"). It starts only under `CANOPY_CALLERS=enforce` (`coordinatorRefusal`, a 409 otherwise, and `/api/about` says `coordinator: false`), since its agent runs with its permissions bypassed and `log` or `off` would let it call any route. `core/coordinator.ts` is the pure half: `validatePlan` (at most 8 tasks a call and 20 a coordinator, a title of 120 characters and a note of 4000, `propose` or `ask`, a repo id this backend holds on this machine; an error names the task and field), `nextToStart` (queued cards in order, at most `COORD_AT_ONCE` children working, a card whose repo is full passed over), `childOutcome` and `childSettled` (below), and `settleNote` and `startNote`, the user messages a settled or unstartable card sends into the chat.

`server/coordinator.ts` holds `CoordinatorHub`. The cards are `Run.tasks` on the coordinator's live run, so every run broadcast carries them and they die with the run; the hub writes them and sends the run out again (`{type: "run"}`). Routes: `POST /api/coordinator/plan {run, tasks: [{title, repo, note, action}]}` (right `plan`) adds them as `proposed` with ids `t1`, `t2`, ... per coordinator (a removed card's number is never given again) and answers `{ok, added}`, the new cards; a bad plan is a 400 with the reason. A coordinator's token may name only its own run, and may leave `run` out; a page or the CLI may post for any coordinator. The run must be a `coordinate` run that has not ended (404 unknown, 409 not a coordinator or ended). `POST /api/coordinator/tasks {run, id, title?, note?, repo?, action?, remove?}` edits or removes a `proposed` card under the same limits (409 once it has started) and answers `{ok, task}` or `{ok, removed}`. `POST /api/coordinator/start {run}` is start these: every `proposed` card becomes `queued`, the hub pumps, and the answer is `{ok, tasks}`. Both need `operate`, and the hub refuses a coordinator's token on them too. route() reads the caller once (`callerOf`) and passes it down through `handleApi` to the hub.

The pump runs on one serial chain per hub and starts one card at a time: `nextToStart` again after each start, working counted as children whose run is still active (a child that ended but waits on the landing queue holds no slot). `startCoordChild` in `server/index.ts` starts a card as a branch run through `startBranchRun` with the repo's job agent (`propose` needs Claude Code), `by` coordinator and `parent` set through `StartExtras.parent`, so the run carries it from its first change. It does not open a PR: there is no per-repo PR default, so a child lands through the queue under the repo's review rules, and "open PR" on the ready run still works by hand. A repo whose branch slots are taken (`branchSlotsTaken`, a trigger's count: branch runs outside a flow plus branch flows, or the runner's own count, which takes held runs too, whichever is higher) leaves the card `queued` with the outcome "waiting for a branch slot in <repo>". So does the runner's cap firing after that check (`BranchCapError`, which `startBranchRun` turns into a 409 `SlotsFullError`), since another start can take the last slot while the card's branch is cut. Any other start that throws marks the card `failed` with the error and queues a `startNote`. Any run ending, and any branch flow ending (`onFlow`, from the flows' change fan-out), pumps every coordinator with a card queued, since anyone's branch work may free a slot.

When a child counts as settled (`childSettled`): at once when it failed, stopped, or its branch was read `uncommitted` or `nothing` (done, "no change", unless Eric turned its plan down, which fails it). A `propose` child stays one run through its plan: it waits for Eric's approval as an active run, holding one of the 3 places and a branch slot, and builds under the same id. A `ready` branch waits for the landing queue's last word: landed (done), sent back (failed), a PR open (done, "PR opened"), or a sort that leaves it to Eric (`land` or `approve` verdict, no entry: done, "ready"). An `auto` verdict with no entry waits, since the queue takes it next, and an entry still in the queue waits however long it takes. A ready branch none of those reach within `READY_WAIT` (2 min; a failed sort, a PR that never opened) settles as ready. The hub reads the branch through its `landing` dep (the landing record's verdict and `openPr`, the queue's entry and the PR watch's URL) and looks again on the runner's changes (`onRun`), on every landing and PR broadcast (`recheck`, called from those hubs' broadcast wrappers) and on a timer when the wait runs out. The timer is set only while the wait has yet to run out: a child still unsettled after it has an entry in the queue, and waits on the broadcasts alone. A coordinator ending clears its children's timers. A child whose run is no longer kept fails its card with no note.

Notes: a settled card queues its note per coordinator. The hub says the oldest through `Runner.say` only while the coordinator is `idle`, one per idle turn, in order, and in a microtask after the change that woke it, since saying moves the run under the other listeners. A `say` that throws keeps the note for the next turn. When the coordinator ends, its `proposed` and `queued` cards become `dropped` and its waiting notes go; its children go on as branch runs, and their cards still settle, with nothing said. A child's asks go to the inbox like any run's.

In the UI: the card menu offers "coordinate…" only where the repo's backend's about says `coordinator`, and it opens the plan sheet (`plan(repo, "coordinate")`, the goal as the note, no branch toggles, the `coordinate` role's agent); a coordinator going at the repo turns the entry into "coordinator going", which shows it. A coordinator holds no repo here either: `activeRunFor` passes over it, so asks, chats and workflows start beside it, and `coordinatorFor` finds it. `qRun` qualifies `parent` and each card's `repo` and `run`. `components/CoordTasks.tsx` sits between a coordinator's timeline and its composer, in a block that scrolls on its own: a card's title, repo, kind and note, an edit form and remove while it is proposed (`coordPlanEdit`, `coordRemove`), its state chip, and once started its child's newest step, landing word or PR and "open its run"; "start these" (`coordStart`) shows while any card is proposed. Wider than a phone the block sits under the timeline capped at 40vh and its "hide cards" folds it to its head. On a phone (`useMedia(PHONE)`, so it follows the width live) the sheet keeps one height and shows the cards or the chat, switched by a cards/chat pair in the block's head: the cards view takes the timeline's room (the timeline unmounts, so it comes back following the newest step, and the sheet's error shows under the cards) and hides the composer without unmounting it, so a draft survives; the chat view keeps only the head, with its count, start these and the switch. The sheet opens on `coordView` (pure, in `coord.ts`): the chat with no cards, a question waiting or the coordinator working on started cards, otherwise the cards; picked once per open, never on a status change, and `Console` is keyed by run so the view and fold never carry over. Coming back into sight never focuses the composer, so a phone's keyboard stays down. Words and lines come from the pure `ui/src/coord.ts` (`cardStatus`, `childLine`, `cardCounts`, `coordTurn`). An idle coordinator is your turn only while cards wait for start these (`coordTurn`): `turnRows` leaves it out of "waiting on you" otherwise, and on the agents board its card reads `idle` rather than waiting, since between turns it waits on its children. The writes answer nothing the page keeps, since the run's broadcast carries the change. A child's sheet opens with `ParentLine`: "started by coordinator" and its card's title, with a link back. The agents board marks the coordinate role on a backend with no coordinator runner.

## The kit

The kit (`docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-14-kit-design.md`, phase 1): one set of skills for every agent on every machine, with dotclaude (`~/.claude`) as the store and `~/.claude/kit.json` as the manifest (`CANOPY_KIT` overrides the path). `core/kit.ts` (pure, browser-safe, tested): `parseKit` (the one store target takes no `machines`, so it is every machine's first target), `targetsFor` (the store target first), `skillNames` (what git tracks under `skills/`, less `exclude`, plus `only` entries with a `dev:` or absolute `source`), `wanted`, `planKit` (`mkdir`, `link`, `relink`, `remove`, `pull`; a link target gets a relative link from its dir to the store's, a source gets an absolute link in the store) and `cellsOf` (`ok`, `missing`, `drift`, `foreign`, `local`, `off`, `no-source`, `noagent`, `away`). A name the store does not hold reads `local` in any target (Omarchy's links, an agent's own skills), as does an entry the manifest does not give that agent; an entry for a wanted store skill that the kit did not place reads `adoptable` in a link target and `foreign` in the store target and a copy target, and apply leaves both alone. `relink` and `remove` act only on a placed link that still reads what the kit wrote; one pointed elsewhere by hand is `drift` and stays out of the plan. A scan whose store git fails (`X` line) reads `away` as `store: <git's message>`, so an empty tracked list never plans removals, and an ssh fetch that never printed its `up` marker ends the scan there rather than trying again. `core/kitscan.ts` reads a machine with one sh script through `onHost` (after a `git fetch` with its own 20 s timeout), so gpd reads over ssh exactly as the Mac reads itself; `core/kitapply.ts` acts only on links and on dirs it makes, so a real folder is never replaced or removed, and records what it placed in each dir's `.canopy-kit.json`. `server/kit.ts`'s `KitHub` scans the machines whose `backend` is this backend's name and the ssh machines whose `via` is, at start, every 10 minutes (off under `bun test`), after an apply and on `POST /api/kit/scan`; `GET /api/kit` is `KitInfo`, `POST /api/kit/apply` takes `{machine, actions?, dryRun?}` and refuses an action not in the current plan. The page merges every backend's `KitInfo` (`ui/src/kit.ts`, `components/Kit.tsx`, the `kit` view); CLI `canopy kit status|apply`. On the mini the `canopy` service mounts the host's `~/.hermes` under `/home/bun`. Phase 2 adds MCP servers through the secret store; phase 3 part B (below) adds per-cell apply, skill adopt and tools. OpenClaw copies are not planned.

The MCP writers (`core/kitmcp.ts`, tested with the fake claude in `core/kitfake.ts` against a scratch `HOME`) are the only code that writes an agent's MCP config. Codex's `config.toml` is spliced as text: canopy's tables sit under a `# canopy-kit: managed` line (`codexBlocks`), `spliceCodex` sets, removes, adopts a hand-made table in place or restores one to a plain table, refuses a file that does not parse, a hand-made table of the kit's name and a table split across the file, and parses its own result before handing it back, so every byte outside the marked tables stays. `inplaceWrite` sends the new text on stdin (`ExecOptions.input`, which `onHost` passes over ssh), stages it beside the file under umask 077, compares the file's stamp with the scan's and then `cat`s over it, so the inode and a bind mount of it stay. The stamp is `<mtime> <size> <cksum crc>` (`STAT_FN`, GNU stat then BSD's), since mtime is in whole seconds and a same-size rewrite within the second would pass on the first two; a file that moved is left byte for byte and the write answers "changed since the scan". A final `cat` that fails can leave the file cut short, so the staged copy (0600, the whole new text) stays and the error names it for a restore by hand. Claude's servers go through `claude mcp add-json --scope user` and `claude mcp remove --scope user`. claude 2.1.296 refuses an add over an existing name (exit 1, "already exists in user config"), so `claudeSet` removes first and puts the old entry back when the add fails (a put back that fails too is named in the error), and every error line passes through `scrub`, which takes out env and header values, a url and long args, each also as it reads JSON-escaped. A splice leaves the blank and comment lines that head the next table outside canopy's block, so removing, updating or adopting a table keeps them. One known exception to "no value in argv": add-json has no stdin form, so put back and `claudeSet`'s rollback can pass a hand-made entry holding a literal value on the command line. That is accepted, since the same user can already read `~/.claude.json`, and the kit's own entries never hold values (a secret reaches a server through `canopy-mcp-env`).

Phase 2 (`docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-14-kit-secrets-phase-2-design.md`) adds MCP servers. `kit.json`'s `mcp` block defines each server once (command and args, or a url, with `env` and `headers` holding literals, `${env:NAME}` or `${secret:name}`); `options: { claude, codex }` carries per-agent literals the shared shape cannot (executor's `headersHelper`, Codex's timeouts) and is refused on a server with secrets. An `mcp` block that is not an object is a parse error, as is an `agents` filter naming no agent with an `mcp` target or a `machines` filter naming an undeclared machine, so a typo never plans the removal of servers canopy wrote. A target's `mcp` names the agent's file and format (`~/.claude.json` as `claude-json`, `~/.codex/config.toml` as `codex-toml`). `core/kit.ts` parses the block, hashes a definition (`mcpHash`: SHA-256 over the canonical JSON of command, args, url and the sorted env and header names, before `~` resolves), builds the entry each agent should hold (`buildEntry`; a server with secrets runs through `~/.claude/bin/canopy-mcp-env <server> -- ...`) and plans and paints the server cells (`planMcp`, `mcpCells`) from the scan and the store's plaintext metadata, so allowed, not allowed and stale read the same on every backend without a session. A reader's copy trails the writer's until its next pull, so on a reader `not-allowed`, `stale-allowance` and a missing sink say "in the store this machine last pulled" and to wait for the next pull if it was allowed just now, rather than asking for a second passkey commit, and a sink with no state yet reads as not written until this machine's next sink pass; the writer keeps the plain copy. After an allow or a revoke the allow sheet asks every reader backend to pull (`readersOf`), since the act answers once Forgejo has the commit; the Mac never relays an act, so the pull comes from the page. A server cell reads `ok`, `missing`, `drift`, `foreign`, `adoptable` (a hand-made entry of the kit's name), `not-allowed`, `stale-allowance` (the allowance names an older hash), `no-sink` (the env sink is not in step on that machine), `unsupported` (the agent cannot hold that shape), `unresolved`, `phase-3` or `off`; a server already written stays when its sink only reads `waiting`. `core/kitscan.ts` also reads both files and the ownership file `~/.claude/.canopy-kit-mcp.json` into memory, and whether dotclaude ignores `mcp-env/` and the ownership file and the wrapper runs, and finds `claude` (`KIT_CLAUDE`, the PATH, `~/.local/bin/claude`, and only then a login shell); a scan that fails paints each server the machine wants `away` with the reason rather than leaving the server table empty, and plans nothing for them; `core/kitapply.ts` hands the MCP actions to `applyMcp` in `core/kitmcp.ts`, which writes the entries and then the ownership file (0600, git-ignored by dotclaude). An ownership file that is there but cannot be read or does not parse (`ownedError`) stops every MCP write on that machine and paints its server cells `drift` with the reason, since read as empty its next save would wipe `adoptedFrom`. Adopt moves a hand-made entry into the kit's name and keeps the old one in the ownership file's `adoptedFrom`, so adopt, one or all, answers 409 while dotclaude's git does not ignore that file (`ownedIgnored`, the scan's `J` line), and the adoptable cell says it waits; put back writes it back as it was, through `add-json` (the argv exception above). `KitHub` serves the extra routes `GET /api/kit/preview?machine=&agent=&name=` (the entry with every value masked), `POST /api/kit/adopt` (`names` left out adopts every adoptable server on that agent) and `POST /api/kit/restore`; adopt is refused until the kit's version could be written, and neither runs as part of apply. Apply, adopt and put back run one at a time per machine (`serial`), each from its own checks on, because `applyMcp` saves the whole ownership file from the scan it was handed and two overlapping writes would each save their own copy; each one reads the previous one's rescan from its view, and a scan that began before a write never replaces the view the write's rescan left (`scanOf`). `KitInfo` carries names, states and masked previews only; raw entries stay in `KitHub`. The kit view (`components/Kit.tsx`, `ui/src/kit.ts`) adds a server table under the skills: a cell opens the adopt, put back or allow sheet its state calls for, and sheets are keyed per server and column (`sheetKey`), so one that answers late closes only itself. The allow sheet blocks with a plain reason when the backends hash the server apart (`mcpViewsOf`'s `split`: one has not pulled dotclaude, so one allowance cannot fit both), and when a machine's backend reports no home (`homesFor`: an older build, which needs the new one, apart from a machine not seen yet, which needs a rescan). On the mini the scan runs as `/home/bun`, but `~` in a server and every sink path take `CANOPY_HOST_HOME`, since `~/.claude.json` is shared with the host; the `canopy` and `shells` services mount `~/.claude` at its host path as well so the wrapper's host path runs in both.

Phase 3 part B (`docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-15-kit-phase-3b.md`). **Per-cell apply:** a cell's sheet posts `cellActions(view, cell)` (`ui/src/kit.ts`) to `POST /api/kit/apply`: the plan's `link`, `relink` and `remove` for a skill cell, after its agent's `mkdir` when the plan has one, or `mcp-set` and `mcp-remove` for a server cell, never the other kind of the same name; a store cell whose fix is the pull gets the pull alone. A 409 (an action no longer in the plan) rescans and says the plan moved; the sheet never tries again on its own. **Skill adopt:** `skill-adopt` and `skill-restore` are never in a plan, the same as `mcp-adopt`. `KitHub.adoptSkill` and `restoreSkill` (`POST /api/kit/skill-adopt`, `/api/kit/skill-restore`, `{machine, agent, name}`) run through `serial` and only on a cell that is `adoptable` (or carries `restore`) in the current scan. The apply script's `adopt` moves the entry (a folder, a file or a foreign link, as it is) to `.canopy-kit-adopted/<name>` beside the skills dir (`adoptedPath`; the dir made 0700, refused when that name is taken there), then links, and moves it back if the link fails. It sits in the agent's own folder rather than under `~` because on the mini the container's `~` is not a mount, so a folder moved there would cross devices and be lost on the next recreate. `putback` takes out only the link the kit placed (a link pointed elsewhere by hand, or a real entry, refuses and moves nothing) and moves the entry back. The dir's `.canopy-kit.json` records `adoptedFrom: {name: path}` beside `placed` (written only once an adopt ran there, and kept by every later write); a cell whose name is in it carries `restore`, and an adopted name the store no longer holds keeps an `off` row so put back stays reachable. **Tools:** `kit.json`'s `tools` block (`{name: {check, install?: {darwin?, arch?, debian?}}}`, `parseTools`, errors per field). After the skills scan, `seeTools` (`core/kitscan.ts`) runs one sh script per machine through `onHost`: it reports the os (`darwin`, or `ID` and `ID_LIKE` read from `/etc/os-release`, never sourced), reads the login shell's PATH once from a marked line (a profile that prints cannot spoil it), and runs each check in sh with a 10 s limit and its stdout dropped. Exit 0 is `ok`, 127 is `missing` (or `no-install` when `installLine` finds no line for the os or an `ID_LIKE` word, so ubuntu takes debian's), a check past its time or any other exit is `check-failed` with the first stderr line, and a script that fails paints every tool `away` with the reason. A tool cell has `kind: "tool"` and an empty agent: it is the machine's, makes no column, and the view's tools table gives each machine one cell across its agent columns (`machineSpans`). A missing tool's cell carries its line (`install`) and `sudo` when `needsSudo` sees `sudo` as a word or a path ending in `/sudo`. `POST /api/kit/install {machine, tool, line}` (`KitHub.install`, through `serial`) refuses a tool kit.json does not list (404), a cell that is not `missing` with a line, a sudo line (it cannot prompt over a non-interactive ssh, so the sheet shows it to copy), a tool on a machine whose scan ran in a container (`container`: the tools script reports `/.dockerenv` or `/run/.containerenv`, or `KIT_CONTAINER` when set, which the tests use; on the mini that is canopy's container, built `FROM shells`, so the check holds for the shells' agents, but an install would land in canopy's container alone and be gone at the next recreate, so the sheet says to add the tool to the image) and a line other than the one the page showed (409), then runs the line in the machine's login shell with a 10-minute limit, rescans that machine and answers the exit and the output's last 40 lines. An install is never part of apply all. On the mini the canopy service checks and installs inside its container, not on the host.

## Version

Version: `core/version.ts` (browser-safe, pure, tested: `versionLine`, `buildFrom` with `CANOPY_COMMIT`/`CANOPY_COMMITTED` winning over git, `sameBuild` by commit prefix else version, `buildMoved` for a backend that changed build since the page first asked) and `core/build.ts` (`readBuild`, package.json plus `git log -1`/`status` off canopy's own checkout, plain node APIs since `ui/vite.config.ts` stamps the bundle through it as `__CANOPY_BUILD__`, read as `PAGE_BUILD` in `ui/src/build.ts`). The server keeps `state.about` (`About`) for `GET /api/about`; `canopy version` prints `versionLine`; Settings' `AboutRow` shows both and offers a reload when `sameBuild` says the page and server differ. The image has no `.git`: `scripts/redeploy.sh` exports the commit, compose passes it as build args, and the Dockerfile declares them after the slow layers. The store reads home's `/api/about` at load (`readAbout`, kept as `aboutAtLoad`) and again on every resync of home's stream, so a redeploy, which drops the stream, is noticed when it comes back; the top bar's `ReloadChip` shows while `buildMoved(aboutAtLoad, home's about)`. It compares against the backend's first answer rather than the page's bundle, because a Mac server restarted on a commit made after its last `bun run build` always differs from its bundle and a reload would not clear that (Settings' about row still reports that kind of staleness).

## ui/

`ui/` — separate tsconfig (both are typechecked). State in one Zustand store (`ui/src/store.ts`); server types imported directly from `src/core/types.ts` — keep that file browser-safe (no Bun/node imports). Per-browser preferences (grouping, open target, theme, density, and which of a forge's repos get cards) live in `ui/src/settings.ts` under localStorage `canopy.settings`; every size and every gear choice is also kept per kind of screen and per kind of window (`ui/src/screens.ts`, pure and tested: `screenClass` buckets the screen's longer side into phone, pad, laptop, desktop, wide (up to 3800 CSS px) and ultra (4K and up at their own size); `windowKindOf` names the window a route makes, main, solo, section or shell; `screenSlots` is the chain of slots under `screens` a window reads, closest first: `<class>.<kind>` for any window but the main one, then `<class>`, then `wide` for an ultra screen, which is where its sizes went before it was a class; `withScreen` lays the chain over the flat fields on load; `putScreen` writes the main window's change flat and into the first slot, and a pop-out's (`writesFlat` false) into its own slot alone, leaving the flat copy as the main window set it, so nothing a pop-out sizes reaches the main window, and a screen or window never sized starts from the nearest one that was, then from the last size the main window set anywhere; localStorage is the browser's own, so all of it is per device already, and every gear's menu says at its foot which screen and window it is keeping for, `keptForNow`); the keys are `SCREEN_LAYOUT` in the store (the tree being open and its width, the dock's `dockLayout`, the closed columns' `panelWidths`, solo width, strip, panel shell and feed heights, the focus size, the feed being up, and `dockWidth`, which is read for the phase B migration and never written) and `SCREEN_SETTINGS` in settings (fonts, zoom, preview height, the bench's seams, the popups' sizes, and every gear choice: open target, level, shell place, the changes list's view, columns and sort, section order and hidden sections, close buttons, the carousel and the inbox gear's), a window moved to another kind of screen re-reads them on `resize` or the screen's `change` (compared by the whole slot chain, `slotsNow`), and Settings says which class this screen is and which kind of window this is; the open targets `dock` and `tabs` are the two arrangements of the dock's grid (see Layout and motion): `Dock` renders the same keyed `RepoPanel` children whatever the layout (columns of cells, each column with a seam down its left edge and each cell of tabs with a `CellStrip`, a panel that is not its cell's showing tab `hidden`, which `.panel[hidden]` must say since `display: flex` beats the attribute), so a change of arrangement moves the panels rather than remounting them and a panel shell keeps its pty; the store's `activePanel` is the showing tab, set by every path that opens a panel through the pure `focusPanel` in `ui/src/dock.ts` and moved to a neighbour on close by `nextActive` (tested); grouping logic is pure in `ui/src/grouping.ts` (tested); `changedAt` there is the newer of a repo's last commit and its newest changed file's `mtime` (`newestEdit`), which is what the default `recent` grouping buckets by (and `activity` orders each of its groups by) and what a card's time shows, with the tooltip naming the file when an edit is newer than the commit; the panel head shows the commit's time. `changedAt` also takes `tipAt`, the remote-only commit's time, so a push elsewhere moves the card; `RemoteTip.tsx` holds `whenTitle` (the time's tooltip naming the edit or the branch that set it), the `⇣ origin/branch` chip (dropped when the branch is the upstream, which `↓n` already covers) and the `⇄ n` pull request link, on the card and in the panel head. `ui/src/routes.ts` reads `?repo=&view=solo`, which renders one panel edge to edge for the new-tab / new-window targets, and `view=shell`, one shell the same way; `popupFeatures` sizes that window off the screen it opens on (pure and tested). `RepoLink` is the remote's glyph, on a card and in the panel head (it swallows its own click, middle click and Enter so the card underneath does not open); `RepoMenu` is the per-card ⋯ menu (portal-rendered, keyboard-navigable, closed by Escape and an outside pointerdown heard in capture, since every trigger stops its own; it and every `Gear` open one at a time through `oneMenu` in `ui/src/menus.ts`, and their buttons let an Escape go on while their menu is shut, `triggerKeeps`, so a full-screen panel hears it; one Escape still closes one layer, since a surface's `useLeaveOnEscape` yields while a run sheet, a top-bar popover, a menu or the drawer is up (`LAYER_ABOVE` in `surface.ts`, passed to `escapeLeaves`; not the guided panel's tour, a dialog with no Escape of its own; the page's own keys in `App.tsx`, the drawer's Escape, select mode and `n`, wait on the same less the drawer, `OVER_PAGE`) and the run sheet, the drawer and every popover and the new-shell menu (`escapeCloses`) `preventDefault` the Escape they take, for when their listener runs first; chat, the jobs, agent, herdr and agent settings under "with claude", the plain apps under "open in"); `RunSheet` is the one modal (pre-flight, the run console with the chat's composer, the agent settings form, or the search across the repos in view); `RunChip` is the run's word on a card or panel. `TermDock.tsx` holds the shells: `TermView` is one xterm.js on the websocket (inside `.term-view`, a column of `.term-screen`, the xterm's host, and on a coarse pointer the `KeyBar`: esc/tab/arrows/^C and sticky ctrl/alt whose bytes come from the pure `ui/src/keys.ts`, `keyBytes` honouring xterm's `applicationCursorKeysMode` and `withMods` applying an armed modifier to the phone keyboard's next single character in `onData`, tested in `keys.test.ts`; its buttons act on pointer down with the default prevented so the xterm keeps focus and the keyboard stays up, and `index.html`'s viewport has `interactive-widget=resizes-content` so the keyboard shrinks the page; a paste key reads the clipboard where the page is a secure context. The clipboard on a desktop: a program's OSC 52 (Claude Code copies its own mouse selection so, through tmux's passthrough and `load-buffer -w`; a plain program's raw one, vim's or codex's or anything's over ssh, through `set-clipboard on` in `lib/tmux.conf`) goes through the pure `osc52Text` in `ui/src/osc52.ts` (a query is never answered) to `copyText` in `share.ts`, whose fallback off a secure page is the copy command with the text handed to its event so the xterm keeps focus; `shortcutOf` in `keys.ts` is ⌘C (the xterm selection, else a program's copy the browser refused) and ⌘←/⌘→/⌘⌫ as `^A`/`^E`/`^U` on an Apple keyboard, Ctrl+Shift+C elsewhere, attached through `attachCustomKeyEventHandler`; ⌘V stays the browser's paste event; `macOptionClickForcesSelection` makes Option-drag select past a program that has the mouse. xterm 6 has no touch handling of its own, so `TermView` listens on `.term-screen` (`touch-action: none` on a coarse pointer): a drag past `TAP_SLOP` scrolls through `term.scrollLines`, arrows in the alternate buffer, or synthetic wheel events when the program tracks the mouse; a flick glides on rAF; two fingers pinch the font size into the setting for the spot the shell shows in, which every shell there follows; a touch that stays put is left to arrive as xterm's click, which focuses it without raising the keyboard (below); the key bar's ⌨ raises it through `showKeyboard`. The sums are the pure `ui/src/touch.ts`, tested in `touch.test.ts`. The phone's keyboard comes up only for a finger on a text field: `installSoftKeys` (`ui/src/softkeys.ts`, run once from `main.tsx`) watches pointer down, focusin and focusout on the document and, on a coarse pointer, gives any text field focused without a tap on it or its label (`tappedOn`, `typesInto`, pure and tested) `inputmode="none"` until it loses focus, so a dialog's caret, a shell taking focus as it connects or shows, and a tap on a shell's screen keep the keyboard down while a hardware keyboard still types; a later tap on the held field drops the hold so that tap raises the keyboard, and `showKeyboard` (the ⌨ key) lets go of the focused textarea and takes it again with the hold off, since a phone decides on its keyboard as focus arrives. On a coarse pointer the tab row's buttons are finger sized and the grip becomes a row with a handle), kept mounted and hidden when not showing so switching never ends a session (the theme is read off the CSS tokens through a probe element since `light-dark()` does not resolve in `getComputedStyle` of `:root`); a socket that drops without an exit frame is reconnected after a second, then at doubling waits up to 30s for as long as the view is up, with `attach=1` once a socket has been open (one that never opened, the server being away, may still start the shell), the screen cleared in band (`ESC[2J ESC[3J ESC[H`, never `term.reset()`, which stopped xterm 6 painting) when the replay's first bytes land, and `TERM_GONE` ends the tab with "[the shell is gone]"; `TermDock` is the strip along the bottom, `PanelShells` the foldable, drag-resizable section in a repo's panel, `ShellSolo` the `?repo=&view=shell` page a new tab or window shows, which writes its shell's name into the url as `term=` (`nameShellHere` in routes.ts) so its reload comes back to it. A tab's id is the shell's name on the server (`termId()` in `ui/src/term.ts`, 32 hex digits), `TermTab` lives there too, and the layout persists `terms` and `activeTerm`: `init` reads `GET /api/terms` and sets the tabs through the pure `reconcileTerms` (tested; the saved tabs whose shell is still held, then every held shell no tab names as a tab at its `place`, both only for repos in the scan), so no tab shows before the server has said which shells it holds (a tab shown sooner would open its socket and start a shell under the old name), opens the panel (shell section unfolded) of any panel shell whose repo has none here, the way `openTerm` does, and `loadTermTabs` drops a saved tab that had exited. Two grove windows share `canopy.layout` and `terms` is one field, so a tab one window adds can be overwritten by the other's next save; the shell is still held, and the next load adopts it back. A dockless window keeps no saved tabs and writes none, except that a pop-out (`popped=1`) takes up its own panel's held shells as tabs, so a panel's shells travel with it (`adoptHere`). The store keeps every shell in `terms` with its `place` (panel or strip), `activeTerm` for the strip, and the persisted `termHeight` (strip) and `panelTermHeights` (panel shells, by repo id, read through `panelTermHeightFor`, pruned with `panelWidths`); `openTerm` resolves the `shell` setting through the pure `shellPlace` in `settings.ts` (tested), opens the repo's panel and unfolds its shell section when the shell lands there; since a socket closing only detaches, `closeTerm` ends the shell behind the tab it drops through `DELETE /api/terms` (`endShells`); `closePanel` only drops its panel shells' tabs, leaving the shells held, and `openPanel` adopts them back. The changes section is `ChangesList` in `Dock.tsx`: a filter box, a list/folders toggle, a header whose buttons sort on click and reorder by drag or Alt+arrow, and the rows on one grid (`.file-table`, rows through subgrid so the columns line up; the folders view adds a full-width heading row per folder, folders in the order their first sorted file comes); the column order, sort and view are the `fileCols`/`fileSort`/`fileView` settings, and the pure parts (`sortFiles`, `filterFiles`, `groupByFolder`, `moveCol`, `colOrder`) live in `ui/src/files.ts`, tested. Each file carries `mtime` from the server: `getStatus` stats every changed file (local via fs, remote through one `sh -c` loop that tries GNU then BSD stat), only for a repo with changes and best effort. Each panel section (changes, shell, search, history, launch, claude) folds through `toggleSection(repoId, key)`; the closed keys persist in `closedSections`, a record by repo id (a repo with no entry folds `DEFAULT_CLOSED`: search, history, claude, launch), read through `closedIn(s, repoId, key)` (a boolean, so the selector is stable) and pruned with `panelWidths` when a repo leaves the scan. The layout (`canopy.layout` in localStorage) also keeps `panels`, `dockLayout` and `activePanel`, so a reload shows the same dock; it is written by one store subscription that saves only the fields a change touched (`changed` + `saveLayout(patch)`), so no action saves by hand and two windows on the same key do not overwrite each other's dock; a solo or shell window (`dockless`) never writes `panels`, `dockLayout`, `activePanel`, `popped`, `terms` or `activeTerm`, since what it holds is the grove's copy, and `treeState` keeps the same `panels` array when a scan prunes nothing so a scan is not a change. `RepoPanel` is a `.panel-body` that scrolls (head through claude) with `PanelShells` below it as the panel's footer, so a shell stays in view whatever the body shows. `Search.tsx` holds both search surfaces: `SearchSheet` (the magnifier in the top bar or ⌘⇧F; searches `visibleRepos` minus forge and unreadable ones through `api.grepAll`, one foldable row per repo with hits, and **panel** calls the store's `searchIn`, which closes the sheet, opens the repo's panel, unfolds its search section and leaves the term in `pendingSearch`) and `SearchSection` (the panel section, its own box scoped to one repo, which takes `pendingSearch` when it is for its repo and runs it). Hits render through `groupHits`/`markHit` in `ui/src/hits.ts` (pure, tested), and a hit click is `api.openFile`. A zustand selector that builds a new array (like `visibleRepos`) must go through `useShallow`, or the component re-renders forever and React unmounts the whole tree. A folded shell section hides its body with `display:none` rather than unmounting, so the pty keeps running; One thing at a time is in front: the store's `front`, the `Front` union in the pure `ui/src/front.ts` (tested), is the strip's shells (`{kind: "strip"}`, dropped by `keepFront` when the strip empties) or one project's bench (`{kind: "project", repoId, task, pick}`, dropped only when its panel closes or leaves the scan). The strip in front keeps `FrontOthers` under its tabs, every other running shell through the pure `otherShells` in `ui/src/term.ts` (tested); a click is `bringTerm`, which joins the shell if needed and moves the front to the strip or the shell's project bench (`frontForTab`), `pick` telling the bench's shells which tab to show. `TermGrip` drags the strip's or a panel shell's height (`edge` picks which way it grows); a panel shell's grip is on the section's top edge (the shells are the panel's footer, so that is the edge with room) and its `--panel-term-h` sizes the `.term-body` alone (the tabs sit above at their own height), its grip stops where the panel's scrolling part would fall under `PANEL_BODY_MIN` (120px, `shellRoom` through `TermGrip`'s `fit`, with `.panel-shells`' `max-height: calc(100% - 120px)` holding the same floor when the window shrinks), and its default is `PANEL_TERM_ROWS` (5) lines through `rowsPx` in `ui/src/term.ts`, which measures a row the way xterm 6 does (canvas font metrics times the line height) off the one `TERM_FONT` both it and the xterm read; each panel's shell has its own height, and the one global `panelTermHeight` a layout kept before is not read. The store keeps `runs` by id, `agents` as each backend's `AgentRoutes` (`agentFor(s, repo, role)`, `routesOf`), and `sheet` for what the modal shows. `history` in the store is the claude-history overview (fetched after first paint, on rescan, and every 10 min); `Rings` draws the per-day strip on cards and in the panel (levels from the pure `ui/src/rings.ts`, tested); `Claude` is the panel section (list, search, turn-by-turn detail, open note). `Commit` is the drill: `CommitRow` renders a log row as a button and, open, the commit's message, files with counts and bars, and per-file diffs; the panel's history and a session's commits both use it, one open at a time, keyed by hash. Details are cached per hash for the page's life since a commit never changes. `FlowSheet.tsx` is the modal for one flow or one fleet: a step strip and, on a gate, continue/retry/stop buttons for a flow, or per-repo rows (skipped, pending, active, done, failed) for a fleet; it imports `fleetSkipReason` straight from `src/core/flow.ts`, which is why that file must stay free of Bun/node imports; the class is pure and takes its Runner and evaluator as injected hooks, and only the server instantiates it. `SelectBar.tsx` is the select-mode bar scoped to the git view: a tri-state master `Tick` (exported, reused on the grid and tree headings), the count, all/none/invert, an "only…" facet picker that picks just the repos in one state through `pickWhere`, then the workflow picker and start. Select mode starts empty; a card or tree row click toggles a repo, shift-click sets the stretch from the last click (`selectAnchor`) to the anchor's state, ⌘A picks all, Escape leaves (a sheet, menu or popover owns Escape while it is up), `x` toggles the mode. The pure parts live in `ui/src/select.ts` (tested): `pickState`/`pickCount` for a tick, `setPick`/`togglePick`/`invertPick` over id lists, `rangeIds` over `boardOrder` (the grouped order the board shows, folded groups left out so a range cannot cross into one), `PICK_FACETS` and `pickWhere`; `pickable` is the per-repo predicate in `flows.ts`. The store keeps `selected` as picked, but reads through `pickedIds` (picked ∩ pickable ∩ in view), so a pick hidden by a filter drops off the count and the fleet and comes back when the filter clears (all/none/invert/facet replace `selected` with what is in view, a card toggle folds into it); `toggleSelected` refuses a repo `pickable` rejects, so no caller needs its own guard. `ui/src/flows.ts` holds the pure words and arithmetic for flows and fleets (`stepWord`, `flowWord`, `fleetCounts`), tested. The store's `flowRuns` maps a step's run id to its flow id for every run a flow owns; those runs stay off the cards, since `runFor`/`activeRunFor` skip any run listed in `flowRuns` and the flow's own chip already speaks for the run underneath. `Launch.tsx` is the launch section of the panel (folded by default; `showLaunch` in the store opens a repo's panel with it unfolded): a `Seg` over builds, releases and pull requests, the repo's jobs above the list with their progress and output tail, and the builds re-read on the store's `buildsAt[repoId]`, which every `builds` event bumps. `LaunchForm` in `RunSheet.tsx` is the settings sheet (`{kind: "launch"}`), four text lines saved on blur. `launchFor(s, repo)` and `jobsFor(s, repoId)` (through `useShallow`) are the selectors. `Feed.tsx` is the event feed: a strip along the bottom above the shell strip (toggled by the top bar's list glyph or `e`, height dragged through the same `TermGrip`), one line per server event across every source, chips to narrow it to one source, a "quiet lines" box for the lines that only say a repo was re-read with no change, and a jump button that counts what arrived while the reader scrolled up. The lines come from the pure `ui/src/feed.ts` (tested): `describeEvent(ev, prev, at)` diffs the event against the store's state before it is applied (a repo event becomes branch, commit, ahead/behind and per-file lines through `statusLines`; a scan becomes one line per source with new and gone repos by name; runs report their start, each new step, prompts and how they ended; flows and fleets their step and status changes), `appendFeed` numbers and caps them at `FEED_CAP`, `filterFeed` applies the source and quiet choices. `applyEvent` computes the lines first, then applies the event; the window's own rescans and source edits also go through `applyEvent` with a `scan` event so they land in the feed and the broadcast that follows finds the same `scannedAt` and says nothing twice. `feedOpen` and `feedHeight` persist in the layout; the feed itself is page-lifetime only. `ui/src/peers.ts` (pure, tested): `peerable(repo)` is the same launch-root/no-host/no-forge/no-error test the server's own `peerable` makes, so nothing peer-related ever shows for a repo peer sync does not cover; `peerChips` turns a `PeerState` into a diverged chip (`⇅ peer ↑behind ↓ahead`), a wip chip (`WIP on peer/branch 12m ago`, aged through the shared `ago()`, every peer timestamp riding in as milliseconds and divided by 1000 at the call site) or an only chip (no peer has this repo); `seenWord` is the top bar's `mini · 3m ago` or, for a peer that failed to answer, `gpd · offline` with no age, since `PeerSeen.at` stamps the failed attempt, not the last time it actually answered; `peerLines` is the feed's diff between two `PeerState` readings (a branch that fast-forwarded, a divergence or a WIP not reported before); `mergeAction` says what "merge with claude" should do about a repo's live run: `new` when there is none, `say` into an idle chat's next turn, `busy` to refuse. `PeerChips` in `RemoteTip.tsx` renders those chips on the card and the panel head; `TopBar.tsx`'s `PeersChip` is the top bar's one word per peer off `peerSeen`, rust for an offline one; `Dock.tsx`'s `PeersSection` is the panel's peers fold (closed by default, in `DEFAULT_CLOSED`), showing each pending WIP with take and view (opening its commit through `CommitRow`), each divergence with shell and "merge with claude" (`openChat` with a fixed first message into the repo's existing chat or a new one, no new run type, amendment 5), each peer-only branch with track, and a "sync now" footer button; it renders nothing when the backend's `peerSync` is off. The store's `takeWip`/`trackBranch`/`seedRepo`/`syncPeers` each `POST /api/repos/peer` and fold the answered repo back in through `applyPeerRepo`, and `takeWip` also answers where the WIP went, which the Dock's WIP rows say in a hint line, in the same `takeWords` the command line prints; a `peers` server event sets `peerSeen` and re-reads `/api/peers` so the mode follows the config. Several backends on one page (`ui/src/backends.ts`, `ui/src/registry.ts`, `ui/src/qualify.ts`, `ui/src/checkouts.ts`, `ui/src/components/Backends.tsx`; the design is `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-26-multi-backend-design.md`, amendments 6-8): `init` reads `GET /api/backends` through `api.backends()` before anything else; failing that, the cached `settings.backends` names still decide who owns an id without connecting, and with no cache the registry is the home backend alone, named `home`. The home backend, the one that served the page, keeps its ids bare; every other backend's ids read `<name>|<id>`, and only `ui/src/api.ts`, through `registry.ts`'s `qual`/`split`/`backendOf`/`plainOf`, ever adds or strips that prefix (the store mints a new shell's own id the same way, `qual(backend, termId())`). `registry.ts` is the module's live state: the `Reg` (`home`, `names` in preference order) and each backend's base URL (`setBase`/`baseOf`, `""` for the page's own origin). `backends.ts` is the pure arithmetic over it: `qualify`/`split`, `onPublicSide` and `pickUrl` (a public-side page uses a backend's `public` URL, any other uses `tailnet` and falls back to `public`, an https page never takes an http URL, and a URL that is the page's own origin means that backend is home), the `BackendState`/`BackendStatus`/`BackendSignal` union and the pure reducer `backendState` (answered or a stream opening means online, a 401 means `signin` with the gate's login page, no response means `offline` with a reason, a dropped stream after a sign-in prompt stays a sign-in since the gate refuses the stream the same way, and `prev` itself when nothing changed so the store does not churn), `sliceIn` (a merged list with one backend's part replaced, in registry order), `wsUrl`, `isLaunchSource` and `signinUrl` (the gate's login link with `next` set to the backend's own base). `ui/src/qualify.ts` qualifies every id a backend's answers and events carry before the store ever sees them (`qRepo`, `qRun`, `qFlow`, `qFleet`, `qJob`, `qTerm`, `qKept`, `qDevice`, `qHistory`, `qGrep`, and `qEvent` dispatching per `ServerEvent` type; `workspaces`, `agents`, `launchers`, `helpers`, `peers` and `chan` events pass through `qEvent` unqualified, since they carry paths and names rather than ids that already belong to one backend) and merges several backends' archive overviews onto the first available one's days (`mergeHistory`), so one set of rings spans the whole board. `applyEvent(ev, from = home)` drops anything from a backend not currently shown (`isShown`) before doing anything else, then tags the rest onto `from`: `agents[from]`/`launchers[from]` replace that backend's own map, a `helpers` event lands on that backend's `Conn`, and `workspaces` and `chan` are dropped outright for a non-home `from`, since those stay the home backend's alone (amendment 8); a `peers` event re-reads that backend's own state (`Conn.peerSync`), but sets the shared `peerSeen` only from home. `ui/src/checkouts.ts`'s `joinRepos` turns every backend's repos into `RepoCard`s, at most one checkout per backend on a card (the key is `forge:<id>` for a forge-only repo, which never joins another card, else the first remote `webUrl` maps to a web page, lowercase host/owner/name, else `rel:<plain id>`; a second checkout of the same remote on one backend gets its own `base#plain` card, so a single-backend board is unchanged), and `leadOf` says which checkout a card shows: the one last preferred for it while its backend is online, else the first online one, else the last preferred, else the first. The store keeps `conns`, one `Conn` per backend (base, `BackendStatus`, `about`, `Backend`, `ClientInfo`, helpers, keeping, `peerSync`, read through `connOf`/`homeConn`/`isOnline`/`multi`); a `repo`, `scan`, `run`, `flow`, `fleet`, `job`, `devices`, `kept` or `terms` event from `applyEvent` folds into `from`'s own slice through `sliceIn`, `recordIn` and `pruneByRepo`, so an id home also has is never confused with the foreign one and a scan from one backend never prunes another's panels; a backend whose connect fails (no usable URL or no answer) is tried again by itself at `retryWait` (doubling from `RETRY_FIRST`, 2s, up to `RETRY_MAX`, 60s, in `backends.ts`) while it is shown, the `retries` map in the store cleared when it answers, is hidden or its init is cleaned up, so a machine asleep at load comes in when it wakes; `parkedTerms` holds a saved shell tab whose backend has not connected yet, so it survives a home scan and comes back once that backend answers; `checkoutPref` is which backend's checkout a card opens, by card key in the layout; and `agents`, `launchers` and `histories` are kept per backend, since the same absolute path can name a different checkout on a different machine. `RepoGrid.tsx`'s `MachineStrip` puts one chip per checkout on a card (name, dirty count, ahead/behind, dashed and grey for a backend not online) shown only when `multi`, more than one backend is currently shown; `Dock.tsx`'s `PanelMachines` is the panel head's switcher over a card's checkouts, and `PanelAway`/`PanelWaiting` cover a backend that is offline, asking to sign in, or still connecting; `Backends.tsx`'s `BackendsChip` is the top bar's one word per backend, rust for anything but online, with a popover of state, the URL in use, a version-mismatch note, retry and sign-in links, and the "show here" checkbox that hides or restores a backend (gated on the registry itself, not on how many are shown, so hiding the only other backend never also hides the control that brings it back); `SettingsMenu` gains a backend picker that scopes desktop openers, keep-shell-history and About to one backend at a time. Settings is a rail of tabs beside the open one (`.settings-pop.prefs`), the rail split by where a setting lives: "this browser" (look, layout, opening, this device, keys) and the backend the picker names (desktop, signed in, shell history, ranger, about), each tab showing its current value. A tab with nothing to show there (no ranger, no caller check) is left off the rail. A find box tops the rail: typed words are matched against each section's heading, choices and a few names people use for it (`words` on each entry of `sections`), every matching section shows at once in the panel, and the rail fades the tabs with no match. The first Escape clears the box, the next closes Settings. The menu fetches About and the callers each time it opens, so the about tab and the rail's foot can say the page is another build than the server. On a phone the rail is a row of tabs pinned along the sheet's top. Workspaces, tailchan, the retro lessons and the Library and Ports views stay the home backend's alone (amendment 8, the incubator's projects since excepted; the preview section followed until a backend could name its slots publicly), and a workspace filter keeps only home checkouts. A workspace's tab shows a `ws-dot` in its color, and the active tab's `WsGear` (a `Gear` with `perScreen` off, since its choices live in the backend's config) lists the run entries `WS_RUNS` ("ask in workspace…", "chat in workspace…", which `openWsPlan` turns into a plan sheet on the primary with `Sheet.workspace` set and `startWsRun` posts), the member to make primary (one a workspace run refuses to start in shows off with why, `primaryRefusal` in `ui/src/workspaces.ts`: missing from the tree, on another host, on the forge, or an incubator seed; the server keeps its own refusal, and a Codex route is left to it, since that depends on the action) and the color, each saved through `api.wsLook`. The plan sheet takes its blurb and note placeholder from `planWords(action, workspace)`, which words them for the workspace on a workspace run and returns the action's own text otherwise. A card in the active workspace takes its color as `data-ws-color` (a rule down its edge) and a "primary" chip when `isPrimary`, and the run sheet and the feed say "in workspace X" for a run with `Run.workspace`. `src/server/multi.test.ts` runs two real servers and drives the ui's api layer against both, loaded by a dynamic `import()` since the ui's DOM-typed modules and the server's node-typed ones cannot share a tsconfig, to prove a foreign id's calls reach the right backend with the plain id and its answers come back qualified.

Plan, then build in the UI: `propose` is a job beside `ask` (`JOBS` in `RepoMenu.tsx`, "plan, then build…", and "plan, then build in workspace…" in the workspace gear, `WS_RUNS` in `TopBar.tsx`). The repo menu disables its entry with "plan, then build needs Claude Code" on a Codex repo; the workspace gear always offers its entry, and on a Codex primary the server refuses the start with that same 400. `ProposalForm` (`Prompts.tsx`, reached through `RunPromptForm` for a `proposal` prompt, shared by `RunSheet`) shows the plan preformatted with "approve, ask before commands", "approve, run on its own" only when `prompt.auto`, a note box whose `revise` (Cmd/Ctrl+Enter too) sends `reviseAnswer(note)` (a `deny` carrying the note, or "Revise the plan." when empty) and "turn it down" (a bare `deny`). `RunSheet` keeps the latest plan after it settles as a `details` card ("the approved plan" for either approval, since a refused bypass falls back to asking, "the plan, sent back", "the plan, turned down", `PROPOSAL_SUMMARY`), chosen by `settledProposal` in `runs.ts` from `run.proposalState`, so the card stays through a build of any length and shows nothing while a proposal waits (its form shows it) or on a run from a backend that did not keep the state; `RunChip` reads the same field through `progressWord`, so a propose run's "planning…" turns to "building…" once the plan is approved and renders `run.todos` as the checklist (`ol.todos`, the `active` form while in progress). The timeline is folded by `ui/src/runs.ts`: `hideTodoSteps` drops the main thread's todo tool steps once the checklist exists (a subagent's stay), and `nestSteps` groups each subagent's steps under their `Agent` call by `RunStep.parent`, so parallel subagents read as lanes; `Step` shows them in a "n steps inside" `details` (`KidSteps`) that opens when one of them starts running, since an async `Agent` step is done the moment it launches, and otherwise keeps the user's own toggle, so a fold the user opened does not close when the last subagent step finishes. In the inbox a proposal is its own `kind` (`InboxItem`, glyph "≡", title "plan to approve", the plan's first 400 characters as detail) with no form: it is answered in the run sheet, where the whole plan shows, so it never falls to the permission form. The feed says "has a plan to approve" (the same words the tailchan hand-off uses).

Incubator action responses carry their sprout's backend into `applyEvent`,
including when that backend was hidden while the request was pending, so
the visibility check applies to the right machine. Sprout lists have a
generation per backend: a newer read or hiding the backend invalidates
earlier reads, including ones that arrive after reconnection.

Live counts in the view menu and the group headings come from `ui/src/badges.ts` (pure, tested). `ViewBadge` in `App.tsx` selects `badgeOf(state, key)` through `useShallow`: git is `attentionCount` (the pill's own number), agents the home broker's live cards (rust while one waits), health the checks `down`, `healing` or `gave-up` on every backend, incubator the sprouts from `queued` through `deploying` plus `approving` (rust while one approves); the other tabs carry none. `GroupSummary` (`GroupHead.tsx`) sums a group with `groupSum`: dirty repos, `lines`, ahead, worktrees and live agents (each `agentsOn` list is the store's memoized array, so the shallow compare holds), the whole line in the grid's heading and the dirty count and agents in the tree's, the rest in its tooltip.
