The views are one `ViewMenu` (`App.tsx`), a `PickMenu` (`components/PickMenu.tsx`) whose groups are `VIEW_GROUPS` (repos, agents, system); its button is the view showing with its badge, and a dot in the tone of the most pressing badge elsewhere (`elsewhereTone`, a string so the selector settles). `PickMenu` is also the workspace menu (`WsMenu`, whose openers and gear sit outside it, since a `Gear` opens in a portal and a press there would close it) and the grouping menu; on a phone it comes up as a sheet like every `.settings-pop`. The backend's chips are one `BackendMenu` (`components/BackendMenu.tsx`): the live light and the backends' names, with `BackendsSection` and `DevicesSection` (from `Backends.tsx` and `Devices.tsx`) and the peers in its menu.

"Fix with the agent" (`components/AgentFix.tsx`, the prompt in pure `ui/src/fixit.ts`) sits beside every failed move an agent can finish: the dock's notes (`GitNote`: pull, push, commit, commit + push, a file's stage checkbox, spec sync), the peers section (peer sync, track), a peer WIP's take, the "waiting on you" rows' push and pull, and a command-line push, pull or commit (`CliEntry.fix`, set by `fixFor` in `clirun.ts` for a git failure, never for a refusal of canopy's own). A failed or given-up task's row in the Tasks section and in the top bar's task list (`TasksChip`) gets one too, worded "fix" (`short`) for the room: pressed, it reads the task's log, keeps what follows the last start marker (`lastRun`) and sends `taskFixPrompt`, the task's name, command and exit with the last 60 lines of that run, and asks for the command to be run again until it passes, without a commit or push. `fixMove` is the allowlist: opening an app, a commit message the agent CLI could not write, seeding `.env`, starring and archiving offer nothing. A move the user pressed is the agent's to supervise, so a fresh failure goes over without a press: `freshFailure` holds within `AUTO_FIX_MS` of the failure's `at`, which defaults to the button's mount and rides on `CliEntry.fix`, so a reopened transcript starts nothing, and a module-level set of repo, move and `at` hands each failure over once. A task fails with nobody pressing anything, so it waits for the press. Either way a move sends the store's `openChat` `fixPrompt(move, text)`: the move pressed, the last 40 lines of what came back, and the go-ahead to finish it (a plain commit adds "Do not push"), keeping the user's work and asking before anything that discards it. An idle chat on the repo takes it as its next turn; a busy run's refusal shows beside the button (`.note-why`). Once handed over, the note follows the run in `FIX_WATCH`'s words (on it, needs you, answered, done, failed, stopped) with "open chat" (`showRun`), and the button comes back when the run failed, stopped or was dismissed. `GitNote` passes `onDone`: when the repo's status shows the move finished (`moveDone`: nothing ahead or behind after a push, nothing behind after a pull, nothing staged or conflicted after a commit), the failure turns into `fixedNote`, "The agent finished the push." A seed (`isSeedId`) gets no button, since its agents run through the incubator. The lock note's "clear lock" and this button share one row (`.note-acts`).

Keyboard shortcuts (FR-021, FR-022) are one table, `SHORTCUTS` in pure `ui/src/shortcuts.ts` (tested in `shortcuts.test.ts`): each entry's press (`pressOf` names ⌘ and Ctrl alike as `mod+`, adds `shift+` only under it, keeps a bare key as typed since Shift is what types `?`, `*` and `:`, and gives null with Alt), what it does, the views it works on (every view when absent) and whether it works from a text box (`anywhere`, ⌘K and the find chord). `App.tsx`'s one keydown handler reads a press through `shortcutFor(press, view)` and switches on the entry's `action`, so a key that moves views or changes meaning changes in the table and nowhere else; the checks that depend on what is up (a sheet, `OVER_PAGE`, select mode, the drawer) stay in the handler's cases. `?` opens the sheet `{kind: "keys", view}` (`openKeys`; `KeysSheet` in `components/Keys.tsx`), which lists `shortcutsOn(view)` with `keyCaps` (⌘ and ⇧ on an Apple keyboard, Ctrl and Shift elsewhere) and then `GO`. `g` arms a chord for `CHORD_MS` (1.5 s, a ref in App, not the store): the next key outside a text box is the chord's whatever it means alone, `chordView` names the view it goes to (`g` git, `b` agents, which opens on its board, `s` sessions, `t` triggers, `i` incubator, `l` library, `p` ports, `h` health, `c` costs, `k` kit), and a letter that names none does nothing. Neither `?` nor `g` acts while a sheet or anything in `OVER_PAGE` is up.

## The command line

The CLI's words in the page (pure `ui/src/cli.ts`, tested in `cli.test.ts`; the runner `ui/src/clirun.ts`; the components in `components/CommandLine.tsx`). One source for the verbs: the CLI's usage text is `HELP_TEXT` in browser-safe `src/core/help.ts` (the CLI bolds the name and prints it), and `helpEntries` reads it into verb, usage, words and the deeper-indented lines under each; a line like `push <repo> | pull <repo>`, two verbs taking the same words, becomes two entries, while `list | show <id>` stays one. On an empty line `complete` offers every verb, not the first eight: a verb with subcommands shows them (`SUBS`), the rest show their words, or the first indented non-flag line when the usage left no room for words (`library`, `new`). `ENTRIES` adds the command line's own `help` and `clear`. `tokenize` splits a line as a POSIX shell does, `parseCommand` turns words into a `Command` union (an error carries the usage line to quote), `findRepo` resolves a word (id, then name, then an id's `/tail`, then a unique prefix of either, the last only for commands that read or go somewhere: `repoFor(word, true)` in the runner), `underDir` takes the repos under a `[dir]` word, and `complete` offers the next word by slot (`SLOTS`, `SUBS`, `FLAGS`) with the ghost text being the rest of the first offer.

`runLine` never spawns the CLI: it carries the command out with the store and the API, so a repo's command goes to that repo's backend (remote and `source:` ids included) and works in the container; `peers`, `version`, `ws` and `source` ask the home backend. `source add` refuses a relative local folder, which only the backend could resolve. The answers are `Line`s from `src/core/treelines.ts` (browser-safe, pure, tested): toned segments (`moss`, `lichen`, `sky`, `rust`, `dim`, `bold`), a segment with `repo` being a link. The tree (`treeLines`), the status marks and summary, and the spec listing (`specLines`, `SPEC_WORDS`, `SPEC_TONE`) live there, and `cli/render.ts` paints the same lines as ANSI (`paintLine`), so the terminal and the page draw one layout. A command that goes somewhere (`goesElsewhere`: open without `--app`, `library`, `incubator`, `new`, `launch` with more words, which opens the launch section) never opens the transcript; the rest open it as they start. `new` parses to a `SproutDraft` (idea, links, repo; `--file` and `--backend` are refused, the form takes both), which the store's `draftSprout` puts on the `new-sprout` sheet for `IntakeForm`'s `initial`. A whole `peers sync` reads `/api/peers` first and refuses when sync is off, since the server accepts the request either way. Machine-side commands (`ui`, `helper`, `peers init`, `peers gate`, `source add --token`) parse to `{kind: "terminal"}` and only say where to run them: `peers gate` is the forced command a peer's ssh key runs and must never be reachable from a browser.

The transcript is a reserved dock panel, `CLI_PANEL` (`/cli`), beside "waiting on you": `isReservedPanel` in `waiting.ts` covers both wherever a panel id would otherwise read as a repo's (`mineOf`, so no scan prunes it; pop out and the bench refuse it; the dock's strip and grid). The store keeps `cliLog` (at most `CLI_KEEP`, never saved) and `cliOpen`; `App.tsx` takes ⌘K (from anywhere, a shell included) and `:` (outside a text box) and mounts `CommandPop`, and binds its view switch into the runner with `bindNavigate`. The zoom kind is `cli`.

The page's workspace add/remove commands accept only home-backend repos,
as the workspace menu does. A remote-backend member is refused locally
with an explanation before the home workspace API is called; workspace
membership remains stored as paths the home backend can resolve.

## Gears

Gears (`components/Gear.tsx`, `components/Surface.tsx`, pure `ui/src/surface.ts`, browser `ui/src/share.ts`): every panel head, panel section, shells row (strip and panel) and the feed has a ⚙ whose menu (portal, `.menu.gear-pop`, built from `GearGroup[]`) holds zoom, layout and share. Zoom is per kind (`settings.zoom`, `ZOOM_KINDS`; a surface brought to the front has its own, `settings.frontZoom` through `useZoom(kind, front)`, `frontZoomOf` following the in-place zoom until one is set and `withFrontZoom` dropping it back there, css `zoom` on a section's `.section-main` (below its head), on `.panel-body` for a panel (never in the bench, whose panes sit outside the body's scroll), on `.feed-list` for the feed, never on an ancestor of an xterm); the shells' zoom is their font size by `ShellSpot` (`shellSpot(mode, dockless())`: `place`, `full`, `front`, or `window` for a lone window, handed to every `TermView` under a set through the `ShellSpotHere` context): `termFont` in place, `termFonts` elsewhere, each following `termFont` until set (`termFontIn`/`withTermFont`, `saveTermFont`). Layout is `SurfaceMode` (`normal`/`full`/`focus`, the shells' old modes lifted into `Surface.tsx` with `FocusBackdrop`, `FocusGrips`, `useLeaveOnEscape`; a section fills its panel through `.section-full`, a panel or the feed fills the window through `.surface-full`, the strip and the feed float through `.surface-focus`; a panel section's "in front" is its project's bench, and only a section in a window of its own keeps a mode of its own), the settings that already existed (`openIn` dock/tabs, `fileView`, `shell`), pop-out (`view=section&repo=&section=` renders `SectionSolo` inside `SectionWindow`, which forces the section open through `useSectionClosed` without writing the grove's folds, and is `dockless` and a lone window for `shellPlace`, with `PanelShells` under it; a shell pops out as `view=shell&term=` joining the held shell), and a panel's section order and hidden set (`settings.sectionOrder`/`sectionsHidden`, repaired by `sectionOrder`/`sectionsHidden`; `RepoPanel` renders `PanelSection` by key, the shells stay the footer; unhide lives on the panel gear). Every section goes through `Section` (its head is `.section-head` with the fold and the gear), and `ChangesSection` owns the commit box, its notes and `Unpushed`. Share is copy text (`surfaceText`, or a section's own `copy`; a shell's text through `GET /api/terms/text`, tmux's own `capture-pane` through `paneText`, the screen alone while `alternate_on` says a full-screen program is up since tmux draws that program on xterm's normal screen and each redraw leaves a stale frame in xterm's scrollback, with `shellCopyOf` falling back to the buffer through the `LIVE` terminal map for a plain pty or a server that does not answer inside `COPY_WAIT`), capture (`modern-screenshot`, loaded on first use, to the clipboard on a secure page else a png download named by `captureName`; blank for the preview's cross-origin iframe, so that is marked off) and paste (shell, search box, commit message; needs a secure page).

The views and columns with no layout to pick each carry `WidgetGear` (`Surface.tsx`): the repo tree and the board at the end of their first heading's branch line (zoom on each `.tree-list` and `.grid`, the heads unzoomed), the agents and the incubator at the end of `.agents-bar` (zoom on `.agents-body` and `.incubator-body`), the library and ports at the end of `.library-toolbar` (zoom on the framed page's root, since css zoom on the iframe sizes the frame too; capture is off, copy reads the frame's text). Their zoom kinds are `sidebar`, `board`, `agents`, `incubator` and `library` in `ZOOM_KINDS`. There is one settings icon, `GearIcon` in `Gear.tsx`, which every gear and the top bar's settings button draw.

The inbox has a gear of its own in its head (`useInboxGear` in `Inbox.tsx`), outside the zoomed part so it stays put: the inbox zoom (`ZOOM_KINDS` "inbox", css `zoom` on `.inbox-zoom` with the popover's width scaled to match through `--inbox-w`, which a size off its grip overrides, and `useFitPop` refitting on the zoom), the raw command's text size (`settings.inboxText`, `INBOX_TEXT` 9 to 18 px, a per-screen setting), wrap or one line (`inboxWrap`) and folded at first or always shown (`inboxFold`), then every shown backend's remembered rules as `forget` entries (the rule in words, where it applies under it, the backend when more than one has rules). The gear's menu is a portal, so the inbox's outside-click handler lets a pointerdown inside `.gear-pop` through. `GearEntry` gained `forget` (a kept thing with a ✕), `note` (a line of text) and a zoom's `what`, for an entry that sizes text rather than zooming.

Folding everything at once. The panel gear's "sections · every panel" group ends with "fold every section of <repo>" and "unfold every section of <repo>", which act on that panel's repo alone (`foldSections` in the store over pure `foldAllIn` and `unfoldAllIn`). The keys are `foldKeys` in `surface.ts`: the section order less the hidden ones, plus "shell", less any the bench holds. Unfolding writes an explicit empty list, since a repo with no entry means `DEFAULT_CLOSED`. The repo tree's and the board's `WidgetGear` take an extra "groups" group, `useGroupFolds`: "fold every group" and "unfold every group" over the groups shown (`foldGroups` in `grouping.ts`). Alt+click on a `GroupHead` does the same, Finder style. The tree and the board share `collapsed`, so folding in one folds the other. "close buttons" (`settings.panelClose`, default on, a per-screen setting, also in Settings under "repo panel") sits in the panel gear's layout group: off, it hides a docked panel's ✕, its strip tab's ×, the middle click on the tab and the close on `PanelWaiting`; a solo window keeps its own close.

## The project bench

The project bench (`components/Bench.tsx`, pure `ui/src/front.ts`): a panel in front is its project's bench, entered from the ⧉ on the panel head, on the preview, tasks and panel shells, the panel gear's "bring to the front", `bringTerm`, `bringTask` or `bringProject(repoId)`, and left by the bar's ⧉, Escape or the backdrop (`bringProject(null)`). Inside the bench those parts' own ⧉ (and a section gear's "take the bench") no longer leave it: each has its part take the whole bench or give it back, `solo` on the project `Front` (`files`, `app`, `shell` or `log`, a section mapped by `paneOf`; `withSolo`/`benchSolo`, the store's `soloBench`), which the frameless preview's float carries as ⤢ too; in a filled bench `frontForTab` hands the room to the shells and `frontForTask` to the log, another project's bench starts whole, and Escape steps back one level, the part first, then the bench. `RepoPanel` adds `.panel-bench` and `BenchBar` (the projects `benchProjects` lists: open panels in dock order, then anything with a shell or a task running, each with `▶`/`▸_` counts; a click is `bringProject` for that one), and the css places the parts in one fixed box: the panel body as the column on the left, the preview section and the tasks' `.task-front` absolutely out of that column's scroll (nothing between them and the panel may be positioned, and the body's zoom is off), the shells under the preview and the log beside them. `BenchSeams` (in `Bench.tsx`) lays three draggable edges straight on the panel over those seams: the rail's width (`benchRail`, px, null for the stylesheet's own), the shells' share of the height under the app (`benchDock`) and the log's share of that row (`benchSplit`), per-browser settings repaired by `benchRailOf`/`shareOf` and set from the pointer by the pure `railAt`/`dockAt`/`splitAt` in `front.ts` (tested); the panel carries them as `--bench-rail-user`, `--bench-dock-f` and `--bench-split-user`, which the bench's own variables read, so the no-preview and no-log rules still win, and a seam hides wherever its edge is not there. With an app showing, the bench's preview has no frame of its own (no head, port or path bars, the page edge to edge) and `.preview-float` puts reload, the port picker and open-in-a-tab over its bottom right corner. They are placed rather than moved in the tree, since reparenting would reload the preview's iframe and unmount a `TermView`. `benchHolds` holds changes, tasks, preview and shell open through `useSectionClosed` without writing `closedSections`, so leaving the bench leaves the folds as they were (a preview that was folded is unmounted again). A part filling the bench is `data-pane` on the panel, the others `visibility: hidden` so the iframe keeps its page and an xterm its size; the bar's `Seg` picks it too (all, changes, app, shell, log), and below `PHONE` there is always one (no "all", in the bar's second row), the app or else the shell when none is picked.

## The guided panel

The guided panel (`docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-29-guided-panel-design.md`, plan in `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-29-guided-panel.md`): the per-browser `level` setting (`intermediate` or `advanced`, with `onboarded` for the tour) picks what `RepoPanel` shows. `levelOf` in `settings.ts` gives a browser that saved settings before levels existed `advanced` and no tour; a fresh browser gets `intermediate`. At intermediate `RepoPanel` renders `GuidedPanel` (`components/Guided.tsx`: the status line from the pure `plainStatus`, Run my app / Stop on the dev task, Save my work, show more) in place of the head chips and sections, and "show more" flips that one panel to the advanced body until it closes, with "show less" to go back; `PanelShells` is the footer in both, so the shell never remounts, and `.guided-panel` in the css lets it fill the panel. The panel gear and Settings both switch the level, and Settings has "show the tour again". `Tour.tsx` is the three coach marks (shell, run, save; steps from the pure `tourStep`), shown in the active panel only, never in a dockless window, and it waits a few seconds for a target that mounts late. A panel opens its own shell: a store subscription beside `panelsStarted` (`panelsShelled`, once per panel open) adds a panel tab when `needsPanelShell` in `term.ts` says the repo has no panel tab and the backend holds no panel shell for it (a hidden one counts), only once the repo is in the store (its backend's shell list lands in the same set) and its backend is online, never for a forge, host or errored repo, and without focusing the panel; at intermediate the tab carries `start: "agent"`, which `socketUrl` sends as `start=agent` (with the tab's `harness=`/`profile=` launch pick) on a socket that starts a shell, and the server types `agentLine` for the shell route's settings in after a beat, the tab's `prompt` (sent as `prompt=`, capped at `PROMPT_MAX`) quoted after it as Claude's first message (`typeAgent`; never on a join or `attach=1`; `startServer({ agentLine })` swaps the line for tests, `server/start.test.ts`). `GET /api/terms/agent?term=` answers `{agent}` on demand, `agentIn` over `paneInfo` on tmux, and always null on a plain pty, where nothing can say whether what canopy started is still running; null too for a shell not held (a panel asks as its tab appears, before its socket starts the shell, and every caller reads a failure as none anyway), 400 for a task. `AgentButtons.tsx` puts ▶/■ (the dev task), 🐞 (the debug prompt with the dev task's last 40 log lines) and ✓ (the save prompt) on the tab row of the showing shell, and "set up run" when the repo has no dev task; `askAgent` pastes into the first of `agentCandidates` that the agent route says runs either harness, else waits for a shell canopy just started with its agent (`pendingAgent`), else opens a panel shell with the agent and the prompt as its first message; one ask per repo at a time. Pasting goes through `typeInto` in `ui/src/liveTerms.ts`, which also holds `LIVE`, the xterm behind every mounted shell view (moved out of `TermDock.tsx`): a paste and the focus, never Enter, since Claude may be on a question of its own where Enter picks an answer. The prompts, `devState`, `canSave` and `agentCandidates` are pure in `ui/src/guided.ts`, tested.

## Harnesses and routing

Harnesses and routing (`docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-30-agents-design.md`, phase 1): canopy starts Claude Code or Codex. `core/harness.ts` (browser-safe, pure, tested) is one `HarnessInfo` per harness (`HARNESS`: binary, glyph, the model list (claude's closed, codex's an open hint list checked by `MODEL_RE` and never a claude alias, `takesModel`), `efforts` (`ultra` codex's alone, `takesEffort`), `modelArgs`/`effortArgs` (`--model`/`--effort` vs `-m`/`-c model_reasoning_effort=`), `permissionArgs` (yolo `--dangerously-skip-permissions` vs `--dangerously-bypass-approvals-and-sandbox`; codex ask spelled out as `-a on-request -s workspace-write`), `always` (`--no-daemon` on every codex line, so the process and env stay in the pane), `envArgs` (codex filters its commands' env, so an `AgentEnv` like a shell's `TAILCHAN_AS` rides as `-c shell_environment_policy.set.K="v"`; later phases add `CANOPY_*` through the server's `shellEnv`), `resume` and `continue`); `agentArgs`/`agentArgv`/`resumeArgv`/`continueArgv` build from it, and the shell lines are `agentLine` (openers), `resumeLine` (sessions) and `continueLine` (keep). `AgentSettings` has `harness` (an entry without one is claude), `model: string`. `core/route.ts` (browser-safe, pure, tested): roles `AGENT_ROLES` (`shell` every interactive start, `chat`, `job`, `flow`, `suggest`, `coordinate`), a pick is `{profile}` or settings, `RepoAgent` is `{all?, roles?}`, config keeps `agents` (by path; an old plain-settings entry reads as `{all}`), `profiles` (`default` always there, the builtin when unset) and `agentRoles`; `resolveAgent(routes, path, role, explicit?)` walks explicit (a `LaunchPick`: `{profile}` or `{harness}`, which takes the first route layer of that harness, else the first profile of it, else its defaults), repo × role, repo, role, default, builtin, returning `{settings, from, profile?, skipped?}`; a missing profile or a harness the role cannot run falls through into `skipped`, and `ROLE_HARNESSES` is the seam: since phase 2 every role takes both harnesses (the runner drives either, `suggest` runs either CLI) but `coordinate`, which is Claude Code's alone and runs at effort `low` unless the settings it resolves to set one (`forCoordinate`), and a later harness some role cannot run yet goes in there. `pickRefusal`/`repoAgentRefusal` refuse settings of their own that a role cannot run on write (a profile pick is let through and falls through at resolve), `normalizeRoutes` also reads an older backend's plain map, `launchPick` reads `profile=`/`harness=`; a profile name (`isProfileName`) is never one `Object.prototype` has (`constructor`), and every lookup by name is own-only (`profileOf`/`hasProfile`, server and UI), so no pick resolves through the prototype. Routes: `GET /api/agents` is `AgentRoutes` `{profiles, roles, repos}` (also the `agents` event), `POST /api/agents/profile {name, settings|null}` (null deletes; `default` resets), `POST /api/agents/role {role, pick|null}`, `POST /api/repos/agent?id=` takes a `RepoAgent` (a plain-settings body from an older page sets the whole-repo pick alone through `setRepoAll`, the builtin defaults its reset, the repo's per-role picks kept either way; and the page sends a backend older than routing, one whose `Backend` has no `harnesses` (`hasRouting` in `ui/src/agents.ts`), only `flatAgent`, the whole-repo pick's own settings, since that backend reads any other body as its defaults and deletes the entry, while the sheet and the routing tab offer it no profiles or roles), `GET /api/agents/resolve?id=` is an `AgentTable`. `Backend.harnesses` is what this process finds (`availableHarnesses()`: PATH plus the installers' dirs, `hasBinary`) together with what the user's login interactive shell finds, the shell a canopy shell and the agent opener type into (`LoginHarnesses` in openers: `probeLoginShell` runs `$SHELL -l -i -c probeScript()`, marker lines read by `parseProbe`, `PROBE_TIMEOUT` 10s, asked at start, again in the background once `PROBE_EVERY` (10 min) old, and before refusing a start when the answer is `PROBE_AGAIN` (30s) old; `harnessesFrom` offers every harness while the shell has not answered or could not, with one warning, so a failed probe never refuses; off under `bun test`, and `startServer({ harnesses })` fixes the list), and a start on a missing one is refused in words (`missingHarness`: "codex is not installed on mini", through `harnessRefusal`, never for a repo with a `host`, whose agent runs there over ssh, and the UI's `startHarnesses` offers every harness for such a repo's shells): the socket's `start=agent` (or an old page's `start=claude`) resolves the shell role with the launch pick before the upgrade and closes 1011 with that reason before any shell starts, resume and restore answer 400, and the backend's own agent/herdr openers check too. `agentIn(command, title, argvs?)` reads a bun-installed codex (pane command `node`) off the argv under the pane: `PANE_FORMAT` carries `pane_pid` and `core/procs.ts` (`paneAgent`, `processTree` over `/proc` or `ps`, pure `parseStatPpid`/`parseCmdline`/`parsePs`/`descendants`, tested) walks it only when tmux's words name nothing and the pane is not at a shell. `codexSessions` reads `$CODEX_HOME/sessions/YYYY/MM/DD/rollout-*.jsonl` for the last `CODEX_DAYS` (30) by the local calendar (`codexDayDirs`), the first line through `parseCodexMeta` (cwd must equal the repo path; a `subagent` source is skipped) memoized by mtime and size (a read that failed, EACCES or EMFILE, is not, so the session is back once readable; `CODEX_READS` (16) files at a time through `mapPool`), the prompt through `parseCodexHead` (a `user_message` event or a user item not starting `<`) only for the repo's own; `hasAgentSession` checks either. UI: `ui/src/agents.ts` (pure, tested: `pickValue`/`pickOf`/`pickLine`, `effectiveRows` with flags, `fromWord`, `missingProfiles`, `profileUses`, `withPick`, `slotHarnesses`, `overrideRows`, `harnessesOf` with a stable claude-only fallback), `components/AgentForm.tsx` (`AgentSettingsForm` with the harness first, greyed where the backend lacks it or the slot cannot run it; `PickEditor`; `EffectiveTable`), `components/AgentsView.tsx` (the `agents` view in `ViewNav`, its routing tab: profiles, roles, repo overrides, effective, with its own backend picker on a multi-backend board), the `{kind:"agent"}` sheet is `RepoAgentSheet` (that repo's override row with the effective column), `RepoMenu`'s "with an agent" has "new claude shell"/"new codex shell" and a profile row (`openTerm(repoId, place, "agent", prompt, pick)`, the pick riding on `TermTab.harness`/`profile`), `NewShell.tsx` is the panel shells' `+` (click a plain shell; right click or a long press offers agent shells), `Shells.tsx` lists both harnesses' conversations with a glyph and resumes by harness, `KeptShells` offers "restore + continue" for either; tests in `harness.test.ts`, `route.test.ts`, `procs.test.ts`, `server/agents.test.ts`, `ui/src/agents.test.ts`.

## Codex headless

Codex headless (the agents spec, phase 2 and amendment 2): chats, jobs, workflow steps and the commit message run on whichever harness the repo's route names (the runner, flow and suggest bullets above say how). In the UI the run's own harness names it: `ui/src/runs.ts` (pure, tested: `harnessOf` (a run without one was Claude's), `AGENT_NAME`, `agentWord`, `resultLine`, the tokens where there are some else the cost, `tokenTitle`, `tokenCount`) feeds `RunChip`, the `RunSheet` console's heading, prompts, composer and footer, and the feed's lines; `FlowSheet` shows a step's `agent:` profile in the plan and on the step strip; `RepoMenu`'s chat and ask items carry the glyph of their resolved harness and are greyed where the backend lacks it. Run-facing copy says "the agent" or the harness's name, never Claude alone.

## Moving a session

Moving a session (`docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-10-session-move-design.md`): the `move to <backend>` button in a shell's tab row carries its running Claude or Codex conversation and the repo's uncommitted work to the same repo on another backend. The browser drives it, one call to one backend per step (`runMove` in `ui/src/move.ts`: check, stop, transcript, load, clean; pure, tested with fake deps), and carries the transcript between the two, so neither machine needs a route to the other beyond the git peering it already has. The source answers `/api/terms/move/plan` (GET: the session, branch, head, transcript size, last prompt and the changed files), `/move/out` (POST: stop the agent and snapshot), `/move/transcript` (GET), `/move/finish` (POST) and `/move/undo` (POST). The target answers `/api/repos/movecheck` and `/api/repos/movein`, since `/api/repos/<action>` takes one lowercase word. All of it works on local repos under the launch root in plain tmux shells (not tasks or the ranger), on a branch, between backends that are each other's peers. An incubator seed never moves: `plan`, `out`, `movecheck` and `movein` answer 400 with `SEED_AGENT_REFUSAL`, as resume does, since a seed's agents start through the incubator alone (`undo` has nothing to resume, since `out` never leaves a record); the page looks the target up by backend name, which is the peer name.

`src/core/move.ts` holds the pure parts. `rewriteTranscript` parses every line as JSON (a cut-off line is a 400), then moves only the `cwd` fields, the top-level one Claude writes and the one in Codex's `payload`, from the source repo's path to the target's, for the repo itself and anything under it. A line is edited in place when that gives exactly the record with its own `cwd` moved, so the rest stays byte for byte; otherwise the record is written out again. Message text and tool input keep the paths they had. `placeTranscript` files the result where the target's harness looks (Claude under `projects/<folder of the repo's path>/<session>.jsonl`; Codex by its own `YYYY/MM/DD/rollout-...-<session>.jsonl` name, checked against the session id) and writes through a temp file and a rename. The prefix rule: an earlier copy there is replaced only when it equals the incoming text or is a line-for-line prefix of it (the same conversation, moved here before); any other earlier copy is a split and gets a 409. `transcriptConflict` asks the same question without writing, which is how `movein` refuses before it lands any work. The body cap is `MOVE_MAX`, 64 MB, enforced at the plan, the check, the content-length and the file. `endAgent` quits the agent as a person would: Escape (stops a turn), Ctrl-C (clears a half-typed draft, which one Escape leaves in place in both harnesses, so the quit command is never appended to it and sent as a prompt; Codex at an empty prompt quits on it alone), the quit command (`/exit` for Claude, `/quit` for Codex), then Enter as a separate key send, since Codex swallows an Enter sent with the text. It polls the pane for up to 20 s and answers whether the pane is back at its shell. The pane driver answers `unread` when tmux will not read the pane, unless canopy is ending or has ended that shell (then no agent is left in it); a first read like that refuses the stop before any key with a 409, and one while it waits counts as not yet.

The source keeps a record per shell in `state.moving`, a stage union, each stage carrying the move id. The sheet mints that id (32 hex digits) once per try and passes it as `move` in the query of `move/out`, which keeps it on the record and echoes it in `MoveOut`; `move/transcript`, `move/finish` and `move/undo` must name it again and answer 409 when they name another move (`ownMove`), so a second window, whose out was refused as already moving, can never finish or undo the first window's move. The page mints the id rather than the source because the source's answer can be lost after the agent quit (a snapshot that fails, a tunnel that drops), and the undo still needs it then. `stopping` is held before the stop's first await in `move/out`, so a second out is refused while the first waits on the agent, and dropped if the agent never stops. A record past `stopping` whose shell runs an agent again gives way to a new out: no page can name it any more (a reload or a closed tab lost its id, and someone started the agent by hand), and undo would forget it anyway; any other record answers 409 already moving. `stopped` is set once the agent is gone, before the snapshot, so a snapshot that fails still leaves the undo something to resume. `saved` is set when the snapshot is in hand: `snapshotWip` ran under the peer lock, the `refs/wip/<branch>` it wrote sits right on the HEAD read, and that commit is the move's `wip` (null for a clean tree). Out moves on to `stopped` and to `saved` only while the record in the map is still the very object it set; otherwise the shell ended or the move was undone under it, and out answers 409 "the move was called off" (a snapshot already written stays in `refs/wip`, and nothing resets). `endTerm`, a shell exiting and a session gone from tmux all drop the shell's record, so none outlives its shell. `move/transcript` and `move/finish` refuse until the stage is `saved`, and the transcript route also checks the session id. Plan and out refuse with a 409 before anything stops while an agent runs in another shell at the same repo (`otherAgentAt`, task and ranger sessions aside), whose tree the snapshot and the reset would pull from under it, and when the tree has changes `resetBlocker` says finish could not reset; a session the page names that the repo does not file here is a 404, never the newest. `move/finish` answers 409 when an agent runs in the pane again (started by hand), since ending the shell would end that conversation, or, with a tree to reset, in another shell at the repo, or when tmux will not read the pane; it asks again under the peer lock, along with whether the record is still the move's own, before it calls `resetTree`, ends the shell and drops the record. `move/undo` with no record answers `resumed: false`; otherwise it drops the record and, if the pane has no agent, types the resume line into the same shell (`resumeLine` with the repo's agent profile); an agent already back in the pane is left alone, a pane tmux will not read is a 502 that keeps the record, and a record still `stopping` answers `resumed: false`.

`resetTree` and `treeIs` live in `src/core/peersync.ts`. `treeIs` builds the working copy's tree in scratch (untracked files in, ignored ones out) and compares it to a commit's, writing nothing. `resetTree` runs under the peer lock with the agent already stopped. It refuses when HEAD is not the snapshot's parent, then on whatever `resetBlocker` names: a merge or rebase in progress, any file marked assume-unchanged or skip-worktree (they hide edits from status yet `reset --hard` would overwrite them), and unmerged entries in the index. Plan and out ask `resetBlocker` too while the tree has changes, so a move never strands a session on the target with a source it would then refuse to reset. It then keeps the snapshot under `refs/canopy-moved/<session>`, a namespace peer sync neither fetches nor prunes, and returns `clean` if the tree is already clean. Otherwise it refuses unless `treeIs(wip)` holds, so an edit made after the snapshot is never thrown away. The snapshot is the working copy only, so a staged version that differs from the file on disk is kept too (`keepIndex`): when the index's tree (`git write-tree`) is not HEAD's, it is wrapped in a commit with the snapshot as parent, under canopy's own identity from the environment, at `refs/canopy-moved-index/<session>`. A commit rather than a bare tree ref, so `git log` and `git show` read it like any other and its parent names the snapshot it belongs to; a separate namespace, since a ref under `refs/canopy-moved/<session>/` would clash with the snapshot's own ref. An index with unmerged entries refuses the reset. Then it runs `git clean -f -d` before `git reset --hard` with `submodule.recurse=false`. The clean goes first because it must see the ignore rules the snapshot used; an uncommitted `.gitignore` rule would otherwise hide files from it, and the reset can restore an older `.gitignore`.

`movecheck` answers 409 when a run or workflow is active on the repo, and otherwise `{ ok, reason?, path, existing }`. It refuses with `ok: false`, without landing any of the source's work, when peer sync is in dry mode, the harness is missing, the source cannot be fetched now (a fetch made for the check, since the pass keeps refs from the last one), or the branch is not where the move says; `existing` says whether the target already holds a copy of the session. `movein` takes multipart `meta` and `transcript` (the page leaves the move id out of `meta`; only the source checks it). The term id in `meta` is the idempotency key: if that shell already runs on the repo, `movein` answers with it and touches nothing, and a load of the same term that arrives while the first is still at work (`state.moveLoads`) waits for that one and answers the same. A term id any other shell holds, here or kept from a machine that went down, is a 409 before anything else. Everything it can refuse comes before the work lands (the shell name, harness, dry mode, active runs, `transcriptConflict`, the fetch and branch checks). The work goes first and the transcript second: when the tree already equals the named snapshot (`treeIs`), a retry after a lost answer skips the fetch and the take and goes on to place the transcript. The take must land in the tree; if it landed on a branch the move refuses. Last, `resumeTerm` starts the shell on the target with the resume line.

The sheet is `MoveSheet` in `ui/src/components/MoveSheet.tsx`. It keeps the tab, harness and session it opened on (`Moving`), since the stop quits the agent and the tab row may come to show another shell. It runs plan and check on open and shows the prompt, the changed files (each named as the snapshot carries it: new, modified, deleted, or renamed from its old path, by `moveFileList` in `src/core/move.ts`) and the count of gitignored entries that stay. A failed run reports the step, and whether the source was stopped and whether the target loaded. Out failing with a 4xx means nothing was touched (`runMove` counts it as not stopped), apart from the 409 for an agent that did not quit within 20 s, where the quit keys went in but the record is already gone, while a 5xx or no answer at all may have quit the agent. A stopped source with no load offers "resume here" (`move/undo`, `offersResume`), and closing a sheet in that state runs the undo too (`closeUndoes`), so no record is left to refuse the next move; a sheet that went away mid-move sends the same undo. The target's shell name is minted once per try, and a load that fails with no HTTP status (`statusOf`: a network error, or a tunnel's error page that is not JSON) is tried once more under that name. Still unanswered, the load is unknown: the target may already run the session, so the sheet says so, offers "check again" (the load once more under the same name; an answer with a status turns it into a plain failure) and "resume here anyway", and closing it does not undo. A failed clean (the target already runs the session) offers "clean source" and keeps the sheet up. `landOnce` wraps the landing of the loaded tab (`landMove` in the store) so the success path, the clean retry, closing and the sheet unmounting land it exactly once.

Move cleanup also refuses ignored content that restoring HEAD would replace:
a formerly tracked file now removed from the index, an ignored file blocking
a tracked directory, or ignored children blocking a tracked file. This check
runs in `resetBlocker` before the agent stops and again before cleanup, since
neither snapshot contains those bytes. Transcript placement treats only
`ENOENT` as a missing conversation; other read failures refuse the load
before any work lands, rather than replacing an unreadable earlier copy.
Path collision checks honor `core.ignorecase`, so an ignored case variant
of a tracked path receives the same protection on a case-insensitive tree.

## Layout and motion

Popup sizes: every sheet and every `.settings-pop` popover ends in a `PopGrip` (`ui/src/components/PopGrip.tsx`), a corner grip with the math in `ui/src/pop.ts` (pure, tested). Each popup names its `PopAnchor`, the edges that hold as it grows: `CENTERED` for the sheets, which grow both ways and so twice the drag, `HANGS_RIGHT` for a popover off its chip's right edge (the grip at bottom left, growing left) and `HANGS_LEFT` for the pick menus and the folders. `popRoom` is the most it can take, `gap` clear of the window (24 for a sheet, its veil's padding, 8 for a popover), and `gripSize` turns a drag into a size held to that room and to `POP_MIN`. The grip writes `--pop-w`/`--pop-h` and `data-sized` straight onto its parent while dragging and saves on release into `settings.popSizes`, keyed by popup (`sheet <kind>`, `pick <label>`, `inbox`, …) and kept per screen with the other sizes; on load and on every window resize the saved size is painted held to the room there is then, and a double-click drops it. The `[data-sized]` rules sit last in `styles.css` so they win over each popup's own width and height, give a sheet's body the height, and make a sized popover scroll; `.sheet-back`'s single `minmax(0, 1fr)` cell is what lets a sized sheet's `max-width`/`max-height: 100%` hold it inside the veil. A popover that scrolls carries the grip along with its content, so the grip is translated back down by `scrollTop`. Under 761px the popups are bottom sheets: the grip is hidden and no size applies. Menus and the command line have no grip.

Each popup also has a gear (`PopGear`, its lines from `usePopEntries`): its zoom, kept per popup in `settings.popZooms` (`popZoomsOf`, a zoom of 1 is no entry), and its size, "fill the window" (saves `popRoom`) or "its own size" (drops the entry). A sheet's sits in its head just before the close: `RunSheet` hands its parts the popup through the `PopHere` context, and the move sheet and the file viewer name theirs outright. A popover has no common head, so its grip puts the gear in its top corner (`PopGrip gear`), and its first row keeps 34px clear on the right; the inbox's own gear takes the size lines in place of a second gear. The grip paints the zoom as `--pop-zoom` and `data-zoomed`, and the zoom goes on every child but the head and the controls, so the gear stays put as it steps. A popover's outside-click close goes through `leavesPop`, which lets a click in a gear's menu (a portal, `.gear-pop`) through, and `.gear-pop` sits at z-index 50, over the sheets' veil.

Palettes: `settings.palette` (`PALETTES` in `ui/src/settings.ts`) sets `data-palette` on `<html>` beside `data-theme`, in a layout effect so a saved one never paints forest first, and `paintThemeColor` in `App.tsx` re-reads `--bark1` into index.html's theme-color metas. Each palette is a `[data-palette]` block in `styles.css` setting the same colors as `--pal-*` `light-dark()` pairs (forest's block is also `:root`), and one block on `:root, [data-palette]` maps them onto the tokens the rules paint with (`--ink: var(--pal-ink)`); the blocks match any element, which is how a swatch in Settings previews its palette. `settings.moreContrast` sets `data-contrast="more"` on `<html>`, and a block on `:root[data-contrast="more"], [data-contrast="more"] [data-palette]` maps the same colors through `color-mix(in srgb, …)` instead, surfaces a share of the way toward the scheme's paper (`--to-paper`, white or black) and text, accents and lines toward its ink (`--to-ink`), so it works over any palette, swatches included; under it a resolved token reads back as `color(srgb …)` rather than `rgb()`, which xterm and the theme-color metas take. `settings.test.ts` holds every block to forest's color list, the two mapping blocks to that list, and the text to floors against the worst of `--bark0`, `--bark1`, `--bark2` and `--float` on each side (ink 9:1, ink-dim 6:1, ink-faint and every accent 4.5:1; with more contrast ink 14:1, ink-dim 10:1, ink-faint and every accent 7:1, `--hair2` and `--float-edge` 3:1, computed from the block's own shares); a published theme's color that falls short is darkened or lightened in its own hue until it clears, so those values differ from upstream. The non-color tokens (radii, shadows, `--trunk`, and the washes `--wash`, `--wash-strong` and `--well`, tints of `--ink` over transparent) stay on `:root`, shared. The rules are one box deep (the header comment of `styles.css`): only a card, a floating layer (popover, menu, sheet, focused shell, bench), a text field and `.mini.strong` (with the guided panel's primary) have an edge or a fill of their own, and nothing with an edge sits inside another. Buttons, segmented choices, tabs and filter chips are words that take a wash under the pointer or when chosen (a view, a dock tab or a shell tab is underlined instead); facts on a card or a row (branch, machine, tip, peer, stash, run, harness, capability, stage) are coloured text; a callout (`.note`, `.panel-hint`, `.ask`, `.outcome`, `.gate`, a run note) hangs off a left rail in its colour; code and output (`.diff`, a commit or session drill, a step's output, a job) sit in a well of `--bark0` with no edge. The Library frame follows the same rules through `lib/devhub/theme.css`'s last block, and `bridge.js` hands it moss, lichen and rust for its green, amber and red. The shells repaint on a palette or contrast change through the same probe as a theme change, and the Library frame's `bridge.js` copies the page's tokens, unresolved, into `theme.css`'s names whenever `data-theme`, `data-palette` or `data-contrast` changes. Layout and motion: the git view has one header, `TopBar` with the `ViewNav` (git, library, ports) beside the wordmark; the library and ports views keep `.app-nav` as theirs. `ui/src/media.ts`'s `useMedia` follows the two widths the stylesheet turns at: `PHONE` (760px) gives `TopBar` its three-row phone layout (views and a few buttons, the filter box, then a row that scrolls sideways), and below `NARROW` (1100px) the tree is a drawer (`drawerOpen` in the store, never saved, closed by `openRepo`, Escape or the backdrop) instead of a column. On a phone the popovers (`.settings-pop`) are bottom sheets, and so is a `Gear` given `sheet` (the workspace gear, `.gear-sheet`, which drops the inline position it is hung by elsewhere; above PHONE that gear is 288px wide through `width`, since its "plan, then build in workspace…" line is cut short at the usual 256, and `menuSpot` places a menu of any width), and the dock sits over the cards inside `.body`, not over the bars. `ui/src/live.ts` is a beat per applied event, outside the store so it wakes no selector; the top bar's `LiveDot` flashes on it and shows the home stream's state. Cards rise in staggered by `--i` (their place in the group) and pulse on a `repo` event; every animation sits under `prefers-reduced-motion: no-preference`, and none animates a transform on an element whose descendants may be `position: fixed` for longer than its entrance.

The dock is columns of tabbed cells (pure `ui/src/grid.ts`, tested). A `DockLayout` is a list of `columns`, each an `id`, a px `width` and its `cells` top to bottom; a cell is an `id`, its `panels` (its tabs, in order), the `active` one showing and a `share` of the column's height, and a column's shares sum to 1. Ids are `c` and a number, one past the highest in use (`newId`). Every operation is pure, and every one but `regroup` gives back the layout itself when it changes nothing, so an action that moves nothing saves nothing (`regroup` always builds a fresh layout with new ids, which is why `arrangeDock` calls it only when the arrangement changes, and `stepPanel` gives null rather than the layout): `place` makes a layout hold exactly the open panels (closed ones leave, a cell whose showing tab left shows the one `nextActive` picks, and each new panel becomes a column of its own side by side or a tab of the active cell as tabs), `moveTo` takes a panel into a cell's middle as a tab or beside it as a new cell above or below or a new column left or right, `moveWithin` makes it a tab of a cell at an index, `moveColumn` and `moveCell` move a whole column along the row or a cell up or down its column, `regroup` makes every panel a column of its own or puts them all in one cell, `stepPanel` moves a panel one place left or right (below), `resizeColumn` sizes a column and `resizeSeam` the boundary between two cells (no cell under 0.1 of its column, the others keeping their shares; `seamRange` gives a seam's two limits and its midpoint), and `rename` swaps one panel id for another in place, which is how `switchCheckout` keeps a panel's place. `normalizeLayout` repairs a layout read back from storage: entries of the wrong shape go, a panel saved twice keeps its first place, widths and shares become finite and positive with no share under 0.1 (`floored`), and an id that is not `c` and one to six digits (`SAFE_ID`), or that repeats, is issued again, since ids become CSS custom property names.

`gridOf` turns the layout into one CSS grid. Each column is two tracks, a 6px seam and the column itself; the rows are the union of every column's cell boundaries, in `fr`, with boundaries closer than 0.001 merged into one line (`MERGE`), so thirds in one column against halves in another leave no sliver of a row. A cell's `grid-area` spans the rows its share covers (a column's last cell reaches the bottom line), and the plan lists each panel's area, its cell and whether it is the cell's showing tab, a strip for every cell of more than one panel, a column seam per column, and a row seam over every cell but a column's first. `gridTemplate` writes each column track as `var(--col-w-<id>, <width>px)` and sets every variable from the layout on each render, so a seam's live write is replaced by the next render rather than outliving a width change; with `fill` (a lone column under the carousel) the column takes the whole dock and its seam no room. `rowsTemplate` makes the rows `minmax(0, …)`, so the grid fits the dock's height and scrolls only sideways. A column id becomes part of a property name only through `cssId` (`columnVar`): every character outside `[A-Za-z0-9_-]` turns into `_` and an FNV hash of the whole id follows (`--col-w-c1-11s3ljt`), so two ids that differ only in those characters never share a name.

The dock never moves a panel's element (`Dock` in `components/Dock.tsx`), since a moved element reloads a preview's iframe and a remount drops a shell. `.dock.grid` holds the panels first, the same keyed children of the same element whatever the layout, sorted by id (`stableOrder` in `ui/src/dock.ts`) and placed by `grid-area` (`placements`); a panel that is not its cell's showing tab is `hidden`, and one the plan does not hold takes no room rather than auto-placing. The strips and seams come after the panels as free elements on the same grid, so their churn never moves one: a `CellStrip` over the top of each cell of tabs (`.cell-strip`, whose tabs are handles too; the cell's panels are padded by `--strip-h` under it), a `.seam-col` down each column's left edge (a `Resizer` that writes `--col-w-<id>` live and calls `resizeColumn` on release, with `follow` on, so once the dock is at its widest and scrolls, the drag moves the dock's `scrollLeft` to keep the seam under the pointer and a cancel puts the scroll back; when overflow is hidden on the right, the part of a shrink the scroll cannot take back widens the column's left neighbour instead (`neighbour`, `lentLeft`), so the tracks' total holds, the seam stays under the pointer and the column's far edge stays put, and the release saves both widths in one layout (`resizeColumns`); the first column's seam has no neighbour, so the moment its shrink outruns the scroll it pulls the whole hidden overflow in at once (`trailing`, `pulledIn`), from the last column first, which is the part nobody sees, then off the dragged column down to 240, and the dock narrows with the seam under the pointer, the release saving both widths; a neighbour at the most a column may take lends no more, so that seam lags as before; its arrows step it, Home and End take it to 240 and to the most that fits now (`keyedWidth`), and a key at its limit, or a double-click on a column already at 440, saves nothing), and a `.seam-row` along the top of every cell but a column's first (`RowSeam`, which lays the draft plan straight onto the dock between renders with `paintGrid`, so every area follows the renumbered row lines, and calls `resizeSeam` on release; its arrows move it, Home and End take it to its limits, a double-click splits the two cells evenly, and `aria-valuemin` and `aria-valuemax` are its real limits from `seamRange`; a click that moved nothing or a key at the seam's limit saves nothing, and `pointercancel` paints the layout back). `.dock-corner`, the carousel switch, spans the grid's first row with `justify-self: end` and is not sticky: held to the visible edge it sat on the × of whichever head the edge cut through, so it scrolls with the last column, in the room every head and strip keeps for it, and every panel gear's "carousel" stands in once it has scrolled away. The drag layer renders last. `.dock.grid` and the corner are the only positioned boxes there. Below `PHONE` there is no grid: `placements` gives each panel a CSS `order` in the flat dock, in the layout's order, every panel side by side in the swipe row a phone always had, or in tabs mode (`.dock.phone-tabs`) one strip of every open panel over the one showing, as before phase B. The store's `movePanel` moves a panel one place left or right, from Alt+Shift+Left/Right on a focused head or tab and from the gear's "move left" and "move right". It keeps phase A's flat index: the panel at index `to` of `panels` is where this one heads, and `stepPanel` in grid.ts maps that onto the grid, a tab among its cell's tabs moving within the cell (`moveWithin`, the showing tab kept), a lone column moving as a column (`moveColumn`), a tab heading out of its cell of tabs going no further than the cell's edge (its sibling tabs never move with it, even in a stack), and a cell of one panel in a stack moving up or down its column (`moveCell`). `stepPanel` gives null for a move that changes nothing, and the gear runs it dry, so "move left" and "move right" are greyed out wherever they would do nothing. `moveWithin` is the grid's own op for joining a cell at an index and does not replace it. Dragging a panel's name or glyph, or its strip tab, is a pointer drag (`startPanelDrag`, on the grid only): past `PAN_SLOP` a `.drag-shield` covers the grid so a preview's iframe never takes the pointer, `dropTarget` turns the panel cell under the pointer (`elementsFromPoint`, `.panel[data-cell]`) and `dropZone` into a drop that a `.drop-preview` outlines (`zoneRect`, cut by `clipBox` to the part of the window the dock shows, so a cell scrolled partly out never draws it over the cards; the strip's mark is cut the same way), and letting go calls the store's `dropPanel`. The dropped panel comes forward, and a panel's reveal effect also runs when the forward panel's grid area changes, so a drop of the panel already forward still scrolls it into sight. Over a cell's tab strip (`.cell-strip[data-cell]`, checked before the cell under it) the edges and the middle do not apply: `stripDrop` takes the pointer's x and where each tab is drawn to the gap before the first of the cell's other tabs whose middle is at or right of it (after the last otherwise), a `.drop-preview.drop-mark` bar marks that gap, and letting go calls the store's `dropTab`, which is `moveWithin` at that index. So a tab dragged along its own strip reorders, one from elsewhere joins the cell there, and a drop on a strip never splits; back at its own place, showing or not, is no drop and saves nothing. Any drop that changes nothing likewise moves and saves nothing; Escape before letting go, a cancelled pointer, a lost capture, a blur, a context menu, a move with no button held (a release the page never heard) or the dock leaving its grid moves and saves nothing (the phases are `dragStep` in `ui/src/drag.ts`). So does a layout changed from outside the drag (a window taking up another screen class's arrangement, a scan pruning a panel), since the drag's drop cell, like a seam's column and index, is an id the new layout may give to another cell: a store subscription in `Dock.tsx` takes a live drag down as Escape does and cancels every seam drag in progress (`holdSeam`), a row seam painting back the layout it was pressed on and a column seam putting its custom property back. Each gesture lets go of its hook before its own commit, and a seam unmounted mid-drag (its cell closing, the window narrowing past `PHONE`) ends its drag and lets go of it on unmount, so a later layout change never replays a stale paint or width. Escape mid-drag takes the layer down but keeps listening, and the release that follows is swallowed rather than taken as a click; under the slop it ends the press outright. A finger starts a drag by resting on a handle for `TOUCH_HOLD` (350ms, `dragStep`'s `hold` input; one that moves past the slop sooner is scrolling, and the press ends), with a short buzz where `navigator.vibrate` exists. Once a finger holds a panel, `keepStill`, a non-passive `touchmove` listener on each handle (React's are passive, and only a listener there from the touch's start can cancel its moves), keeps the row from scrolling under it, and the long press's context menu is cancelled rather than ending the drag. The gear's move, split and new-column entries still work too. A drop that rebuilds the arrangement it started from is no move (`moveTo` gives back the layout itself, its row shares kept). The gear's "split below the panel on the left", "move to a new column" and "join the cell on the left" make the same drops from the keyboard (`gearDrops`). After a move by keys, and whenever the showing panel changes in a side-by-side dock (opened from its card, back from a pop-out), the panel's head is scrolled into sight (`revealHead`, nearest edge, without smoothing under reduced motion). The rest of a head is left free for panning the dock, and below `PHONE` the dock stays as it was: no handles, no move keys, no move entries and no "pop out" in the gear.

The store keeps `dockLayout` beside `panels`, and `panels` is always `panelsOf(dockLayout)`: columns left to right, cells top to bottom, tabs in order. Every action writes the two together, taking `panels` from the layout (`panelsFor`, the same array when it reads the same, so a move that changes nothing is no change to save). `dockPanel` is the one writer for every way into the dock by hand (its card, a shell, the bench, a task; the load and a backend's connect place the layout themselves): a new panel becomes a column of its own side by side, or a tab of the cell that was showing as tabs, and then shows. `closePanel`, `movePanel`, `dropPanel`, `arrangeDock`, `returnPanel`, `switchCheckout` (through `rename`), the tree's prune and the loader compute the layout first in the same way. Each action reconciles the layout from `panels` before reading it (`laidOut`), so a `panels` set on its own (a test, say) is followed: closed panels leave, new ones join, and a list reordered by hand rebuilds the layout flat, losing its splits. A subscription registered after every other one in the store module is the net for any path that sets one of the two without the other (the gesture cancel in `Dock.tsx` registers later, but only reads `dockLayout` and ends gestures, so the order is safe). The load and a backend's connect already compute `panels` from the layout, so for them it is a fallback; a screen change sets `dockLayout` alone and relies on it, the layout leading and the open set taking its order once it is placed over this window's panels.

`dockLayout` is kept per kind of screen (it is in `SCREEN_LAYOUT`, so `putScreen` writes it to the screen's slot). A window moved to another kind of screen takes up that screen's own arrangement, placed over the panels open here; a screen never arranged starts from the nearest one that was, by the `screenSlots` chain (in practice the last arrangement the main window made anywhere), and keeps it as its own from then on. A screen change never regroups. `arrangeDock` does, and only from an explicit choice that changes the arrangement: the panel gear's "panels side by side" and "panels as tabs" and the Settings page's open-target picker (when it picks the dock or tabs) each call it, which sets `openIn` and regroups the dock into a column per panel or one cell of tabs. The gate compares arrangements, not `openIn` values (`arrangementOf`): `openIn` "tabs" is the tabs arrangement, and "dock", "tab" and "window" are all columns, which is also what lights the panel gear's "panels side by side". A pick whose arrangement is the one already in place sets `openIn` when the value differs and does not regroup, so going from a tab or a window to the dock, or picking the entry already lit, keeps every split and tabbed cell. The picker's tab and window set `openIn` alone (`setSetting`) and never regroup; otherwise `openIn` only decides how a new panel joins. Widths go by panel, not by place: `regroup` itself reuses widths by position, but `arrangeDock`, going to tabs, saves each column's width under the panel leading it (alone in the column's top cell, `leadWidths`) into `panelWidths`, and going to columns gives each new column its lead panel's width from those (and the old columns' leads) straight away, so a panel that leads a column after the regroup takes its width back. A lone column's width is also saved to `panelWidths` when its panel closes or pops out (`claimPanel` goes through `closePanel`) and when a checkout switch onto a sibling already open takes its column away, and `place` reads it for that panel's next column (`widthOf`), so a pop-out coming back keeps its width.

A layout saved before phase B has no `dockLayout`. `loadDock` builds the first one with `fromLegacy` from the saved open `panels` in order (and from this screen class's own slot: a class whose slot holds sizes from before B but no `dockLayout`, while no slot in its chain has one, builds its own rather than taking the flat copy another class wrote after the upgrade, `legacySlot`; a class never visited has no slot and inherits as above): each a column at its own old `panelWidths` width, or, when `openIn` is tabs, one cell at the old tabbed dock's `dockWidth`. `dockWidth` is read only for that and never written, and `panelWidths` stays on as the closed columns' memory. A saved layout is repaired (`normalizeLayout`) and its widths clamped to `PANEL`, and either way the result is placed over the open panels.

The stable DOM has one accepted cost: Tab and a screen reader walk the panels in the DOM's order, sorted by id, not in the order CSS shows them, so after a move the focus order and the visual order differ (WCAG 2.4.3). The strips and seams share it: they come after every panel in the DOM, so a cell's strip is reached after all the panels, where phase A's tabbed dock had its strip before the panel. A reorder is never a DOM move, so the fix cannot be one either; a roving tabindex over the heads, or `aria-owns` on the dock listing the panels, each with its strip and the seams beside it, in `panels` order, is the follow-up.

The carousel (`settings.dockCarousel`, kept per kind of screen like the gear choices; the gear's "carousel" entry and the `.dock-carousel` switch at the dock's top right) puts `carousel` on `.body`: the cards' column (`.main`) goes and the dock is placed in the last grid track by hand, so it does not slide into the emptied one; a column may be as wide as the window less its seam (`dockRoom`); and the row scrolls sideways with `scroll-snap-type: x proximity`, each column snapping at its seam (`.seam-col`, the column's left edge). The row pans only with more than one column; under the carousel a lone column, in either mode, takes the whole dock (`gridTemplate`'s `fill`, with no seam) and does not pan, while with the carousel off it keeps its width and seam. `useCarousel` in `Dock.tsx` does the panning, with the arithmetic pure and tested in `ui/src/carousel.ts`. A drag on a panel head pans once it passes `PAN_SLOP` (6px), and so does one on the dock's background past the last panel, but a drag on the head's name or glyph moves the panel instead (they are its drag handle, `[data-drag-handle]`) and a press on a head's button, link or menu stays theirs (`NOT_PAN`); a touch is left to the browser, which already scrolls the row under a finger. The wheel: a vertical wheel over a head pans; over content only shift+wheel pans, and not where the content scrolls sideways itself by more than a pixel (`overflowsX`, so a wide diff keeps its shift+wheel; the ancestor walk runs only for a shift+wheel, `wheelTake`); a sideways trackpad swipe is the browser's; a ctrl or cmd wheel, which is also how a trackpad pinch arrives, never pans, since it is the page's zoom; and the row takes a wheel only when it can still move that way (`canPan`), so at either end the wheel goes on to whatever is under it. The wheel listener captures, so a terminal in mouse mode never sees a wheel the row took. Ctrl+Alt+Left/Right jumps to the previous or next column's edge (`snapTo` over the column seams' positions, `panelEdges`); the listener captures on window, so while the carousel is on those keys never reach a focused shell. While a panel is over the window (full screen, filling it, or the bench, each a fixed box still inside `.dock`), the wheel, the drag and the keys leave the row behind it alone. On the grid every head keeps 32px of right padding, and every strip 34px, so the switch never covers a close button. With one column (as arranging the panels as tabs makes, or a single panel) the carousel gives it the cards' room and does not pan, and the switch's title says only that; below `PHONE` there is none.

Screen layout profiles (pure `src/core/screenlayouts.ts`, the page's side in `ui/src/screenprofiles.ts`, both tested; kept on the home backend, see "src/server"). A `ScreenProfile` (`types.ts`) names a screen (name, device, optional model, physical width and height, optional dpr) and a `ScreenLayout`: arrangement, carousel, the tree open and its width, every dock column's width, panel zoom, shell font, level, hidden sections and the feed, each optional, never a panel. `PRESETS` covers 1280x800 to 7680x2160 plus a phone and a tablet, each layout `recommendLayout` of its CSS px room (tabs and no tree on a phone, the tree from 1100 px at about an eighth of the width, the carousel when fewer than two 560 px columns fit beside the cards, the feed from 1400 px tall). `matchProfile` picks by device or model name, then exact physical size (1% either way up, for a browser zoom's rounding), then the nearest preset by `|Δ ln aspect| * 4 + |ln area ratio|`; the user's own come first at each step and are never "nearest". `applyScreenLayout` in the store goes through the usual setters (`arrangeDock`, `setSetting`) and then writes a fresh `dockLayout` with the column widths, the tree and the feed, so the per-screen slots keep it and the screen counts as arranged. It applies on its own only from the `onScreen` handler, in the main window, and only when the new slot keeps no `dockLayout` (`neverArranged`, read before the handler's own write); otherwise Settings' "screen layouts" offers apply by hand, with create, edit, copy, reset for a changed preset, delete asked in the row, and "detect" (`detectModel`: Chrome's `getScreenDetails` label, which asks for permission, else the UA-CH model). `sidebarOpen` joined `SCREEN_LAYOUT` for this, so the tree being open is now kept per kind of screen.

Tab templates (FR-030; `parseTabs` and `recommendTabs` in `src/core/screenlayouts.ts`, the page's half in `ui/src/tabtemplates.ts`, both tested). A layout's `tabs` is a `TabTemplate` with no repo in it: `panel`, the sections every open card shows as tabs; `split`, a `TabTree` of those names and `"shell"` (the card's first panel shell); `strip`, the popups in the strip. The server checks names against `/^[a-z]{1,20}$/` only, and the page drops one it has no section or popup for. `recommendTabs` seeds the presets: none below NARROW, history beside the shell to 2400 css px, changes, history and the tasks popup to 3600, then files and health too. `applyScreenLayout(layout, true)` (Settings' apply) lands it through `applyTabs` on `tabCards`, the open panels that are repos, each card's old panel section tabs going back to it first; the `onScreen` auto-apply passes no `withTabs`, so moving a window never moves sections out of the cards. `screenLayoutOf` captures one through `captureTabs` (the first card with panel section tabs, its group renamed by key with its first shell as `"shell"`, and the strip's popups), which "take this window's layout" and the form's "shell tabs: this window's" use; "none" is an empty template, "leave" none at all. A profile's row says its template in words (`tabsWords`).

Full screen (a panel's gear, "full screen", beside "fill the window") puts the panel in its fill-the-window mode (`surface-full`) and asks the browser for full screen on `document.documentElement` (`enterFull` in `ui/src/fullscreen.ts`), never on the panel, whose element would have to move and whose gear menus, portals to body, would not show inside it. A browser that refuses (no user gesture) leaves the panel filling the window; one with no full screen at all (iOS Safari, `fullscreenEnabled` false) shows no "full screen" entry in the dock's gear, and a solo window's entry reads "fill the window" (`fullWord`). Every way out ends both: Esc leaves the browser's full screen, which `useFullscreenExit` hears as `fullscreenchange` and drops `surface-full` with it; picking another mode leaves the browser's full screen; closing the panel leaves it, even when the close lands while the browser is still asking; and a tabbed panel hidden by another tab showing (a pop-out returning, say) leaves it and drops back to normal, so it does not come back filling the window. Where the browser has a keyboard lock (Chromium), full screen once granted locks Escape to the page (`lockEscape`), so a shell in the panel gets its Esc, an agent's interrupt: a single Esc inside a shell or a text field is theirs, a single Esc anywhere else leaves at once (the panel's `useLeaveOnEscape`, with `escapeLeaves` keeping text fields while locked), and holding Esc leaves from anywhere, which is how to leave from inside a shell. Every exit and unmount lets go of the lock (`leaveFull`, `unlockKeys`). Without a lock, Esc stays the browser's and leaves at once, as before.

The top bar (`components/TopBar.tsx`) is two `.tb-line` rows on anything wider than a phone: the views, the scanned folders, the workspace, the finders and the makers above; the grouping, the filters and the filter box, then the chips, below. The filter box (`.tb-show`) takes what the second row has spare, up to 340px, and gives it back first, down to 120px (96px under 940px). Past that the words go in tiers of media queries in `styles.css` ("the top bar narrowing"): at 1240px the attention pill's and the filters button's words and the version, at 1100px the sign-in chip's backend name (those three only off a phone, whose bar keeps them) and the ranger's word (`compact` from `NARROW`), with the gaps drawn closer, at 940px the wordmark's name and the backend names. Each of these buttons carries an `aria-label` with the whole wording, so it keeps its name when the words are gone. A row still too wide wraps. Measured with a workspace in use, both rows hold down to 761px; a sign-in chip wraps the second under about 850px, and live runs, kept shells or more backends can too. On a phone (`PhoneBar`) the two lower rows sit in `.tb-fold` and fold away while `.main` scrolls down and come back on the way up (`useTuck` over `nextTuck` in pure `ui/src/topbar.ts`, tested): a move of more than `TUCK_SLOP` past the anchor, always shown within `TUCK_TOP` of the top, never folded when the cards scroll less than three times the rows' height measured with the rows out, and each fold followed by `TUCK_HOLD_MS` in which scrolls only move the anchor, since the fold changes `.main`'s height and the browser's clamp of `scrollTop` would read as a scroll back. The focus in the filter box or a menu open in the bar holds the rows out (a tapped button keeps the focus, so a button's does not); folded, they are `inert`, and `/` or `f` unfolds them in the capture phase, through `flushSync`, before App's handler looks for the box or the button. The fold animates `grid-template-rows` only under `prefers-reduced-motion: no-preference`. The sideways row (`.tb-scroll`) fades only at an end with more past it, from `data-more-start` and `data-more-end` that `useScrollEdges` sets on its scroll, resize and child changes (`scrollEdges`).

Pop out and back (`ui/src/panes.ts`, the store's `popOut`, `returnPanel` and `claimPanel`, the main window's listener `listenPanes` in `panes.ts`, tested on a stand-in channel and wired to a BroadcastChannel and the store in `App.tsx`, and the pop-out's in `App.tsx`): the gear's "pop out" opens the panel's solo url with `popped=1` in a window named `canopy:pop:<id>` (`popOutWindow`), first, so a blocked popup leaves the panel where it was, then closes the panel and keeps its dock slot in `popped`, an id to index map saved in `canopy.layout` with the panels. Only a window opened that way joins the protocol: "open in a new tab", a shift-click on a card or a typed solo url has no `popped=1` and never takes a panel out of the dock. The windows talk on `BroadcastChannel("canopy:panes")` in five messages, each checked on arrival (`parsePaneMsg`), since any same-origin page can post: `hello` (a pop-out shows its panel; the main window takes that panel out of its dock, `heardHello` then `claimPanel`, which is also how a second main window learns of it), `bye` (on `pagehide`; the main window waits 300 ms, `BYE_WAIT_MS`, before putting the panel back at its slot, so a reloading pop-out's next `hello` calls it off), `return` (the pop-out's "back to the dock", at once), `close` (the panel's own close in a pop-out: the main window forgets the slot, `forgetPopped`, and the window closes, so its `bye` finds nothing to bring back) and `who` (a loading main window's question, which every pop-out answers with `hello`; after 1.5 s, `WHO_WAIT_MS`, the main window takes back every panel that was out when it loaded and that no window answered for, which is how a pop-out killed without `pagehide` gives its panel back). A panel this window pops out comes back after 1.5 s, `HELLO_WAIT_MS`, unless its window says hello first (`popOut` calls `poppedOut`, which the listener watches), since a pop-out closed before it loads sends neither hello nor bye; a slow pop-out whose hello comes later claims the panel again, as one back from a `bye` does. A main window ignores another's `who`, and two main windows each apply the messages to their own `panels`. A pop-out keeps its panel's shells: it takes up its own repo's held panel shells as tabs (`adoptPoppedTerms` through `adoptHere`), and `returnPanel` adopts them back into the dock the way `openPanel` does. On the grid the slot is a hint: `returnPanel` finds the panel that sat after the popped one in the flat list (`rest[slot]`, its old neighbour) before placing, puts the panel last with `place`, then moves it beside the neighbour, as a column of its own just left of the neighbour's column side by side (`moveTo` to the left of its cell, at the panel's `panelWidths` width or the default) or as a tab just before it in tabs mode (`moveWithin`); a split it was part of is not put back, and with no neighbour it stays last. A panel docked again by any road (its card, a shell, the bench, a task, all through `dockPanel`; switching a card's checkout to it; a saved panel shell reopening it at load) or closed loses its `popped` slot, so a later `bye` or load sweep cannot bring back a panel the user closed. One the user docked or closed by hand while it was out is also `recalled`, and the main window ignores a `hello` for a recalled id, so a pop-out left open after its panel was docked from the card does not take the panel again when it reloads. A panel the `bye` timer or the load sweep brought back is not recalled, and a later `hello` claims it again: that is how a pop-out back from the back-forward cache, or one whose reload outran `BYE_WAIT_MS`, gets its panel back instead of showing it twice. Popping the panel out again un-recalls it. `recalled` lives in the main window only and is not saved, so after that window reloads, a stale pop-out answering `who` claims a hand-docked panel again. A popped home panel whose repo leaves the scan loses its slot with the scan (`treeState`), and `returnPanel` brings back no home panel without a repo once the tree has loaded. The loader takes `popped` only as a plain object of finite slots (`poppedOf`), so a hand-edited array cannot turn into panels "0" and "1". Dockless windows strip `popped` from their layout patch along with `panels`, `dockLayout`, `activePanel`, `terms` and `activeTerm`, since their copy is the grove's.

## Voice

The top bar's mic (`ui/src/components/Voice.tsx`) holds a spoken conversation with an Eleven Realtime model, which speaks OpenAI's Realtime events over a WebSocket. The server only mints: `POST /api/voice/session` (`operate`, since minutes are billed) posts the session config to ElevenLabs' `/v1/convai/realtime/client_secrets` with `CANOPY_VOICE_KEY` and answers the 60-second secret, the model and the socket URL. `GET /api/voice` answers the model, or null with no key, and GPT-Live's as `live`; the button hides when both are null. `src/core/voice.ts` holds the config and the mint (the key in a closure, deleted from the env with the other `SECRET_ENV` names); `src/core/voicewire.ts` is the browser-safe half both sides import: the tool list, the instructions, the view names, the 24 kHz rate. The session config sent at mint is authoritative, so the page never sends `session.update`.

The page opens the socket itself with the secret as the subprotocol `openai-insecure-api-key.<secret>`, so audio never passes through canopy. `ui/src/voicecall.ts` is the browser half. The `AudioContext` is built inside the click (Safari plays only from a context made in a gesture) at the device's rate, the mic is taken with echo cancellation on (without it a laptop's speaker reaches the mic and the model interrupts itself), and an AudioWorklet loaded from a Blob URL copies each block out. The page resamples to 24 kHz 16-bit PCM and appends it in 100 ms slices. The answer's `response.output_audio.delta` PCM is queued on the speaker back to back. `input_audio_buffer.speech_started` drops what is queued: the server cancels its own answer, but audio already scheduled locally would keep playing.
