A tool call (`response.function_call_arguments.done`) is answered at once with a `function_call_output`, and one `response.create` follows the `response.done`, so several calls in one response get one reply. `end_conversation` hangs up once the goodbye has played. `ui/src/voice.ts` is the pure half, tested: the resampler (it carries its place across blocks), PCM and base64, `parseVoiceEvent`, the reducer that turns events into the panel's lines (a placeholder at `speech_started` keeps what was said above its answer, though its transcript lands later), and `runVoiceTool`.

GPT-Live is the other engine (`CANOPY_LIVE_KEY`), picked per browser (`pickEngine` in `ui/src/voicelive.ts`, kept under `canopy.voiceEngine`) when `GET /api/voice` names both; with both, the panel's header has a provider menu, and picking the other one hangs up and calls again. GPT-Live has no client secret a page could use, so the page makes a WebRTC offer with the mic as its track and an `oai-events` data channel, and `POST /api/voice/live` (`operate`, an offer of at most `LIVE_OFFER_MAX`) takes it to OpenAI's `/v1/live/sessions` with the key and answers only the session id and the SDP answer (`src/core/voicelive.ts`, the key in a closure and in `SECRET_ENV`). Audio then runs on the media tracks between the page and OpenAI; the session config sent there is authoritative. The live model (`LIVE_INSTRUCTIONS`) only talks and delegates; the tools sit with its Responses backend (`CANOPY_LIVE_BACKEND`, `LIVE_BACKEND_INSTRUCTIONS`, one call per response). `ui/src/livecall.ts` is the browser half: a function call comes nested as `response.event` holding `response.output_item.done`, is run by the same `runVoiceTool`, and is answered with a `function_call_output` item and a `response.create` at once. Transcripts come as fragments with no item or turn (`session.input_transcript.delta`, `session.output_transcript.delta`), so `liveReduce` joins a fragment to the last line while the same side talks, and a 1.2 s quiet puts the phase back to listening. `end_conversation` waits for the goodbye to go quiet, then sends `session.close` and lets go at `session.closed` (5 s at most); a close the page did not ask for shows its reason. `ui/src/voicelive.ts` is the pure half, tested.

Dictation is the other mic. `POST /api/voice/dictate` (`operate`) takes a recorded clip as the raw body (at most `DICTATE_MAX`, 25 MB, else 413), names it by type with `dictationName` (Whisper-style servers read the format off the extension) and hands it to the same `Transcriber` the incubator uses (`CANOPY_TRANSCRIBE_URL`); `GET /api/voice` says `dictate: true` when there is one. The page asks home first, then each other online backend, once per set of online backends (`findDictator` in `components/Dictate.tsx`). `DictationFloat`, mounted once in the app and in each single-view window, follows focus: a text field `dictatable` accepts (`ui/src/dictate.ts`: textareas and word inputs, not read-only, disabled, or inside `[data-no-dictate]`, `[data-mic]` or `.xterm`) or a shell, read off xterm's helper textarea. It sits in a body portal and reads the field's rect every frame while shown. `DictateButton` is the fixed mic beside the run chat's composer and tailchan's draft, whose fields carry `data-mic`. Words go in through `execCommand("insertText")` at the remembered caret (undo keeps working and React's onChange runs; the native value setter and an `input` event otherwise), spaced by `dictationText`; a shell gets `term.paste` through `LIVE`. The recording is `useRecorder` (`ui/src/recorder.ts`), shared with the incubator's voice memo. The secrets view is `data-no-dictate`.

The tools answer from the store the page already holds, across every backend it follows: `overview`, `list_repos` (capped at 15, never the file list), `needs_me` (open asks and waiting runs), `list_runs`, `open_repo` (the same `openPanel` a click makes, so a panel set to start its shell starts it) and `open_view`. The tools see one checkout per repo, the one its card shows (`allCards` and `leadOf`), so a repo on both backends is counted and named once. A spoken name opens on an exact or containing match; a name an edit or so off comes back as `did_you_mean` for the model to confirm with the user, and several names come back as a choice. Nothing said aloud commits, pushes, answers an ask or starts a run. Headless Chrome on the Mac never settles `getUserMedia` even with the fake device, so a browser check stands in a MediaStream that plays a recorded clip.

## Costs

The costs view (`ui/src/components/Costs.tsx`, sums in `ui/src/costs.ts`) shows what the paid services spent over today, 7 or 30 local days, by service and provider, by repo card, by run or chat, and the Claude sessions behind the dollars. The session list reads the costliest eight repos' sessions through `/api/repos/sessions` (30 days) and keeps those that began in the window, so a long session never counts in a window it barely touched. Claude's dollars come from the claude-history archive already in the store (`s.history`), since it holds the Claude sessions of every machine it pulls from, canopy's own runs among them, mapped to the repos canopy scans and synced hourly. A card takes its largest checkout's figure, not the sum, because one archive may map the same sessions to a checkout on each backend.

Everything else comes from a ledger each backend keeps: `costs/<YYYY-MM>.jsonl` under the config dir, one appended line per row, in local months (`src/core/costledger.ts`, shapes and pure logic in `src/core/costs.ts`). Rows are written in four places:
- `RunCosts.onRun`, fed every run change from the Runner's `onChange`. Both harnesses report a chat's spend so far (Claude's `total_cost_usd`, Codex's `tokenUsage.total`), checked against a live two-reply chat, so each row is the difference from the last row, and a figure that drops counts whole. `--resume` brings the session's spend back with it (checked too: a resumed first reply reports the earlier total plus its own), so the difference is kept per session, not per run, and a session first seen after a restart starts from the sum of its ledger rows over the last 90 days. Claude rows carry dollars and the model from the CLI's init; Codex rows carry tokens and the model `thread/start` answered with.
- `POST /api/voice/ended` (`operate`): the page posts a call's engine, its length from the first answered phase to the end, and its invocation context once the call ends. A chat context ties the call to that run.
- `POST /api/voice/dictate?seconds=`: the clip's recorded length, from the dictation mic.
- The incubator's voice memos, through a speech model wrapped in the server (`memos`), with the length the model heard: `hear` in `src/core/transcribe.ts` asks for `verbose_json`, whose `duration` Whisper fills, and asks again for `json` when a server answers that format with a 400.

The ledger stores a repo's absolute path, and `GET /api/costs?days=` turns it back into this backend's id where the repo still exists. The view fetches every online backend's report and qualifies the ids itself, since ledgers do not sync. A ledger Claude row on a card the archive has a figure for in the window shows on its run but is not added again; one the archive has nothing for (a repo it cannot map, a run newer than its sync, no archive at all) counts on its own. Only Claude reports dollars. A `prices.json` beside the config (`"<provider>/<model>"` or `"<provider>/*"` to `perMinute`, `inputPerM`, `cachedInputPerM`, `outputPerM`) turns the rest into estimates, shown with "≈"; canopy ships no prices. A test server writes no ledger unless `CANOPY_CONFIG_DIR` is set.

## The secret store

The store (`docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-14-secrets-design.md`, plan `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-14-secrets-phase-1.md`) is one signed file, `secrets.json`, in the private Forgejo repo `eric/secrets`. Every value in it is an age envelope sealed to the machines that may open it (`recipientsFor`: the mini and the break-glass key always, the Mac when a sink on the Mac or on gpd needs it); the rest (names, descriptions, tags, stamps, allowlists, sinks) is plain text. `core/secretfile.ts` (pure, browser-safe) owns the shape, the canonical JSON (keys sorted, so a reordered file verifies) and the ed25519 signature over it with WebCrypto. The signature covers `seq`, which every signed write raises by one: a reader refuses a pulled file whose `seq` is not above the one it holds, and the writer checks Forgejo's file the same way, and its signature, before it merges, so an older signed file put back on top is never taken. `core/secretcrypt.ts` seals and opens with `age-encryption`; a value's envelope binds its name and rotation stamp, so a sealed value moved to another name or stamp fails to open. `core/sinks.ts` reads and rewrites dotenv and JSON files, keeping every line and key it does not manage, refusing a symlink, writing atomically at 0600 and chowning back to the old owner when it runs as root; `repoRefusal` keeps sinks out of a git repo unless git ignores the path. `core/sinksync.ts` decides per key: same, missing (written), drift (left alone until someone picks a side), waiting (the key is locked or the value is not written yet). A key whose file value differs on first apply is drift, never an overwrite.

The writer is `src/secrets/`, its own image and compose service (`secrets`): `store.ts` (the checkout in the `secrets-data` volume, the keys in `data/keys`, signing and pushing as `canopy@mini`, refusing a checkout its own key did not sign), `gate.ts` (passkeys through `@simplewebauthn/server`, the first-passkey code in the log, sessions of 15 idle minutes and an hour at most), `audit.ts`, and `service.ts`, which answers one JSON line per connection on a unix socket only the secretscaller group reaches (`core/secretwire.ts` parses both ends). Every act needs a session and is audited either way; one change runs at a time. Proposals (import findings and "take this value") live in the service's memory for 24 hours, at most 500, sealed to the mini until approved; `same` names a secret or proposal holding the same value, compared in memory, never by hash. The import tab picks many proposals at once by key name (`ui/src/secretsbulk.ts`: `looksSecret` for secret-looking keys, minus flags, limits and addresses such as `REQUIRE_`, `MAX_` or `_URL`) and approves each under its suggested name with its stack as tag, description and sink (`bulkAct`); a proposal's `sinkable` says whether its file can be one (dotenv or json, and inside `sinkRoots` when it is the writer's own file, so the mini's `/import/canopy.env` is approved without a sink), one act and one commit per proposal; a names-only source still needs its value typed on its own row. `suggestName` drops a stack or server prefix the key already carries, so `EXECUTOR_SECRET_KEY` in the executor stack is `executor_secret_key`. No answer, log line, event or audit entry holds a value or a hash of one but a reveal's answer (`src/secrets/leak.test.ts`).

canopy's side is `server/secrets.ts`'s `SecretsHub`. On the mini (`CANOPY_SECRETS_SOCKET` set) it relays to the service, turns a login's token into the `canopy_secrets` cookie (`Path=/api/secrets`, HttpOnly, Secure, SameSite=Strict), lets gated routes in only from the gate origin, and answers a closed gate 401 `{error, gate: "passkey"}` with no `login` field, so the page never mistakes it for Cloudflare's sign-in. Everywhere else it runs `core/secretlocal.ts`'s `SecretsReader`: a checkout pulled `--ff-only` and verified against the pinned signing key (`canopy secrets pin`), which keeps its last good commit and names why it refused a newer one; on the Mac, the Mac's age identity wrapped by a passphrase (`secrets-key.age`), opened into memory by `unlock` and dropped by `lock`, and the Mac's sinks. Readers scan their own import sources and take drift by sealing to the mini and posting to the writer's proposals.

The page is `components/Secrets.tsx` (the `secrets` view) with `ui/src/secrets.ts` (pure, tested). It offers gated acts only on the gate page itself (`gateHere`), links there from anywhere else, shows a revealed value for 20 seconds, and keeps each backend's info in the store's `secrets` record. The CLI is `src/cli/secrets.ts`: `list`, `status` (exit 1 on drift), `unlock`, `pin`, and `recover` from the break-glass identity.

An MCP server's secrets reach it through `canopy-mcp-env` (`scripts/canopy-mcp-env`, copied to dotclaude's `bin/`), which reads `~/.claude/mcp-env/<server>.env` line by line and exports each value without evaluating it. It reads exactly what `quoteDotenv` writes, so a value that sink writer refuses (a quote or a newline together with a `$`) cannot reach an MCP server: its sink reads the writer's error instead.

`mcp-allow {server, hash, def, env, headers, homes}` and `mcp-revoke {server}` (`core/secretmcp.ts`, gated) change the allow lists and the env sinks in one signed commit: `mcp:<server>#<hash>` on each named secret, and per machine the dotenv sink `mcp-<server>-<machine>` at `<home>/.claude/mcp-env/<server>.env` (plus `-headers` for a JSON headers file). The service recomputes the hash from `def` and refuses one that does not match; no path travels in the act, and gpd's home comes from the Mac's ssh scan of it. MCP sinks are found by id, so `secrets.json`'s shape is unchanged. After every pass each writer deletes any file in its own `mcp-env` folder that no sink names (`pruneMcpEnv`), so a revoke takes the value off disk. Only a regular file goes (`lstat`, so a directory or a symlink of such a name stays and a link is never followed), and the prune never rejects: a file it cannot remove is logged on the Mac and audited as a failed `sink-prune` on the mini, by path only. Both writers record the pass (`sinkState`, `sinksAt`) before they prune, and the reader's unawaited calls (its start, the pull interval, the pass after a lock) catch and log, since Bun ends the server on an unhandled rejection. The relay refuses an `mcp-allow` with 409 while `git check-ignore` does not ignore `mcp-env/<server>.env` in that machine's dotclaude (`mcpIgnored`), since the service cannot run git there. `impactOf` lists a secret's reach (its sinks with keys, the servers its allowlist names with the machines holding their env sinks, and the stacks those restart); rotate shows it before the write, delete refuses on it and the page shows it as "used by". `SecretsInfo.sinksAt` (optional, so an older backend omits it) is the commit a backend's last finished sink pass read: a reader publishes its new head before its pass runs, so the reader runs one pass at start and serializes its passes, and the page's "after" list waits on `sinksAt` (`passedOver`) before it reports a sink's state.

Phase 3 (`docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-15-kit-secrets-phase-3-design.md`, plan `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-15-kit-secrets-phase-3a.md`) adds restarts and gpd. A sink's `restart` names the compose project a write restarts, or `none`; unset, `stackFor` finds the one running project whose first config file sits directly in the sink's `/opt/stacks/<dir>`. Only the writer knows the running projects, so it passes `stackFor` into `metaList`, and a reader's reach names set projects only. The project list and the restarts go through `src/secrets/stacks.ts`: ssh from the `secrets` container to `192.168.48.1` with the key in `~/.config/canopy-restart` (mounted read-only at `/restart`, made by `scripts/restart-setup.sh`), whose `authorized_keys` line forces `scripts/canopy-restart`. The script reads the request from `SSH_ORIGINAL_COMMAND`: `list` prints each project under `/opt/stacks` with its files, and `restart <project>` runs `docker compose -p <project> -f … up -d` with exactly those files and its output thrown away, waits up to 60 s for containers to leave `starting`, and prints service, state and health only, since `ps --format json` and a failed `up` can echo interpolated values. The `stack-restart` act runs outside the one-change-at-a-time queue, refuses a project no sink restarts and one whose sinks still wait or carry an error, and is audited with the project only. The service asks for the list at start, every 5 minutes on its tick and after a restart; `SecretsInfo.stacks` and `stacksError` carry it to the page, which builds the copy line from the files and puts the restart button on the after-rotate list.

gpd has no backend, so the Mac's `SecretsReader` writes its sinks through `core/sinkssh.ts`'s `RemoteSinks` for every kit machine whose `via` is this backend (`KitHub.sshMachines`). Each call is a fixed sh script through `onHost`: a probe that answers with gpd's `$HOME`, a read that returns the file as base64, a write that takes the new text as base64 on stdin and renames a 0600 temp file over the target, a repo check (`git check-ignore` in whatever work tree holds the path), and the `mcp-env` prune. No value is ever an argument. Locked, gpd's sinks read waiting with no ssh at all; a probe that fails marks each one `gpd is away: <reason>`, and the next pull passes again even with no new commit, as it does when the kit's machine list changes. The writer reports no state for sinks it does not own, so the page takes gpd's from the Mac. The kit then plans gpd's servers with secrets like any other machine's.

## Callers and tokens

Every call to the API carries a caller (`docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-16-caller-credential-design.md`, plan `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-16-caller-credential.md`). `src/core/callers.ts` is pure and browser-safe: six rights (`read`, `plan`, `operate`, `shell`, `admin`, `helper`), the rights each kind holds (page: read, operate, shell, admin; cli: read, operate, admin; shell and ranger: read, operate; coordinator: read, plan; helper: helper; none: read), and `needs(method, path)`, the route table. A GET needs `read`, so reads stay open to `none`, unless the table names it (the sweep's GETs are filed `admin`: its plan names every shell and chat on the backend), and with three more exceptions: the `/api/term` upgrade needs `shell`, `/api/helper` needs `helper`, and every Library GET (`/library` and below, and `/api/library`) needs `admin` unless it is on `LIBRARY_READS`: the dashboard (`/library`, `/library/`, `/library/index.html`) and `/library/ports`, which the Library view's iframe loads with no token, and the engine's `ping`, `manifest`, `api/dev/state`, `api/ports/state` and `api/files`. `/library/open` stays `admin` whatever its action, since it launches editors, terminals and dev servers. The allowlist matches the path exactly as sent, and only when `normalPath` (cut at `;` and `?`, repeated slashes collapsed, percent-decoded once) leaves it unchanged, because the engine's Python `urlparse` reads `/open;x` as `/open`. A non-GET missing from the table needs `admin`, so a new route is safe until someone files it, and `callers.test.ts` pins the table. `POST /api/auth/pair|claim|first|ticket` are filed `open`: pairing happens before a caller has a token, and `ticket` checks the right it issues inside its handler, since a helper token holds no `read`.

`src/server/callers.ts` has `CallerBook`, `callerOf` and `CallerGate`. The book keeps one entry per token in `<config dir>/callers.json` (0600, written through a temp file and a rename, one write at a time): the SHA-256 hex of the token, never the token, with its kind, label, shell id or run id, and created and last-used times. A token is `cnp_<kind>_` and 32 random bytes in base64url, sent as `X-Canopy-Token` (listed in `PREFLIGHT_HEADERS`, so a cross-site page may send it). A file that cannot be parsed is moved to `callers.json.corrupt-<ms>` and the book starts empty, so every page and CLI signs in again. A page or CLI session ends 30 days after its last use; a shell, ranger or helper token lasts until revoked, until its shell ends (`dropTerm` when a shell closes, and `prune` at start for a shell that did not come back) or until its run settles (`dropRun`). Whenever the book drops a caller, by any of these, a revoke or expiry, it tells the server (`onDrop`), which closes every shell and helper socket that caller opened (`callerSockets`) with 1008. The dock and the helper read that as a drop and dial again, which under `enforce` needs a token that still works. `check` bumps `used` and writes it at most once a minute.

`CANOPY_CALLERS` sets the mode: `off` skips the check, `log` (the default, and what an unknown value reads as, with one warning) lets everything through and counts what `enforce` would refuse, `enforce` answers 401 `{error, need}` for no token or an unknown one and 403 `{error, need}` for a token short of the right. Neither body has a `login` key, which the page reads as the edge gate's sign-in. The `/api/auth/*` routes are checked in every mode, so a token nobody approved during the log week cannot approve later. `GET /api/auth` answers the mode, `refused` (a count and the newest 20 as kind, route, Origin and user agent, would-refuse ones in `log` included), the callers, the pair requests waiting, and `you` and `youId`. The `canopy:callers` health check always reads ok, with the refused count in its detail: the health model has no warning state, and `healing` would colour the top bar for the whole log week. `off` reads as `off`.

Tokens reach a child only where a new process env is built: a pty shell (`shellEnv`), a tmux session (`newProcessEnv`, which drops any token the shell id held and mints a fresh one, since the book cannot hand an old token back) and the ranger's session. A line typed into a shell that is already running (agent start, resume, continue, move) never mints: codex gets the literal reference `"$CANOPY_TOKEN"`, which the shell expands inside the toml quotes, so no token value reaches a pane. Runs get none. `CANOPY_TOKEN` is in `INHERITED_ENV`, which the server deletes from its own env at start, so a canopy started in a canopy shell does not pass that shell's token to its runs. Shells opened before the release hold no token; where no CLI token file exists, their CLI gets the 401 that says to open a new shell. On a machine with a CLI token file, a CLI call inside any canopy shell carries the CLI's admin token, not the shell's (the order below). A shell on another host (an ssh locator) gets none. A coordinator's token is minted for its run alone, and passed to it (FR-010, "The coordinator").

The coordinator's lane (`CallerGate.lane`, ahead of `gate` at the top of route() and in every mode) holds a coordinator to what its brief reads, and goes by the listener a request came in on. `CANOPY_COORD_PORT` (`coordPort`) is the port of the coordinator's own listener, a second `Bun.serve` on canopy's bind address, started after the main one and only with a coordinator's runner set up, whose `fetch` calls the same route() with `lane` true: no preflight answer, no CORS, no websocket handler. Every request there must carry a coordinator's token, and a coordinator's token, on either listener, reaches only `laneAllows`'s method and exact path: `GET /api/about`, `/api/tree`, `/api/runs`, `/api/landing` and `POST /api/coordinator/plan`. Anything else is a 403 `{error}`, counted in `refused` and logged once per kind, method and path, so on the lane the event stream, a socket, the Library, the auth routes and a static file are all refused before they are reached. The origin gate lets `CANOPY_COORD_API`'s host through on that listener alone. A `CANOPY_COORD_PORT` that is unset or not a port, or one the listener cannot take, leaves the listener down, and `coordinatorRefusal` then keeps every coordinator from starting and `/api/about` says `coordinator: false`; canopy itself stays up. The lane went by source address (`CANOPY_COORD_NET`) until a live check on the mini showed docker's userland proxy handing canopy the shells network's gateway for every client, so it never engaged; the socket is the one thing the coordinator's container cannot forge. Without the lane, `none` holds `read`, so anything in that container could read any repo file through `GET /api/repos/diff` with no token.

Pairing replaces a login. `POST /api/auth/pair {label, kind}` answers a pair id, a six-character code to compare and a secret only the asker holds. `kind` is `page`, `cli` or `helper` (`PAIR_KINDS` in `src/core/callers.ts`), any other value is a 400, and `claim` mints exactly that kind. A request with no kind falls back to the old label rule, `cli <host>` for a CLI, `helper <host>` for a helper and anything else a page, so an older CLI still works. The page sends `page`, `canopy auth login` sends `cli` and `canopy helper --pair` sends `helper`. At most three requests wait and each expires after 10 minutes. A request that carries the id and secret of the asker's own waiting request (`id` and `secret` in the body: the page sends its last pair's, so pressing sign in again renews) replaces it, and the old claim then answers 404 "replaced by a newer one". The same label and address without the secret replaces nothing, since on the mini every client shares one address and the labels show in `GET /api/auth`, so a match would let anyone cancel another's request. The whole server takes at most six new requests in a rolling minute, renewals included, and answers 429 `{error}` past that without making a pair; the cap is server-wide because docker-proxy gives every client on the mini one address. It stops a loop, not a hostile asker: three requests under different labels still hold every slot until each is denied (from a signed-in page's inbox) or expires, and both refusals say so. A signed-in page sees the request as an inbox item and an `auth` event, which carries the kind (`PendingPair.kind`); the card names who asks, from which address, the kind and the rights it grants from `KIND_RIGHTS` (`pairAsk` in `ui/src/callerToken.ts`; a request from a backend that sends no kind reads by its label), with `admin` in the warning colour and a long label cut, and answers it with `approve` or `deny`, both `admin`, so a run can ask and never approve. While the book holds no live page or CLI session (one unused for 30 days does not count) it prints a one-time first code to the server's log, and `POST /api/auth/first {id, code}` approves a waiting request with it once; the code is burned on use, and the log prints one only while the book holds no live page or CLI session. When nobody left can approve, `docs/deploy.md` ("Locked out") has the way back in. The asker polls `claim {id, secret}` and gets its token once. `claim` and `first` count a wrong secret or code per route and address and answer 429 after ten in ten minutes, with a wrong secret counted only against a live pair, since a page still polling a dead one is not guessing. `revoke` is `admin` too.

The two sockets a browser opens cannot send a header, so `POST /api/auth/ticket {for: "term"|"helper"}` returns a ticket good for 60 seconds and one use, carried as `ticket=` on the `/api/term` or `/api/helper` upgrade. `callerOf` redeems it, so it runs once per request.

The CLI side is `src/cli/api.ts` and `src/cli/auth.ts`. `callerHeaders(base)` picks the token from `--token`, else the token file `<config dir>/token-<host>-<port>` (0600), else `$CANOPY_TOKEN`, and every fetch in the CLI uses it. The token file comes before `$CANOPY_TOKEN` on purpose, so `canopy secrets unlock` typed in a canopy shell carries admin: on a machine with a CLI token file, a CLI call inside a canopy shell carries the CLI's admin token, not the shell's, and the 401 that says to open a new shell applies only where no token file exists. The flag and the environment variable go to the home backend only (`--backend`, else `$CANOPY_API`, else the local one): a peer reached with `--on` or a fallback address uses its own token file. Every fetch that carries a token uses `redirect: "manual"`, since the edge Worker redirects to its sign-in origin and the header must not follow. `canopy auth login` runs `pairFlow` (pair, optionally the first code with `--code`, poll until approved) and writes the file; `logout` revokes the session and removes the file; `status` prints the mode, who you are and the refused count. `canopy helper --pair` runs the same flow with a `helper` label and keeps the token in `token-helper-<host>-<port>`, which `runHelper` reads before every dial and trades for a ticket; with no token it dials bare, which a backend not enforcing lets through.

The page (`ui/src/callerToken.ts`, `store.ts`, `api.ts`) keeps one token per backend origin in `localStorage` under `canopy.token.<origin>`. Same-origin it sends the token on every call; cross-origin only on writes and on `GET /api/auth`, since reads need none and an edge gate that refuses a preflight would mark a whole remote backend offline. A backend that answers `none` to a stored token has revoked it, so the page forgets the token and the sign-in strip returns. The strip in the top bar starts a pair, shows the code as the one to match in a signed-in page's inbox, and, behind its own words, takes the first code from the server's log. A first code equal to the pair's own code is caught before it is sent (`isOwnCode`). A waiting sign-in (id, secret, code and the end of its ten-minute window) is held in `sessionStorage` under `canopy.pair.<origin>` (`readPair`/`writePair`), and each backend's first load and every resync call `signIn(b, true)`, which goes on polling that same request; so a phone that drops and reloads the tab while its owner approves the code elsewhere collects its token instead of asking again with a new code, which left the approved request orphaned. The entry goes once the token is kept or the request is over. On a phone the chip and strip sit in the first row, on a line of their own, and the strip drops its explaining words; settings lists the backend's sessions and revokes one; the inbox shows waiting requests with allow and deny. The shell dock asks for a ticket only when it holds a token for that backend. It dials with the ticket when it gets one; with no token, or with the ticket refused, it dials bare where the backend is in `log` or `off`, and under `enforce` waits for a sign-in instead of dialling.
